Finland

‘The European Union (BU) VAT Directive (2006/112/FC) is implemented in Finland through the Value Added
Tax Law, in Finnish “Arvonlisdverolaki” (30.12.1993/1501, hereafter referred to as the “VAT Law”). In
Finland, VAT is referred to as “Arvonliscivero” or “ALV”. Before the current VAT system was implemented,
Finland applied a system called turnover tax (sales tax) which was replaced by the VAT system in 1994 due to
Finland’s EU membership.

1. Taxable persons

1.1 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Until 31 December 2024, the VAT registration threshold was EUR 15,000. The supplier of goods and/or
services is not considered to be liable for VAT if its turnover during a calendar year does not amount to more
than EUR 15,000, unless they have been registered as a person liable to VAT at their own request. When

this threshold for VAT liability is exceeded, the supplier receives a relief which gradually decreases with the
increase of the turnover. When the annual turnover exceeds EUR 30,000, the full amount of VAT is levied.
This VAT relief regime is terminated as of | January 2025.

As of 1 January 2025, the VAT registration threshold is EUR 20,000. The supplier of goods and/or services
is not considered to be liable for VAT if their turnover during the current and preceding calendar year does
not amount (o more than EUR 20,000 per calendar year, unless they have been registered as a person liable to
VAT at their own request.

Finland has not implemented a threshold under which exceeding the Small and Medium sized enterprises
(SME) limit of EUR 20,000 has no consequences. From the time of exceeding the limit, the SME scheme can
no longer be applied.

If, as a result of exceeding the SME threshold, the SME regime can no longer be applied, taxpayers cannot
re-apply for using the SME scheme for a period of I year.

Finnish governmental bodies as well as municipalities are liable to VAT in the same way as private
companies, however, not with respect to activities in which they are engaged as public authorities.

A taxable person starting a taxable activity as defined in the VAT Law must notify the Finnish Tax
Administration before starting the activity. Finnish taxable persons use the same start-up notification form for
registering with the Trade Register, the Tax Administration’s VAT register, prepayment register and register of
employers.

If a taxable person does not register for VAT purposes in connection with the start-up but waits until their
business ID is obtained, it must use an amendment notification to register for VAT purposes.

Registration forms can be found using the following link: https://www.ytj fi/en/index/notifications. htm!

1.2 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

Until 31 December 2024, there was no VAT registration threshold or exemption for taxable persons not
established in Finland.

As of 1 January 2025, the exemption threshold for SME’s of EUR 20,000 applies to taxable persons with
domicile in other EU countries than Finland, provided that the turnover of the taxable person in the EU during
the current and preceding calendar year does not exceed EUR 100,000 per calendar year and the person has
not been registered for VAT at their own request. Foreign taxable persons who cannot apply the exemption
based on the threshold rule must register if they perform taxable transactions for VAT purposes in Finland.

Finland | 185

--- Page 2 ---
There are three different kinds of general VAT registration types available. “Mandatory VAT registration” is
the registration type available for taxable persons who are liable to register for Finnish VAT purposes except
for situations where registering for “notification duty” is required. “Notification duty” is available in case
registration liability is to arise due to intra-Community transactions only. Taxable persons not established in
Finland and making other supplies of goods or services than those resulting in VAT registration liability are
upon application entitled to register for VAT purposes in Finland (“voluntary VAT registration”). The VAT ID
is automatically entered into the VIES system as a result of the VAT registration.

The National Board of Patents and Registration and the Finnish Tax Administration share a joint declaration
procedure for business information, the Business Information System, BIS (in Finnish Y7/), where all company
information can be reported with one single form, Completed forms, with appendices, must be sent to:

National Board of Patents and Registration — Tax Administration
Business Information System

P.O. Box 2000

00231 Helsinki

Finland

The following documents are to be submitted for the VAT registration:

+ start-up notification;

+ form VEROH 6204e or 6206e;

+ a trade register extract or a certificate of registration with the commercial register in the country of the
non-established person or other equivalent document (and, if needed, a Finnish, Swedish or English
translation of the relevant parts of the document); and

+ inthe event a fiscal representative is appointed, the following additional information is required:

* a letter of commitment by the fiscal representative;

+ a power of attorney authorizing the fiscal representative;

+ a Finnish or Swedish translation of the power of attorney; and

+ a document indicating the relevant company details of the representative.

After the notification form has been sent to the National Board of Patents and Registration, the registration
process normally takes from 3 weeks up to 2 months.

Registration forms can be found at: https://www.yij,fi/en/index/notifications. him!

13. VAT Grouping
Finnish VAT legislation allows VAT grouping.

VAT grouping is optional, meaning that a taxable person may choose whether to register or join a VAT group
when all the conditions are met. There is no minimum period for how long a taxable person must remain in a
VAT group.

In order to create a VAT group, the members must:

* be established in Finland;

* operate in the financial and/or insurance sector, or be under the direct or indirect control of such a
company/companies, or be a holding company of a credit institution or investment service firm or a parent
entity of an insurance group, or a company under the (joint) control of these kinds of entities; and

* have close financial, economic and organisational links with each other.

In addition, pure holding companies can also be included in the VAT group provided that they meet the
above-mentioned criteria,

‘Accompany may only belong to one VAT group at a time. The VAT group must appoint from its members a
company to be responsible for the VAT group's VAT reporting. However, the members of the VAT group are
jointly and severally liable for the payment of VAT.

186 | Finland

--- Page 3 ---
The VAT group members are treated as one single taxable person for VAT purposes. Consequently, VAT is not
accounted for supplies of goods and services within the VAT group.

14 Fiscal representative

Taxable persons not established in Finland and making supplies of goods or services other than those
resulting in VAT registration liability in Finland, are upon application entitled to register for VAT purposes
in Finland (“voluntary VAT registration”). In case of a voluntary VAT registration, a fiscal representative is
needed if the taxable person is established outside the EU in a state with which Finland has not conciuded
an agreement on mutual assistance with the competent authorities as regards indirect taxes. An agreement on
mutual assistance is currently in place only with Norway.

Fiscal representatives for VAT purposes are not required in any other cases.

The fiscal representative should be domiciled in Finland, be approved by the Finnish Tax Administration, and
have the knowledge and skills required for fulfilling the administrative obligations related to VAT.

The fiscal representative is responsible for all of the foreign taxable person’s administrative duties connected
to VAT liability, However, the fiscal representative is not jointly and severally liable for the payment of VAT
and penalties. The tax authorities may require a security from the non-established taxable person.

2. Taxable transactions

21 ‘Transfer of business

Section 19c of the Finnish VAT Law implements Articles 19 and 29 of the VAT Directive

Under the following conditions, a transfer of business is not considered a sale in the scope of the VAT (and,
thus, the assets transferred are not subject to VAT either):

* assets constitute a business, or a part of il, where all of the elements transferred are sufficient to allow an
independent economic activity to be carried on as defined in case law;

+ _ assets are sold to be used in the transferee’s taxable business activities entitling to VAT deductions;

* transferee continues to conduct such business activities immediately after the transfer; and

* transferee is registered for VAT at the time of the transfer.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

VAT is payable for the goods that are taken for private consumption, are given free of charge or are
transferred or taken to a use which does not entitle for VAT deduction. VAT is payable only if the VAT was
deducted for the goods or if the goods were produced in connection with VAT taxable business.

‘The provision of samples or customary, small value promotional gifts is not subject to VAT. There is no set
value for qualifying gifts as being of ‘small value’ in the Finnish VAT rules.

VAT is payable for the services that are carried out free of charge by a taxable person for their private use or
for that of their staff or, more generally, for purposes other than those of their business. VAT is payable only if
the VAT was deducted for the acquired service, or in case of self-performed services, the service was rendered
in connection with the taxable business and the taxable person supplies similar services to external parties.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra~Community supply

Importation of goods is exempt from VAT if the transport of the imported goods ends in another EU Member
State and if the sale of goods is exempt from VAT as an intra-Community supply. This requires that the

Finland | 187

--- Page 4 ---
importer is a taxable person. In addition, the tax exemption requires that the importer has received the
customer’s VAT number issued by another EU Member State and that the transport of goods to another EU
Member State can be proven.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation ;
The possibility to defer the reporting and payment of import VAT is available for taxable persons registered
for VAT in Finland, These taxpayers declare the import VAT in their VAT returns. The deductible import VAT
can be reported in the same VAT return, which results in a situation where the import VAT does not have to
be paid as it can be deducted at the same time when it is declared, The taxpayers are also liable to calculate
the tax base and the import VAT themselves, The import VAT should be reported in the period that coincides
with the customs clearance date, Other than above mentioned taxpayers pay the import VAT to the Finnish
Customs at the time of the importation.

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

‘Asa general rule, a transfer of goods to Finland from another EU Member State is regarded as an intra-
Community acquisition in Finland creating a liability to register for VAT (see also paragraph 1,2). However,
in order to avoid non-established taxable persons having to register for VAT to make taxable acquisitions of
consignment or of call-off stock, simplified procedures can be applied for allowing the consignee to account
for the VAT on the acquisition, provided that certain conditions are met. The VAT Law is in line with the
Articles of the EU VAT Directive concerning call- off stock arrangements.

Using the simplification is optional. The supplier may also choose to VAT register in Finland.

3.2 Installation supplies

Supplies of goods with installation/assembly are treated as supplied in Finland when they are installed or
assembled in Finland, and the same supplier sells both the goods and their installation/assembly. There is
no formal definition for the terms “installation” and “assembly”, but in general, the definition is regarded as
rather easily fulfilled when any installation/assembly work is required.

The shipment of goods from another EU Member State to be installed in Finland is not deemed an intra-
Community acquisition in Finland. Instead, a supply of goods with installation in Finland is subject to Finnish
VAT.

The normal rules on the reverse charge and VAT registration of a non-established supplier apply. The
applicability of the reverse charge is fairly broad in Finland (see paragraph 8.1) and may therefore be
applied to several situations where a foreign taxable person is supplying goods with installation in Finland.
It should, however, be noted that according to guidance given by the Finnish tax authorities an installation or
construction project, or several consecutive projects, are considered to result in a fixed establishment for the
supplier if they last for more than 9 months.

33 Distance sales

Distance sales refer to the sale of goods transported to private individuals or buyers comparable to them (¢.g.,
online shopping). In the BU, distance sales of goods are taxed in the country of the consumer if the annual
turnover of distance sales of goods, as well as sale of radio and television broadcasting services, electronically
supplied services, and telecommunications services, for a business established within the BU area exceeds
10,000 euros in total.

The supplier must register for VAT in all Member States where they sell goods as distance sales. Alternatively.
the seller can register as a user of a special VAT scheme (hereinafter the Union scheme) in one EU Member
State. The Union scheme allows for centralized reporting and payment obligations through this single EU
country, thus facilitating the management of VAT-related obligations.

188 | Finland

--- Page 5 ---
For certain distance sales from outside the EU (imported goods), a centralized iOSS scheme is available for
reporting consignments not exceeding the value of EUR 150.

3.4 Simplified triangulation

Under the VAT Law, the simplified triangulation rules have been implemented in line with the EU VAT

Directive.

In triangular transactions, VAT is not chargeable in Finland in respect of an intra-Community acquisition in

the following circumstances:

* acquisition is made by a middleman (taxable person) not established in Finland providing a VAT
identification number from another EU Member State than Finland and the country of dispatch;

+ intra-Community acquisition is made by the middieman for the purpose of a subsequent supply of the
goods in Finland;

* goods acquired by the middleman are directly dispatched or transported from an EU Member State to the
person to whom the middleman makes the subsequent supply;

* _ the person to whom the subsequent supply is made is another taxable person or a non-taxable legal person
identified for VAT purposes in Finland; and

* the person to whom the subsequent supply is made has been designated as the person liable for the tax
due on the subsequent supply by the middleman not established in Finland.

The simplified triangulation rules cannot be applied if the middleman in a triangular transaction is established
in Finland, Where Fintand is the country of arrival, the wording of the Finnish legislation does not preclude
the application of the simplified triangulation rules if the middleman is VAT registered in Finland or in the
country of dispatch,

35 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons and taxable persons engaged in
exempt activities from suppliers established in another EU Member State is EUR 10,000

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Currently Finland has not implemented any rules on use and enjoyment based on Article 59a of the EU VAT
Directive.

4, Chargeable event & Chargeability of VAT

41 Principles

In the case of supply of goods, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods are
delivered. The VAT due on services, in principle, becomes chargeable at the time the services are performed.
Alternatively, a taxpayer can also charge and report the VAT according to the date of the invoice or, if no
invoice was issued, according to the date the payment was received. Precondition for such an allocation is
that the invoice was issued or the payment was received during the same financial year as the delivery of
the goods or the performance of the services took place. At the end of the fiscal year, the taxpayer is liable
to report and pay VAT for the supplies that took place during the fiscal year in question, but for which the
taxpayer has not yet issued an invoice or received a payment.

4.2 Advance payments
Ifthe payment is received before the goods are delivered or the service is performed, the VAT for the supply
becomes chargeable according to the date the payment or part of the payment was received.

4.3 Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies of goods are considered being delivered and services performed at the end of each
accounting period and the VAT becomes chargeable at the end of each accounting period. For example,

Finland | 189

--- Page 6 ---
in case of a lease subject to VAT for which monthly instalments are paid, the service is considered being
performed on a monthly basis

Continuous supplies of goods over a period of more than one calendar month which are dispatched or
transported from Finland to another EU Member State and which are supplied or (ransferced VAT exempt,
shall be regarded as being delivered on expiry of each calendar month until such time as the supply comes to
an end. Also, the continuous acquisition of goods over a period of more than one calendar month, which are
dispatched or transported from another EU Member State to Finland and for which the acquirer is liable to
account the VAT, shall be regarded as being delivered on expiry of each calendar month until such time as the
supply comes to an end,

Services ongoing over a period of more than one year, that do not give rise to settlements or payments in that
period and for which the customer is liable to account for the VAT on the basis of Article 196 of the EU VAT
Directive, are regarded as being supplied on the expiry of each calendar year up to the time the provision of
the services ceases.

44 Cash accounting

In some cases, VAT is paid over to the Finnish state on the basis of cash received during the respective VAT period
as opposed to making payment on the basis of supplied goods and services during the period. The cash accounting
regime is available to taxable persons whose annual turnover does not exceed EUR $00,000 and to taxable
persons who do not have a legal obligation to keep books. When applying cash accounting, the VAT should
however be paid after 12 months have passed since the delivery of the goods or performance of the services.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad Debt Relief

A bad debt relief is available to taxable persons who can prove that the amount of VAT they did not receive
from the customer became a bad debt in the relevant period.

When claiming a bad debt relief, the taxable person deducts the amount of VAT it is reclaiming from the
output VAT on the supplies for the period in which the loss is considered to have arisen in accordance
with good bookkeeping practice. The supplier must be able to show that a bad debt exists, and it must
have information showing that the customer lacks the financial means to pay the debt (e.g, bankruptcy or
unsuccessful execution attempt).

If the taxable person later receives the payment of the VAT, it will have to account for the VAT to the tax
authorities.

5.2 Cash Discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
25.5% VAT EUR 255
Total to be paid EUR 1,255

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount.”

In the described scenario, input and output VAT should be adjusted by the cash discount used. The Finnish
Tax Administration considers that suppliers and customers may adjust their input and output VAT without

an obligation for the suppliers to issue separate credit notes for these frequent commercial cash discounts.
However, either the discounted total payable amount including VAT or the amount of the discount including,
the VAT should be ineluded in the invoice. The end result is that both parties pay/deduct the VAT based on the
amount actually received/paid.

190 | Finland

--- Page 7 ---
53 Open Market Value

In regard to Article 80 of the EU VAT Directive and possibilities to implement the open market value in
transactions between related parties, Finland has only implemented Article 80(1)(a). This rule allows the
Finnish Tax Administration to implement the open market value only when the consideration paid for a sale
or intra-Community acquisition is lower than the open market value, and the recipient of the supply does not
have a full right of deduction. The open market value rule is not applied if the supplier is not liable to pay
VAT based on the rules for free of charge supplies.

54 Returnable Packaging

The VAT base can be adjusted based on the consideration received for the sale of retuned packaging. The
supplier must provide the buyer with a credit invoice for the returned packages if the consideration for the
packaging was included in the taxable amount, Reference to the original invoice is required on the credit
invoice only if this is feasible.

6. VAT rates

6.1 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate is 25.5%.

6.2 Reduced rate

There are two reduced VAT rates in use in Finland. As of 1 January 2025, most of the goods and services
previously subject to the reduced rate of 10% were transferred to the scope of the reduced rate of 14%. At the
same time, menstrual products, incontinence products and diapers were transferred from the standard rate to
the reduced rate of 14%.

The reduced rate of 14% is applied to the following goods and services:

+ food;

+ restaurant and catering services;

* animal food;

+ books (both physical and digital);

+ domestic passenger transport;

+ transfer of the right to use premises for accommodation or harbour facilities:

+ services for physical exercise including use of facilities used for physical exercise;

» admission fees to theatres, circuses, musical and dancing performances, cinemas, exhibitions, sports
events, zoos, museums and other corresponding cultural and recreational events and institutions including
the streaming of such events;

+ medicines;

+ menstrual products, incontinence products, and diapers;

* certain works of art;

+ performance fees charged by performing artists, other performers and athletes who have opted for VAT
liability; and

+ compensation related to copyright when paid to an organisation representing copyright holders.

The reduced rate of 14% is not applicable to alcoholic beverages and tobacco products.

‘The reduced rate of 10% is applied to sales of physical and digital newspapers and periodicals and, until 31
December 2025, to the amounts received by the Finnish Broadcasting Company from the State Television and
Radio Fund and the compensations received by Alands Radio och TV Ab from the media fees collected in the
Aland region. As of | January 2026, the amounts received by the previously mentioned broadcasting entities
will be transferred to the reduced rate of 14%.

Finland | 19)

--- Page 8 ---
63 Super-reduced and zero rates

There is no super-reduced rate in Finland.

Finland has a zero rate for:

+ sales of an edition of a newspaper or periodical by the printer, published at least 4 times a year, to a non-
profit organisation which publishes the newspaper or periodical mainly for its members;

+ sales of gold to the Central Bank; and

+ sale of vessels over 10 metres long (excluding those used for sports and leisure).

7. Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with the right of deduction

The exemption from VAT with a right of deduction applies to the following supplies of goods and services

related to international trade and trade under special regimes (warehouses):

* — intra-Community supplies and exports;

+ _ sales of certain services in connection with international trade with third countries;

= international passenger transport;

* — transport of goods to and from Madeira and the Azores;

+ intermediation with regard to certain VAT exempt goods and services;

* — services of travel agents when they relate to the goods or services provided to the immediate needs of the
customers by businesses outside of the EU;

+ sales of goods and services for the needs of vessels used for navigation on the high seas and carrying
passengers for reward or used for the purpose of commercial, industrial or fishing activities, or to aircraft
used by airlines operating for reward chiefly on international routes, including sales and rental of aircraft;

+ sales of goods under VAT warehousing procedure or customs control;

+ sales of certain services performed on goods under VAT warehousing procedure or customs control;

+ goods supplied to travellers resident outside the EU under retail export schemes or through tax-free shops
under certain conditions;

+ _ sales of goods and services to those travelling abroad on vessels or aircraft;

+ sales to certain diplomatic bodies and similar; and

+ _ sales of financial and insurance services to businesses established outside of the EU.

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

A precondition for the zero rating in case of an intra~-Community supply is that the customer is registered as
a taxable person in another EU Member State and the goods are transported to another EU Member State.
The supplier must prove that the transport to another FU Member State has taken place by means of a bill
of lading, transport invoice or some other corresponding document regarding the transport. These rules can
still be used in order to prove the transport besides the presumptions as set out in Article 45a of the Council
Implementing Regulation 282/2011

7.1.2 Export supplies

In order to prove zero rating in case of exports, the supplier must have evidence that the goods have left the
EU and, in certain cases, prove the status of the customer. The evidence required depends on the type of
export.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

In Finland, the VAT warehouse regime can be applied to the raw materials listed in Annex V of the VAT

Directive, such as mineral oils, grain, cocoa beans, coffee, certain metals etc, and pulp (CN codes 4701
4706). The warehouse keeper is required to obtain a Finnish tax authorities’ licence for keeping a VAT

warehouse.

192 | Finland

--- Page 9 ---
The supplies, intra-Commmunity acquisitions and imports of goods which are placed in the VAT warehouse
are exempt from VAT, Also, services performed on the goods in the VAT warchouse are VAT exempted.
Processing of the goods is not allowed.

VAT becomes due when the goods are released from the VAT warehouse regime. The customer releasing the
goods from the VAT warehouse becomes liable for the payment of the VAT. At the same time the warehouse
keeper is jointly liable for the VAT except in cases where the warehouse keeper acts in good faith or where no
fault or negligence can be imputed to him

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

Excise goods under suspension of excise duties can be stored in an excise warehouse. Supply and transfer

of goods, that are meant for certain purposes of equipping an aircraft used in international traffic, to be sold
on an aircraft or on a vessel used in international traffic or to travellers travelling to outside the EU, is VAT
exempt when the goods are placed in an excise warehouse. Certain mineral oils placed in an excise warehouse
are also exempted from VAT.

7.1.3.3 Bonded warehouse

It is possible to place goods in a bonded warehouse in Finland. A licence obtained by a warehouse keeper
from the customs authorities is required for a bonded warehouse in Finland.

If the goods coming from a non-EU country, which are not declared for release into free circulation, are
placed under a bonded warehouse regime, no customs duties and VAT are due. When the goods are released
from the warehouse for free circulation, duties and VAT become due.

The supply of goods within a bonded warehouse is exempt, The possible supplies of services related to goods
placed in a warehouse are also exempted, provided that they are supplied within the warehouse. Processing of
the goods is however not allowed.

7.2 Exemptions without right to deduction

The following exempt supplies do not give the right to deduct or obtain a refund of input VAT:

* health and medical care;

* social welfare;

+ sale and letting of real estate and rights relating to immovable property (with some exceptions which are
mentioned below);

+ insurance services;

+ financial services;

* general, vocational and higher education:

+ sales of investment gold;

* lotteries and betting;

+ certain fees to performing artists;

* fees for using certain copyrights of an author;

* certain cemetery services;

+ self-picked wild berries and mushrooms, sold by the person who picked them;

+ interpretation services for the deaf;

+ certain sales made by blind persons;

* universal postal service;

* transactions concerning bank notes and coins used as a legal tender, with the exception of collector’s
items; and

+ sales of goods which have been subjected to restriction of input VAT deduction.

Even though sales and letting of real estate and supply of easement or similar rights relating to immovable
property are exempt from VAT, the following services are taxable at the standard rate:

+ _ sales of construction services;
+ sales of the right to take soil or stone substance, or sales of logging rights, hunting or fishing rights;
* transfer of rights to use rooms, camping sites, cabins or other similar premises in connection with hotel

Finland | 193

--- Page 10 ---
and camping activities and comparable accommodation activities;

+ occasional transfers of the right to use conference, exhibition or sports premises or other similar premises;

+ rental of parking space for vehicles;

+ supply of harbour or airfield facilities for use by vessels or aircraft;

+ rental of equipment or a machine permanently installed in a building or construction;

+ transfer of rights to use safe-deposit boxes;

+ supply of publicity or advertising space, places for amusement machines or soft drink dispensers, or for
other similar devices, or for games, or immovable property; and

+ supply to the State of comprehensive maintenance services regarding a public road or railroad.

7.3. Option for taxation

Lessor can opt for VAT for letting immovable property if the leased premises are used for activities which
give a right to VAT deduction or the end-user is the state, university, university of applied sciences or a
provider of private vocational education. VAT-taxable letting of immovable property requires a specific VAT
registration. Retrospective VAT registration for letting immovable property is not possible, except in case
of letting a new property, in which case the VAT registration can be applied within six months after the new
property has been taken into use.

Under certain circumstances it is also possible to opt for VAT for the supply of investment gold. Also
performing artists can opt for VAT. It is not possible to opt for VAT on financial services.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charge

In Finland, the reverse charge mechanism is — besides the reverse charge of Articles 44 and 196 of the EU
VAT Directive services - applicable to various types of transactions. In addition, there is also the reverse
charge for the supply of natural gas and electricity supplied by non-resident suppliers to taxable dealers in
Finland.

81.1 General reverse charge mechanism - non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)

Reverse charge applies to most services and the supply of goods taxable in Finland made by a foreign taxable

person not having a fixed establishment in Finland to a Finnish established and VAT registered taxable person.

This means that the customer is liable to account for the VAT on the supply. The domestic reverse charge

cannot, however, be applied in case of the following supplies:

+ the customer is a foreign taxable person who does not have a fixed establishment and who is not VAT
registered in Finland;

+ passenger transport services; and

+ admissions to cultural, artistic, sporting, scientific, educational, entertainment or similar events, such as
fairs and exhibitions, or of ancillary services related to the admission.

The reverse charge applies to the supply of goods by a foreign established taxable person even if he is VAT
registered in Finland, provided that he does not have a fixed establishment in Finland. When the conditions
for the applicability as described above are fulfilled, the application of the reverse charge is mandatory.
However, the reverse charge cannot be applied if the foreign taxable person has VAT registered through a
voluntary VAT registration.

‘The invoice must show the customer’s Finnish VAT number and indicate that the customer is liable for VAT
(e.g. the phrase “reverse charge” on the invoice)

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charge (Article 199 Directive)

194 | Finland

--- Page 11 ---
8.1.2.1 Construction sector

In Finland, the reverse charge is applied to certain construction services. When the reverse charge applies, the

customer is liable to account for the VAT on the supplies of construction services. Construction sector targeted

reverse charge is applied when both conditions are met:

+ the supply is considered either a construction service or renting of staff for construction services for VAT
purposes:

* _ aservice is regarded as a construction service when construction or repair is performed to an
immovable property, including the supply of goods relating to such service.

+ the scope of the services to which the reverse charge for the construction sector applies is not clearly
defined. General guidelines do exist, but there is some room for interpretation. A full case-specific
assessment needs to be done. Under the Finnish case law, the scope of the construction sector has
been considered to be rather wide;

+ the customer is a company supplying construction services or renting staff for construction services

more often than occasionally. These rules are also applied if the customer does not carry out construction

activities itself but resells construction services to companies supplying construction services more often

than occasionally.

These rules apply both to resident and non-resident customers and suppliers. The application of the reverse
charge is mandatory when these conditions are fulfilled.

The reverse charge mechanism applies to e.g. the following services connected with immovable property:
* excavation and foundation work;

* construction work;

+ construction installation;

+ finishing of constructions;

* rental contracts covering both equipment and operator for the construction work;

* on-site cleaning of constructions; and

+ supply of staff to a site.

The reverse charge mechanism does not generally apply to e.g. the following services:

+ installation of equipment used for production;

+ architectural services, planning and design work, other engineering and technical services;
* rental contracts covering only the equipment;

* outdoor cleaning services (including roads, driveways, parks, airports);

* — transport; and

+ building maintenance.

‘The invoice must show the customer’s Finnish VAT number and include a reference to the reverse charge (e.g.
the text “reverse charge — section 8 ¢ of the Finnish VAT Act”).

8.1.2.2 Emission rights

The reverse charge applies to supplies of emission rights provided that the customer is VAT registered.

8.1.2.3 Sales of scrap metal and metal waste

The reverse charge applies to supplies of scrap metal and metal waste provided that the customer is VAT
registered, The scope of the goods subject to this mandatory reverse charge is determined based on the goods’
CN codes.

8.2 di nt and Several Liability

‘The partners of a general partnership and the general partners of a limited partnership are jointly and severally
liable for the partnership’s tax. Similarly, the members of a consortium or a ship-owning company are liable
for the tax of the consortium or company.

‘The members of the VAT group are jointly and severally liable for the group's VAT liabilities.

Finland | 195

--- Page 12 ---
‘The joint and several liability of a partner, a consortium member and a member of a VAT group begins at the
start of the month during which they join the partnership, consortium or VAT group and ends by the end of the
month in which they leave the partnership, consortium or VAT group.

9. Recovery of input VAT

9.1 Non-recoverable input VAT

Taxable persons cannot recover VAT on the following:

+ goods and services not used for taxable business;

+ premises in private or recreational use by a taxable person or ils staff, including goods and services
connected with it or its use;

+ goods and services related to commuting to a regular working place by a taxable person or his staff,

* goods and services used for business entertainment purposes;

+ passenger cars, motorcycles, and caravans, as well as goods and services related to their use including but
not limited to lease, repairs and fuel; and

+ vessels and aircraft which by their structure are mainly intended for recreational or sports purposes, as,
well as goods and services related to their use.

‘The restriction on the right to recover VAT on vehicles and vessels does not apply to a vehicle or vessel that
has been acquired for sale, rental, or use in professional passenger transport or driving instruction, nor to a
passenger car that has been acquired exclusively for use that entitles to a deduction.

92 Excess input VAT

Excess inpul VAT is immediately recoverable and refunded by the tax authorities, potentially after a request
for additional verification from the tax authorities.

93 Adjustment periods

In Finland, special treatment for capital goods is restricted to construction and renovation services including
certain equipment when they become a part of the immovable property as a result of their installation.

The adjustment period for construction and renovation services related to immovable property is 10 years.

‘The first year of adjustment is the year in which the construction or rentovation work is completed, or the
immovable property is taken into use.

9.4 VAT refund to EU taxable persons

The refund application must be submitted electronically through the portal of the tax authorities of the EU
Member State in which the taxable person is established.

A taxable person can apply for VAT refund through the portal provided that he does not have a fixed
establishment in Finland and he does not perform any taxable activities in Finland other than the following
supplies:

+ sales subject to the reverse charge mechanism;

+ _ sales to the Finnish state; and

+ sale of certain zero-rated transport and related services.

If the taxpayer performs intra-Community supplies, exports or other zero-rated activities in Finland (besides

the ones mentioned above), he cannot apply for VAT refund through the refund application. Instead, he is
required to register for VAT to refund the VAT.

196 | Finland

--- Page 13 ---
The annual minimum limit (for applications that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar
year) is FUR 50. The interim minimum limit (for applications that are made for less than a calendar year but
at least 3 months) is EUR 400.

The minimum period for an application is 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year, unless itis the
remainder of the calendar year, The maximum period for an application is a calendar year

‘The deadline for the application is 30 September of the year following the year for which the application is
made.

Supporting documents (e.g. the original invoices / copies of invoices) are generally not required. However,
the tax authorities may request for additional information,

The tax authorities must issue a decision on the refund application within 4 months after receiving the
application. The period in which the authorities must make their decision is extended to 6 months or 8 months
if the authorities have requested additional information.

Ita refund is granted, it will be processed within 10 business days and paid to the bank account number
provided to the tax authorities.

Ifthe processing time of the application exceeds what is prescribed, and payment is delayed, interest will be
paid on the refund. However, no interest will be payable if the applicant has failed to provide information or
additional information requested. The interest rate corresponds to the interest paid on VAT refund payments to
Finnish taxable persons,

The restrictions stipulated by the Finnish VAT Law on a taxable person's right to deduct also apply to VAT
refunds paid to foreign taxable persons (see paragraph 9.1),

Refund decisions may be appealed by lodging a written appeal. The appeal should be addressed to the Board
of Adjustment and sent to the Tax Administration either in Finnish or Swedish. Letters of appeal cannot be
submitted electronically.

Appeals should be submitted within 3 years after the end of the calendar year to which the refund period
relates. However, the appeal period is always at least 60 days after the date when the decision was
communicated to the applicant (excluding the notification day),

95 VAT refund to non-EU taxable persons

‘The right to a VAT refund is not reciprocal, i.e. it also applies to taxable persons from countries without a
corresponding refund system.

The taxable person can apply for a VAT refund with the application provided that he does not have a fixed
establishment in Finland, and he does not perform any taxable activities in Finland other than the following
supplies:

+ _ sales subject to the reverse charge mechanism;

* sales to the Finnish state; and

+ sale of certain zero-rated transport and related services,

If the taxpayer performs intra~Community supplies, exports or other zero-rated activities in Finland (besides
the ones mentioned above), he cannot apply for VAT refund through the refiund application. Instead, he is
required to register for VAT to refund the VAT.

The application is made by filling out and submitting an application form in Finnish, Swedish or English,
An original certificate issued by the tax authorities in the applicant's home country stating the nature of the
applicant's business operations, issued not more than one year before the application, must be enclosed. The
tax authorities may also request additional information such as invoices or import documents,

Finland | 197

--- Page 14 ---
‘The application form is available for download on the Tax Administration's webpage:
https://tinyurl.combyujmedfa

The annual minimum limit (for applications that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar
year) is EUR 50, The interim minimum limit (for applications that are made for less than a calendar year but
at least 3 months) is EUR 400.

‘The minimum period for making the application is 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year, unless
it is the remainder of the calendar year. The maximum period for making the application is one calendar year.

‘The deadline for making the application is 30 June of the year following the year for which the application is
made.

It should be noted that a refund can only be made to a bank account ina financial institution located in an EU
Member State.

‘There are no prescribed processing times, but they are usually between 2 to 8 months as of the filing date of
the application,

Refund decisions may be appealed by lodging a written complaint addressed to the Board of Adjustment
and sent to the Tax Administration either in Finnish or Swedish. Letters of appeal cannot be submitted
electronically. Appeals should be submitted within 3 years after the end of the calendar year to which the
refund period relates. However, the appeal period is always at least 60 days after the date when the decision
was communicated to the applicant (excluding the notification day).

‘The restrictions in the Finnish VAT Law regarding the right to deduction also apply to VAT refunds for
foreign taxable persons (see paragraph 9.1).

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

Invoices must contain the following information for VAT purposes:

+ date of issue;

+ sequential number, based on one or more series, which uniquely identifies the invoice;

«VAY number of the taxable person who supplied the goods or services to which the invoice relates;

© customer's VAT number in case of the reverse charge and intra-Community supplies;

+ full names and addresses of the supplier and customer;

+ quantity and nature of the goods supplied or the extent and nature of the services rendered;

+ date on which the goods or services were supplied or the date on which a prepayment on account was
made;

+ price exclusive of VAT per VAT rate, displaying the unit price and any rebates, discounts and credits (if
not included in the unit price);

+ VAT rate applied;

+ amount of VAT payable;

+ incase of an exemption, a reference to the applicable provision of the VAT Directive, or to the
corresponding national provision, or any other reference indicating that the supply of goods or services is
exempt;

= where the customer is liable for payment of VAT, the following reference to the reverse charge: “reverse
charge” (“kdidnnetty verovelvollisuus” in Finnish);

«if the customer has prepared the invoice, the invoice must include a reference to self-billing: “selF-billing”
(or “itselaskutus” in Finnish);

+ incase ofa sale of a new means of transport to another EU Member State, the invoice needs to contain

198 | Finland

--- Page 15 ---
the details necessary to identify such goods:

* incase of applying the VAT margin scheme when second-hand goods, works of art, antiques and
collector's items or services of travel agents are supplied, the applicable reference:
* margin scheme, second-hand goods (voittomarginaalijerjestelma — kdytetyt tavarat” in Finnish);
* margin scheme, works of att (“voittomarginaalijeirjestelmd — taide-esineet” in Finnish);
* margin scheme, collector’s items and antiques (Cvoittomarginaalijérjestelmda — keréily-ja

antiikkiesineet” in Finnish); and

* margin scheme, travel agents (“voittomarginaalijairjestelmé ~ matkatoimistot” in Finnish);

+ _ incase of taxable supplies of investment gold, an indication concerning this; and

+ if an invoice is amended, a reference to the original invoice.

10.1.2 Time limits

The Finnish VAT Law only sets time limits for invoices for intra-Community supplies and supplies of services
for which the reverse charge applies under Articles 44 and 196 of the Directive. For these supplies, invoices
must be issued by the 15% day of the month following the month in which the goods were delivered, or
services supplied.

10.1.3 Currency
Itis allowed to issue invoices in a foreign currency. The amount of the VAT payable must be in BUR,

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

Foreign currencies are converted into EUR by using the latest selling price published by a commercial bank
or the European Central Bank for the date the VAT becomes due in accordance with the rules on tax point.

10.1.5 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

* — date of issue;

+ name and VAT number of the supplier;

* quantity and nature of the goods supplied or the extent and nature of the services rendered: and
* amount of VAT payable per VAT rate or taxable amount per VAT rate.

Simplified invoices may be used in the following situations:

+ when the total amount is not higher than EUR 400;

+ in retail trade that sell almost exclusively to consumers;

* for restaurant and catering services or passenger transport not meant for resale; and
* receipts issued by parking meters and similar automatic vending machines.

10.1.6 Self-billing

In Finland, self-billing is allowed if the supplier and customer have agreed on it and if an approval procedure
hhas been put in place allowing the supplier to approve the invoices. The supplier remains responsible for the
correctness of the invoices. Invoices must include a reference to self-billing.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices must be stored for at least 6 years after the end of the year in which the accounting period, to which
the invoice relates, ended. Regarding real estate investments, the storage period is 13 years after the end of
the calendar year, in which the accounting period to which the invoices relate, ended.

Invoices may be stored abroad, but they must be accessible from Finland to auditors and authorities without
delay. The authenticity of the origin, the integrity of the content and the legibility of the invoices must be
ensured from the moment of issue until the end of the period for their storage.

Records for supplies under the special scheme for distance sales should be kept for a period of 10 years from
the end of the year during which the transactions were carried out.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

In Finland, electronic invoicing is allowed provided that the invoicing and archiving method meets the
requirements set out in VAT and bookkeeping legislation, Taxable persons may choose any form of electronic

Finland | 199

--- Page 16 ---
invoicing as long as the authenticity of origin, integrity of content and readability are guaranteed. Customers
are allowed to refuse electronic invoices.

10.2 Compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

In general, taxable persons are required to file VAT returns on a monthly basis. However, taxable persons with
‘an annual turnover of not more than EUR 100,000 may report VAT on a quarterly basis. Furthermore, taxable
persons with an annual turnover of not more than EUR 30,000 may report VAT on an annual basis. These
longer reporting periods are only available upon application or the tax authorities’ decision.

Electronic filing of VAT returns is mandatory. Submission of paper returns is only allowed in exceptional
situations.
10.2.2 Filing of a VAT return and payment

‘The VAT return is due on the 12 day of the second month following the tax period to which it relates. If an
annual reporting period is used, the VAT return is due on the last day of February in the following year.

Even if there are no transactions to report, a nil return must be submitted.
The VAT payable is due on the 12 day of the second month following the tax period to which it relates.

‘A copy of the VAT return can be found at:
hups:/Ainyurl.com/S49pk9mnf
10.2.3. European sales listing

Suppliers selling goods or services to taxable persons in other EU Member States must file a European sales
listing (a recapitulative statement), No threshold applies. The due date for filing the European sales listing is
the 20" of the calendar month following the month of the supply.

The monthly European sales listing must be submitted electronically. Submission of paper returns is only
allowed in exceptional situations.

A copy of the electronic form for European sales list can be downloaded from: https./tinyurl.com/44xp86az

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

When applying the call-off stock simplification, the transfers of goods to the call-off stock in another EU
Member State need to be reported in a call-off stock report. This is an enclosure to the European sales listing.

The due date for filing the call-off stock report is the 20" of the calendar month following the month of the
transfer. The return must be submitted for the month in which the transfer to the call-off stock began.

A copy of the electronic form for call-off stock report can be downloaded from:
hutps.//tinyurl.com/bdd7dfew

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

‘The tax assessment period is 3 years from the end of the relevant tax period. This means that in the year 2025,
the last year open for assessment (payable or reclaimable VAT) is the year 2022.

‘Under certain circumstances, the tax authorities may extend the period by deciding on an extension before
the normal tax assessment period has been reached. This is an exceptional procedure meant for material cases
under certain specific circumstances. In addition, in case of tax frauds the tax authorities may impose VAT
until the end of the calendar year following the year in which such a crime was prosecuted,

200 | Finiand

--- Page 17 ---
10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration
In Finland an administrational penalty of up to EUR 5,000 may be imposed for late VAT registration.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

Ifa VAT return is submitted after the due date, the Tax Administration will impose a late filing penalty of
EUR 3 per day for the first 45 days after the due date until the VAT return is submitted (i.e. up to EUR 135).
If the return is submitted later than 45 days after the due date, the late filing penalty is EUR 135 plus 2% of
the amount of the VAT payable. In case VAT is reported late on a revised VAT return submitted more than 45
days after the due date, the amount of late filing penalty is 2% of the VAT payable not reported earlier. When
imposing a late filing penalty, the excess amount of refundable VAT reported is equated with the VAT payable
reported late. In any case, the maximum amount of the late filing penalty imposed on the basis of VAT
payable is EUR 15,000 per tax period (typically a calendar month)

If European sales listing is submitted after the due date or if it is incomplete or incorrect, the Tax
Administration may impose a penalty amounting to BUR 200. Penalties are usually imposed for continuous
failures only, or after failing to meet the tax authorities” specific request to submit or correct a report.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

If VAT has not been paid on time, late payment interest must be paid. Late payment interest is 11.5% in 2025.
‘The rate can be adjusted once a year. Interest will start to accrue on the next day following the due date and it
will stop accruing on the date of payment, both days included.

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessment when VAT is not reported correctly

Penalties (so-called ‘tax increase’) are raised if persons liable for tax have paid less tax than what was due,
have submitted incorrect information or have neglected entirely their obligation to submit tax returns,

‘The amount of the penalty depends on the severity and frequency of the failure as follows:

* typically 10% of the VAT payable;

+ in the range of 15 to 50% of the VAT payable when one’s obligations are neglected frequently or one’s
actions evidence disregard for one’s tax liabilities;

* 25% for VAT the amount of which is estimated by the tax authorities in the absence of information from
the taxable person; and

+ fixed amount of up to EUR 200 in cases where there was no VAT payable.

10.3.5 Offence penalties

Please see paragraph 10.3.4 for administrational sanctions in case of gross negligence. If actions are evaluated
from a criminal law point of view, criminal sanctions governed by the Finnish Criminal Code may be
imposed.

10.4 Intrastat

The monthly Intrastat return must be filed to the Finnish Customs by the 10" working day of the month
following the reporting month.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

In 2025 the threshold triggering the obligation to provide information is EUR 800,000 for arrivals and EUR
800,000 for dispatches.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported
The following information must be reported:

+ statistical period;

* commodity code;

* country of consignment (arrivals)/country of destination (dispatches);
* country of origin;

Finland | 201

--- Page 18 ---
+ nature of transactions;

+ mode of transport;

+ net weight in kilograms;

+ supplementary unit (e.g. litres, items, pairs elc.);

+ invoice value;

+ statistical value (voluntary information); and

+ invoice value/statistical value in another currency and currency conversion;
+ the VAT ID of the trading partner; and

* the country of origin for departures.

It is also obligatory to file Intrastat reports for distance sales if the threshold is exceeded.

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

There are no additional requirements.

VAT exemption up to EUR 27,500.

‘The following operations trigger a VAT registration: domestic sale of goods to customers not VAT registered

identification number. A retroactive VAT registration is only possible in France in certain cases and upon

13 VAT grouping

13.1 VAT Group

The optional VAT grouping regime (“assujetti unique” in French) based on Article 256 C of the VAT code
(transposing Article 11 of the EU VAT directive) is only reserved to members established in France (fixed
establishments located abroad are excluded) or fixed establishments in France of foreign companies, which
are linked from a financial, economic or organisational standpoint.

204 | France

--- Page 21 ---
Option must be made by 31 October of the preceding year and will last at least 3 years with the same
perimeter, Then additions and the exit must be declared by 31 October of the preceding year. The list of
members must be notified by 31 January each year.

Members will lose their independent status of taxpayer as the group is the unique taxable person with a
specific VAT number. Members are defined as sectors of activity (notably for options for taxation in the
financial sector) and remain jointly liable for the payment of the tax

‘There is no specific rule regarding the supplies among group members. ‘The rules applying to operations
between totally distinct taxable persons also apply to supplies among group members. Pure holding
companies cannot make part of the VAT group.

13.2 VAT consolidation

France implemented an optional VAT consolidation regime. It only allows pooling the debit/credit VAT
positions of the companies belonging to the group in order to provide a cash advantage.

The application of such regime is subject to the following cumulative conditions:

+ each member of the group, including the head taxable person, must file electronic corporate tax returns
to the “Direction des Grandes Entreprises” (DGE) which are the relevant authorities for large companies
(with a turnover exceeding EUR 400,000,000). Non-established taxable persons carrying out taxable
operations and having appointed a fiscal representative in France cannot be affiliated to the DGE even if
the fiscal representative is affiliated to the DGE;

the VAT consolidation regime in France is an option whereby each member must elect to participate in
the VAT consolidation group. The option is applied by the head taxable person after having received the
express agreement of the members to participate in the VAT consolidation group. The option takes effect
as of the first day of the first following accounting period and must be made prior to the beginning of this
accounting period;

+ the head taxable person must hold at least 50% of the capital or voting rights of each member. This
holding must be continuous over the period covered by the option;

* the accounting periods of the members must start and end on the same dates; and

+ the members must be registered under the same VAT regime (“régime réel normal”) and file monthly VAT
returns.

If one of the above-mentioned conditions is not met, the VAT consolidation regime ceases to apply. Under the

VAT consolidation regime, the following rules apply:

+ each member of the group, including the head taxable person, has its own VAT registration number and
must file its own periodical VAT return. The members are, however, relieved from the payment of VAT, if
any. Also, a VAT credit incurred by a member during the period of application of the VAT consolidation
regime cannot be carried over on the next VAT return of the said member. The VAT credit can only be
used by the head taxable person as stated below; and

* the head taxable person of the VAT consolidation group aggregates the VAT debts and VAT credits
incurred by each member of the group, i.e. the head taxable person files, in addition to its own VAT
return, a single VAT return on a monthly basis stating the balance between alll tax due and all tax credits
generated by the members of the group. If the balance is positive, the head taxable person must pay the
VAT due. If the balance is negative, the head taxable person can either carry forward this VAT credit to the
next single VAT retum or ask for its refund.

Although the liable entity of the VAT consolidation group towards the French authorities is the head taxable
person, each member remains jointly liable for payment of VAT and corresponding penalties, if any.

‘There is no specific rule regarding the supplies among group members. The rules applying to operations
between totally distinct taxable persons also apply to supplies among group members.

France | 205

--- Page 22 ---
L4 Fiscal representative

Non-EU established taxable persons must VAT register by appointing a tax representative duly accredited

by the French tax administration. The representative is personally liable for the payment of VAT to the
French tax administration. The accreditation process takes into consideration the fiscal morality, the technical
capacity and the financial solvency of the tax representative.

This obligation to appoint a tax representative in France is waived for non-EU taxable persons which are
established in countries with which France has a legal instrument of mutual assistance similar in scope to that
laid down by EU law (Directive 2010/24/BU dated 16 March 2010 and Council Regulation Nr. 904/2010 of 7
October 2010).
