15 Limited fiscal representative/ Agent

The limited fiscal representative ceased to exist as of 31/12/2024.

The limited fiscal agent regime for foreign companies with no obligation to appoint a fiscal representative is
maintained.

A limited fiscal representative (“Mandataire fiscal ponctue!”) can be appointed by EU taxable persons (and
those established in the countries listed in above-mentioned Ministerial Order dated 16 February 2021)
performing exclusively the following operations for which no VAT is due:
the release of goods from a customs or VAT warehouse, when the goods are immediately followed by an
export or an exempt intra-Community supply (Article 277-A- II-4 of the VAT Code); and
* imports of goods which are immediately followed by an exempt intra-Community supply after customs
clearance (Article 291-III-4° of the VAT Code; customs regime 4200).

The limited fiscal agent must be established and VAT registered in France. It must be accredited by the tax
office for foreign residents (DINR).

A non-EU taxable person can appoint different limited tax representatives for each of the above-mentioned
transactions (e.g. when they are performed in different places).

The limited fiscal representative is granted a specific VAT number dedicated to the above-mentioned
operations performed by all non-EU taxable persons he may represent. He must file a quarterly return to the
tax office for foreign residents (DINR) including all the operations performed by the EU taxable persons he
represents, as well as monthly Intrastat returns and European sales listings,

1.6 New Mandataire regime for imports

This regime is implemented as from | January 2025.
This regime is implemented in France since 01/01/2025 (Art. 289 A bis of the French tax code)

A company which is not established nor VAT identified in France can appoint one or more Mandataires

(‘agents’) who will take care of its import VAT obligations (filing returns, payments, VAT recovery or refund,

keeping ledgers). This regime applies when the sole operations carried out by this business are:

- imports for which VAT is fully deductible;

- transactions on goods with third countries on which VAT does not apply (zero-rated, exempt from
payment or suspended), referred to in new Article 95 ter of annex IIT to the French tax code.

‘The Mandataire must meet all the conditions below:

- He must be established in and VAT identified in France since at least | year and be accredited as
Mandataire by its tax office.

- He must not have committed, himself or his manager(s) in the case of a legal entity, serious or repeated
breaches of tax provisions, nor have been sanctioned for a management error that contributed to a
shortfall in assets or for personal bankruptcy, nor have been prohibited from managing the company

France | 207

--- Page 24 ---
for the previous three years, nor be subject to a management prohibition order in the process of being
enforced, He must have filed for at least ! year periodical VAT returns.

- He has a written mandate from the foreign company, specifying the period of application and the
conditions under which the principal entrusts goods in France to the Mandataire.

- The goods must be consigned to the Mandataire in the frame of an agreement for a consignment-stock,
work on goods, assembly, processing, renting, storage, or transit to another territory than France.

‘The Mandataire bears all the obligations (filing, payment, ledgers) on the transactions declared for the foreign
company. He is personally liable for any tax or penalties due. He must keep a registry of the operations
performed by the foreign company.

Under this regime, the foreign company operates in France under the specific global VAT number that is
assigned to its Mandataire. The foreign business does not need to get its own French VAT number,

The Mandataire will have 2 separate VAT filing obligations, one under its standard French VAT registration
for its own operations, the other one under its global VAT number as Mandataire for the operations performed
by the foreign company.

2. Taxable transactions

21 ‘Transfer of business

France implemented the option to not tax the transfer ofa going concern, as provided for in Article 257 bis of
the VAT Code.

The French official documentation states that the notion of a transfer of a going concern should be interpreted
in the light of the EC] Case Law. This notion represents “the transfer of a business or an independent part of
a business, including tangible elements and, if necessary, intangible elements which, together, constitute a
business or part of it capable of carrying on an autonomous economic activity.” The transfer of going concern
does not cover the transfer of goods and services made in favour of a taxable person who does not want to
operate the business and who will immediately liquidate the business concerned. This is the case when there
is a simple transfer of assets without any transfer of business or restructuring.

Also regarded as transfer of a totality of assets or part thereof, is the transfer of part of the assets, for example,
during the restructuring of a group (provision of a complete industry, including complete industry composed
of all the elements invested in a division of the company which is technically an independent business, that

is to say an entity capable of functioning by its own means under conditions qualified as normal in the sector
concerned).

When the transfer takes place between two taxable persons, this transfer is outside the svope of VAT (Article
257 bis of the VAT Code). In this situation, the transferee is regarded as the successor of the transferor
regarding the adjustments of input VAT on capital goods which may be necessary in the future. As a
consequence, this condition of being the successor in case of future regularizations implies that the transferee
is a taxable person VAT identified in France.

The transferor and transferee must declare this transaction in their French VAT retum on line E2 “Autres
opérations non imposables” (“Other non-taxable operations”).

According to the second paragraph of Article 257 bis of the VAT Code, the transfers, for VAT purposes,

implied by the membership or withdrawal of a taxable person as a member of a VAT group in accordance
with Article 256 C of the VAT Code, also benefit from the provisions of this article

208 | France

--- Page 25 ---
2.2 Supplies free of charge

- Gifts of small value

The supply of goods as gifts for which VAT was deducted upon acquisition or manufacture does not give rise

to a taxable operation/self-supply when the following two conditions are simultaneously met:

+ the value of the goods offered must not exceed the amount set by Article 23 N of Annex IV to the VAT
Code per object and per year for the same beneficiary (in 2025, the amount is EUR 73 tax included, the
amount will be updated from January 2026);

+ the application of goods is done for business purposes. If this is not the case, the self-supply is taxable
regardless of the value of the good provided as a gift (for example, withdrawal for the private needs of a
manager).

When these conditions are met, the right of deduction initially exercised, remains.
- Samples

Samples or specimens taken for business purposes do not give rise to taxation for self-supplies, nor to the
challenge of the deduction of the tax charged on these goods, whatever their value.

The French administration provides a stricter definition of the goods concerned compared to the ECJ.

According to the French tax administration guidance, samples are small quantities of goods intended to give
an idea of the products available on the market, which are given free of charge to potential customers in a
packaging different from that of the products marketed, which makes them unfit for sale. Thus, in the field
of perfumery and cosmetics, a good constitutes a sample if its capacity is small (for example, between 2 ml
and 10 ml) and if it bears the notice “not for sale”. The provision of a good free of charge which does not
correspond to this definition triggers a self-supply, subject to the rules applicable to gifts (see above), and the
tax payable is not deductible.

‘The French administration applies the same rules to “specimen” products, i.e. goods identical to the goods
marketed, distributed in limited numbers to certain people who are able. because of their professional activity,
to make them known to the public. Provided that the word “specimen” is clearly and indelibly affixed to the
product, it is mentioned “not for sale” and that the number of similar specimens given free of charge by a
company to the same person is justified (with regard to specimen discs and cassettes, professionals can thus
distribute to the same radio station or television channel up to five copies of the same recording per year, and
a single copy for other categories of beneficiaries: music critics, specialized magazines, discotheques), the
distribution of specimens does not trigger a self-supply. Distributing companies must keep a list of recipients
of the specimen which mentions the commercial quality of the latter.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

It is possible to apply an exemption for the VAT on importation when the importation is followed by
immediate intra-Community supplies or transfers which can be qualified as deemed intra-Community
supplies. The customs regime “4200” should be used.

In order to apply the regime 4200, the taxable person must be VAT registered in France.

EU taxable persons and non-EU taxable persons exempt from tax representation can appoint a limited fiscal
agent.

France | 209

--- Page 26 ---
2.3.2. Postponed accounting on importation

This regime is compulsory.
As from 1 January 2022, the import VAT is deferred to the VAT return and is declared and paid to the tax
authorities instead of to Customs.

Tt applies to any business importing goods into France.
For foreign companies, obtaining a VAT number prior to carrying out imports is an obligation.

The postponed accounting takes the form of a deferment on the VAT return called “reverse charge on import”
in France. The VAT return is in principle prefilled with the elements declared to Customs on the import
documentation (VAT number of the importer for VAT purposes).

3. Place of supply

3A Consignment and call-off stock

A call-off stock transaction refers to the situation whereby a supplier sends goods to a stock in another EU
Member State for an already known intended acquirer, the transfer of ownership of these goods to that
acquirer being postponed to a further event in the destination state when the acquirer takes out the goods from
the call-off stock.

If certain conditions are met, the supplier does not perform a transfer of own goods followed by a domestic
supply, but an intra-Community supply to his customer.

This simplification must be used if all the conditions are fulfilled. The former VAT simplification arrangement
implemented by the French tax authorities, which was applicable as an option, is therefore no longer in force
since | January 2020.

The French VAT legislation is generally in line with the articles introduced in the EU VAT Directive.

3.2 _ Installation supplies

Certain construction and installation supplies are treated as supplied where they are constructed or installed.

Article 258 Ib of the VAT Code states:
“The place of supply of movable goods is located in France when the good is in France during assembly or
installation by the vendor or on his behalf”.

When the goods are assembled or installed in France before the delivery takes place, the operation qualifies as.
a supply of goods taxable in France and not as a supply of services. The reverse charge applies if the customer
is registered for VAT purposes in France (whether he is established or not) and the supplier is not established
in France, even if he is registered for VAT in France (see also paragraph 8.1.1).

3.3 Distance sales threshold

From 2021, the rules and thresholds for distance sales (both intra~Community distance sales and distance
sales of imported goods) have been harmonized across the EU.

‘The yearly distance selling threshold for sales to customers in other EU Member States without a VAT
registration number (e.g. private individuals or PBRD) is now BUR 10,000 for intra-Community distance
sales. Tumover generated ftom the supply of ‘electronically supplied services’, telecommunication
services and broadcasting services are included in the EUR 10,000 threshold. A threshold of EUR 150 per
consignment applies for the application of the special scheme for distance sales of imported goods

210 | France

--- Page 27 ---
3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

Under the French VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rules apply when the following conditions are
met:

(a) if France is EU Member State of arrival of the goods;
+ the middleman must neither be established in France nor registered for VAT purposes in France;

(b) if France is the Member State of middleman B (B is using its French VAT number);
+ the middleman must be established or VAT identified in France. He must not be established in the EU
Member State of arrival.

The French tax administration guidance has been amended further to the ECJ decision C-580/16, Firma Hans
Bihler mentioning that the simplification cannot be applied when B, established in the EU, is identified for
VAT in several EU Member States and uses for the acquisition in question the VAT identification number
issued by the EU Member State of dispatch or arrival of goods.

However, the paragraph in the French tax administration guidance which concerns France as the country of
arrival of the goods, still mentions that the middleman must not be established or identified in France and
must not have appointed a representative there in application of | of Article 289 A of the VAT Code. Article
258 D of the VAT code still mentions this condition and it has not been amended.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the intra-Community acquisitions of goods by non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons
engaged only in activities not giving rise to VAT deduction and flat-rate farmers is EUR 10,000 per year.
Below this threshotd, the intra-Community acquisitions are not subject to VAT,

However, the following transactions are subject to VAT in any case: intra-Community acquisitions of new
means of transport and intra-Commumity acquisitions of products subject to excise duties (tobacco, alcohol
and mineral oils).

All intra-Community acquisitions made by these persons must be taken into consideration for the calculation
of the threshold. The threshold can only apply when it has not been exceeded during the previous year.

3.6 __ Effective use and enjoyment

France has implemented the use and enjoyment rule of Article 59a of the EU VAT Directive under Article 259
C of the VAT Code but only in respect of certain type of B2C services. There are also certain B2B services
falling under the Use and Enjoyment rules. However, they have not been implemented in the VAT Code.

Article 259 C 1° of the VAT Code provides that for services other than those referred to in Articles 259
A (except those considered at 259 A-1°-c) and 259 D of the VAT Code, the place of supply of services is
deemed to be located in France when the services:

+ are supplied to non-taxable persons established, domiciled or habitually resident in an EU Member State;
+ are supplied by a supplier who has established his business or has a fixed establishment from which
the services are provided outside the EU or, in the absence of such place of establishment or fixed
establishment, is domiciled or habitually resident outside the EU; and
+ are effectively used and enjoyed in France.

The use and enjoyment rule of Article 259 C 1° of the VAT Code does not apply to the following services:

+ transport of goods, including intra-Community transports and accessories for these transport services;
+ transport of passengers:
+ expertise or work on movable property;
+ _ services provided by intermediaries acting in the name of and for the account of others;
+ rentals of means of transport, with the exception of long-term rentals of pleasure boats;
France | 211

--- Page 28 ---
+ services connected with immovable property;

+ cultural services, artistic, sporting, scientific, educational, entertainment or similar activities such as fairs
and exhibitions, including the services of the organizers of such activities, as well as services incidental to
these activities;

+ sales for consumption on the premises;

+ the single supplies of travel agencies:

+ services provided by electronic means;

+ telecommunications services; and

+ radio and television broadcasting services.

In addition, Article 259 C 2° of the VAT Code provides that rental of means of transport other than short-
term rental supplied to non-taxable persons established or domiciled or habitually resident outside the EU,
is taxable in France when the means of transport is effectively used in France, regardless of the place of
establishment of the supplier.

Article 259 C 2° of the VAT Code is not applicable to rental other than short term of pleasure boats if the boat is
actually made available to the customer in another EU Member State where the supplier has established his busi-
ness or has a permanent establishment from which the services are provided, even if the boat is used in France.

Finally, 3° of Article 259 C of the VAT Code provides thal the rental of movable property, other than means of
transport, to a non-taxable person established or having his domicile or habitual residence outside the EU, is
taxable in France if the rented movable property is actually used in France.

The criteria of use and enjoyment take into account the nature of each service referred to in Article 259 C of
the VAT Code.

In the French tax administration guidance, for example, the following B2C services are regarded as located in

France if effectively enjoyed and used in France:

+ advertisements when the location from which the advertising messages are transmitted to the target
audience is located in France, that is to say, in particular as part of a billboard advertising campaign, if the
advertising panels are located in France, or as part of a press campaign if the written media is distributed
primarily in France, or as part of an audiovisual campaign, if the media broadcasts to the French public;

+ long-term hiring out of means of transport for the distance covered in France; and

+ leased movable property when made available to the lessee in France.

The services supplied by a supplier established in France to non-taxable local public entities established
outside the EU should normally be taxable in France under the general B2C rule, However, the French tax
administration guidance mentions that these services must be considered as being located outside the EU.
since the use of the services is located on the territory of these entities.

Long-term hiring out of means of transport other than pleasure boats is taxable in France when:

+ the customer is established in France, no matter the place of establishment of the supplier and the place of
use of the service; or

+ the customer is established outside the EU and the service is used in France, no maiter the place of
establishment of the supplier.

The use and enjoyment rule of Article 59a(a) is also implemented for certain B2B services, not by the VAT

Code but by the French tax administration guidance. The following B2B services supplied to a taxable person

established in France are not subject to French VAT if effectively used or enjoyed outside the EU:

* — transport of goods and related commissions:

* conveying of means of transport for the purposes of transport of goods;

+ leasing of vehicles and materials for the purposes of transport of goods;

+ — storage, handling, loading and unloading of goods; and

+ repair, transformation, adaptation or processing services when such services concern goods which were
subject to VAT at the time of importation or re-importation by application of the paragraph | of Article
292 or of Article 293 of the VAT Law (in case of outward processing performed outside the EU).

212 | France

--- Page 29 ---
In practice, the following criteria may be observed

+ the distance covered outside the EU, as far as transport of goods and the conveying of means of transport
are concerned;

+ the distance covered outside the EU, as far as rental of vehicles and materials and commissions related to
transport are concerned; and

+ where the services are materially carried out, as far as guarding, warehousing, handling, loading and
unloading are concerned.

4. Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods, the chargeable event occurs when the power to dispose of the goods as an
‘owner is transferred. As soon as the transfer of the power to dispose of the goods as an owner takes place, in
other words, as soon as the tangible personal property enters into the power and possession of the purchaser,
VAT becomes due, regardless of when the transaction is paid. In general, the rules of private law must be
applied. The transfer of the power to dispose of property as an owner generally occurs when consent is
exchanged, even if the property has not been delivered or the price has not been paid.

However, the parties may delay the formation of the contract and thus the transfer of ownership by including

a condition precedent in the contract. In this case, the transfer of ownership only takes place when the

condition is fulfilled. This type of clause must be distinguished from a resolutory condition in which

the transfer of ownership occurs at the time of the conclusion of the contract, which can be terminated

retroactively if the condition is fulfilled. The tax consequences are as follows:

+ in the case of a sale under suspensive condition, the taxable and chargeable event occurs only when the
condition is fulfilled;

«+ in the case of a sale subject to a resolutory condition, the taxable and chargeable event occurs as soon as
the consents are exchanged.

In the case of intra~-Community transactions, VAT becomes due at the latest on the 15" of the month following
the one during which the chargeable event occurred, i.e. the supply took place. VAT is however due on

the date of issue of the invoice provided that this date precedes the chargeability rule supra (i.e. 15" of the
month following the one during which the chargeable event occurred) and that it is not an advanced payment
invoice.

In the case of a supply of a service which falls under the main rule of Article 44 of the EU VAT Directive,
VAT is due at the time the chargeable event occurs. The chargeable event occurs at the time the service is
rendered.

In the case of a supply of a domestic service which does not fall under the main rule of Article 44 of the EU
VAT Directive, VAT is due at the time of collection date, in practice, the collection date is the date on which
the payment for the services is made by the purchaser to the supplier.

For any type of services rendered, the tax is payable, as a rule, upon the receipt of advance payments, price,
remuneration.

42 Advance payments

There used to be a distinction between advance payments received for goods and for services.

In the case of advance payments received for the supply of goods, prior to 1 January 2023, there was no
chargeable nor taxable event, unless it was contractually agreed that a transfer of title occurred as and when
an advance payment invoice was issued or received. From | January 2023, VAT is due at the time of receipt

of these advance payments up to the amount received. This rule applies automatically to advance payments
collected from 1 January 2023 on all contracts in progress on this date.

France | 213

--- Page 30 ---
In the case of advance payments before services are supplied, VAT is due at the time of receipt of these
advance payments.

4.3 Continuous supplies

In the case of continuous supplics of goods giving rise to successive statements or payments, up to the value
covered by these statements, VAT is due on the last day of each period to which the statements or payments
relate, or at the time of the invoice if the supplier opts for the payment of VAT upon debits,

Services ongoing over a period of more than 1 year, that do not give rise to settlements or payments in

that period and for which the customer is liable to account for the VAT on the basis of Article 196 EU VAT
Directive, are regarded as being supplied on the expiry of each calendar year up to the time the provision of
the services ceases.

4.4 Cash accounting

As mentioned above, in the case of a supply of a domestic service which does not fall under the main rule of
Article 44 of the EU VAT Directive, VAT is due at the time of collection date, Suppliers can however choose
to opt for the payment of VAT upon debits, The debit option allows that persons who provide services for
which the tax is in principle due at the time of payment, may be authorized to pay the tax according to their
debils (thal is to say at the time of the registration of the corresponding amounts to the debit of the accounts
receivable). In practice, this corresponds to the invoice date, provided that the invoice date is not later than
the collection date. If the invoice date is later than the collection date, the collection date should be used. In
case advance-payments are received from the purchaser, the tax point remains the payment date, even if the
option upon debits has been exercised.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad Debt Relief
In France, bad debt relief is available to taxable persons, provided that they prove by any means that the
amount not yet paid by the customer is definitively irrecoverable.

In practice, a debt is regarded as definitively bad debt at the moment the taxable person, having used legal
actions, enters the debt in a final charge account.

The taxable person must send to the customer a copy of the invoice disclosing a specific wording: “facture
demeurée impayée pour la somme de EUR... (prix net) et pour fa somme de EUR ... (TVA correspondante),
qui ne peut faire l'objet d’une deduction (Article 272 du Code Général des Impéts)” (“Unpaid invoice for
the amount of EUR... (price net of VAT) and for the amount of EUR... (VAT amount), for which the VAT
deduction is not allowed (Article 272 of the VAT Code)”).

The VAT paid on these invoices must be deducted before 31 December of the second year following the year
during which the debt became irrecoverable.

The VAT is declared on line 21 of the French VAT return.

5.2 Cash Discounts

‘The financial rebates in case of early payment (conditional discount) stated on invoices may be treated
according to different procedures:

1. The supplier can add a specific item on the invoice explaining that the VAT corresponding to the price
actually paid can be deducted by the customer:

214 | France

--- Page 31 ---
In this case, the customer would be entitled to deduct only the VAT relating to the price paid. The supplier
will pay the VAT on the price paid by the customer after discount. The additional statement can be for
example: “En cas d’escompte pour paiement sous 14 jours, celui-ci sera déduit de notre chiffre d’ affaires
taxable. Le montant de la TVA déductible doit étre diminué de la TVA sur l’escompte » (If discount for
payment within 14 days, it will be deducted from our taxable turnover. The amount of deductible VAT must
be reduced with the VAT on the discount). This specific mention allows the supplier to pay the VAT only on
the price effectively paid without issuing a corrective document.

2. The supplier can add a specific item explaining that he renounces offsetting VAT relating to the
discount offered:

In this case, the supplier must add on his invoice that the discount is net of tax, “escompte net de taxe”.
‘The supplier will pay the VAT on the price mentioned on the invoice and the customer will be entitled to
deduct the VAT mentioned on the invoice.

3. No specific item added by the supplier:

In this case, the supplier must pay the VAT on the total amount of the invoice when the VAT is due. The
customer can deduct the VAT on the total amount mentioned on the invoice.

If the customer met the requirement in order to benefit from the financial rebate, the supplier must issue a
credit note for the amount of the rebate. Accordingly, the supplier will be entitled to recover from the State
the amount of VAT mentioned on the credit note and correspondingly, the customer will have to repay the
VAT mentioned on the credit note to the State.

The above rules are applicable to suppliers of goods and suppliers of services who opted to pay the VAT upon
debits (ie. on the basis of the invoice date in practice).

When the supplier of services pays the VAT according to the common local rule, i.e. when the payments
are received (tax point for domestic services in France, other than those referred to in Art. 44 of the VAT
Directive and for which the customer established in another EU Member State has to reverse-charge VAT
there), he should normally only pay the VAT on the payment received. He would then apply one of the
procedures described in points 1 or 3 above to regularize the invoice issued.

53 Open Market Value

France has not implemented the option for Member States to use the ‘open market value’ as the taxable
amount for specific transactions.

5.4 Returnable Packaging

With regard to returnable packaging, the French tax administration guidance allows businesses to not include
the consideration received for the supply of returnable packaging in the taxable amount of the total supply,
provided that the VAT relating to the amount of the returnable packaging has not been invoiced and that the
packaging delivered to customers is returned within the time limit customary in the profession in question
This period may vary according to the nature of the marketed products and the capacity of the packaging.
Once this period has elapsed, the returnable packaging must be included in the taxable amount.

When returnable packaging is not returned and cannot be considered as ancillary to the good, it is considered
as sold at the consignment price and taxed according to its own regime.

ids Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with the right of deduction

The following supplies and services are VAT exempted with the right of deduction:

+ insurance operations, reinsurance and brokerage transactions on insurance and reinsurance when they
relate to the insured or reinsured domiciled or established outside the EU or to exports of goods;

+ banking and financial operations mentioned in points A to E of Article 261 C of the VAT Code when
rendered to persons domiciled or established outside the EU or relating to exports of goods;

* — exports and intra-Community supplies of goods;

+ goods supplied to travellers resident outside the EU under certain conditions and services supplied by tax
refund operators using the public platform;

+ the supply of services consisting in work on movable property acquired or imported to be subject to work
and dispatched or transported outside the BU by the service provider or the customer established outside
France or for their account;

+ deliveries, processing, repair, maintenance, chartering and hiring of:

+ commercial sea vessels used for navigation on the high seas;
+ vessels used for industrial activity on the high seas; and
+ vessels used for professional sea fishing; sea boats for rescue or assistance.

+ — deliveries, hire, repair and maintenance of objects intended to be incorporated in these boats or used for
their operation at sea, as well as gear and nets for sea fishing:

+ deliveries, processing, repair, maintenance, chartering and hiring of the aircraft used by airlines whose
services to or from abroad (including communities and overseas departments sea, excluding metropolitan
France), represent at least 80% of the services they operate;

+ — deliveries, hire, repair and maintenance of the objects intended to be incorporated in these aircraft or used
for their flight operations;

+ the supply of goods for provisioning of vessels and aircraft designated above, as well as warships, as
defined in 89-01 subheading of the Common Customs Tariff, excluding vessels used for inshore fishing;

+ the supply of services to meet the direct needs of the vessels or aircraft designated above and their
cargoes;

+ air and sea passenger transport to and from foreign destinations or communities and overseas
departments;

+ rail passenger transport to and from foreign destinations, as well as transport of passengers by
international trains and on relations including certain routes (the list is determined by joint order of the
Minister for the Budget and the Minister of Transport);

+ road transport of foreign travellers coming to and from abroad, traveling in groups of at least 10 people;

* — transport between mainland France and Corsica for the part of the journey located outside the continental
territory:

+ transport of goods to and from Madeira and the Azores;

+ the supply of gold to the issuing institutions;

+ the supply of services relating to the importation of goods into France or another EU Member State whose

outside the European Community:

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

In order to prove the VAT exemption in case of intra-Community supplies, the following is required:

+ the supplier must obtain the EU VAT number of the customer. The supplier must check the validity of this
VAT number on the VIES website (Attp.//ec.europa.eu/taxationc_ ustoms/vies/);

+ the EU VAT number must be stated on the invoice;

+ the invoice must state the article referring to the VAT exemption of the intra-Community supply, i.e.
Article 262 ter [ of the VAT Code or Article 138 of the EU VAT Directive;

+ the supplier must file the recapitulative statement (European sales listing) referred to in Article 289 B-]
of the VAT Code; and the supplier must justify the reality of the dispatch or transport of goods outside
France. This can be done by any means:

* means of evidence may be direct or indirect;

+ all indications resulting from usual commercial documents that allow the supplier to prove for each
delivery, the existence of the dispatch or transport. The following documents may be provided as
proof: transport documents (CMR, AWB, sea bill of lading etc), carrier invoices, insurance for the
transport of the goods, contract with the customer, business correspondence, written order from the
customer, removal order, written confirmation from the customer about the receipt of the goods in
another EU Member State (tax entry certificate), duplicate of the supplier invoice stamped by the
customer, notice of settlement from a foreign bank, etc.

This list is not exhaustive and the overall value of justification submitted is assessed on a case-by-case basis.
Tn most cases, the French tax authorities will ask as main evidence for the transport, a document on which
the supplier is mentioned as sender and the signature of the customer for receipt of the goods in another EU

Member State is shown.

If the supplier estimates that it does not possess sufficient justi
existence of the dispatch or the transport, VAT must be charged.

12 documents enabling him to prove the

The above rules can still be used in order to prove the transport besides the presumptions of transport as
mentioned in Article 45a Implementing Regulation.

218 | France

--- Page 35 ---
7.1.2 Export supplies

In order to prove the VAT exemption in case of exports, the following is required:

+ the customs document issued by the customs authorities. The supplier, when established in the EU, must
appear on the export document as the exporter of record (box 2 “exportateur”) with his EORI number.
When established outside the EU, the supplier must appear in box 44 with his French VAT number (rule
in force in France since 01 October 2020); and

+ there must be a confirmation of exit of the European Union.

‘The export may also be proved by alternative means such as the authenticated customs declaration in the
country of import of the goods or the documents relating to the transport when this transport is outside the
EU. However, these other means of proof are generally not so easily accepted by the tax authorities.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

France has introduced VAT warehouses for importation, exportation and processing, upon authorization.
Besides the products listed in Annex V of the EU VAT Directive, all non-Community goods or Community
goods coming from outside the fiscal territory of the Community, if they are allowed into a warehouse by the
EU customs legislation, can be placed in a VAT warehouse.

By tolerance, a non-established trader is not required to register for VAT when placing (except further to
intracommunity acquisitions) or selling goods in a VAT warehouse, or to another fiscal warehouse, but must
register for VAT when releasing goods from a VAT warehouse (if responsible for the release of the goods).
For a non-EU established trader who would have the obligation to appoint a fiscal representative, it is
tolerated that the warehouse keeper (owner of the license) provides its own VAT number to the foreign seller
It is possible to make use of a limited fiscal representative (see also paragraph 1.5).

An EU established trader must have a French VAT number to make an intra~Community acquisition of goods
destined to enter the fiscal warehouse.

If the goods are placed in a VAT warehouse, importation is provisionally exempt for VAT purposes. The sale
within the VAT warehouse is exempt from VAT. The VAT applies on the release from the VAT warehouse
unless a further exemption applies (e.g. in case of an intra-Community supply, exportation). In addition,

the reverse charge may apply if a non-established taxable person reteases the goods and sells the goods to a
French taxable person.

The warehouse keeper must take responsibility for compliance (including recording movements of the goods,
keeping an overview of the storage and discharging documents). Special invoicing requirements apply.

‘The warehouse keeper, the transporter of the goods where goods are shipped out of the VAT warchouse and
his principal are jointly and severally liable for any VAT due, together with the supplier and the customer.
7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

Excise goods under suspension of excise duties can be stored in an excise warchouse. When a trader wishes
to receive goods under suspension of both VAT and excise duty, he must meet the conditions laid down in the
relevant legislation to benefit from both suspension arrangements.

7.13.3 Bonded warehouse

It is possible to store goods coming from outside the EU in a bonded warehouse before the importation
occurs.

This is subject to a prior authorization from the customs authorities which can be only granted to companies
established in the EU. The applicant must justify the economic need for using such a regime. The goods
subject to absolute prohibitions on importation under all types of customs regimes cannot be placed in a
bonded warehouse.

The sale of goods while still in bond is not subject to French VAT.
France | 219

--- Page 36 ---
In order for the supply of goods to be not subject to French VAT, the goods must be maintained under the
bonded regime under which they were originally placed. Taxable persons are required to indicate on their
invoices, issued in accordance with Article 289 of the VAT Code, the authorization number of the opening of
the customs regime as well as the name of the bonded warehouse license holder and of the warehouse keeper
when this is a distinct person.

When goods have not been subject to sale and they are still in bond, VAT is paid on the release either by the
recipient for supplies of goods or by the acquirer for intra-Community acquisitions.

For goods having been subject to a sale while in bond, VAT is paid on the release by the customer of the last
sale performed while the goods were bonded.

Certain services relating to bonded goods are also zero rated.

7.3 Option for taxation

731 Financial and banking
The option for taxation is possible for the following transactions:

+ financial and banking; and
«normally exempt transactions connected to banking, financial activities, and, generally speaking, to
securities and money business, such as these activities defined by decree.

Banking or financial transactions normally exempted from VAT can be subject to VAT by option, except when
they fall within the scope of one of the categories of transactions expressly excluded from the right of option
for taxation according to Article 260 B of the VAT Code.

Only certain operators can opt for the taxation, namely professionals of financial and banking business and
other persons usually carrying out transactions linked to banking and financial activities. The option takes the
form of a written declaration printed on the letterhead of the taxable person and takes effect as of the 1" day
of the month following the one during which the filing of the declaration to the local tax office of the place of
exercise of the profession has been made.

Each time a taxable person who has exercised the option, carries out a banking or financial transaction
falling within its scope, he can choose whether to subject it to tax or to exempt it. In practice, this option
may lead the taxpayer to decide, according to his own motivations, to apply the option not only transaction
by transaction, but also by category or type of transaction, or even, in a global approach, to apply it to all
banking or financial transactions falling within the scope of the option.

The option initially covers a period of 5 years, including the one during which the option is declared. It takes
effect from the first day of the month following the one during which the option is declared to the local tax
office.

The option may be terminated as from | January of the fifth year following the year in which it was exercised.
The termination takes effect from the first day of the month following the month in which itis filed with the
tax office.

7.3.2 Lease and sale of immovable property

Supplies of immovable property taxable upon option to tax are:

+ supplies of land which is not building land within the meaning of Article 257 12 1° of the VAT Code; and
+ supplies of buildings built for more than 5 years.

‘The option can generally take the form of a written declaration printed on the letterhead of the taxable person
sent to the relevant tax office, The property for which the option is made should be clearly identified in the
letter; some other conditions may exist depending on the immovable property concerned.

The option generally takes effect from the 1* day of the month following the one during which it has been
sent to the tax office.

France | 221

--- Page 38 ---
‘The duration of the option varies depending on the immovable property involved.
Regarding rentals of immovable property, the following supplies can be submitted to VAT upon option to tax are:

+ the landlord who rents unfurnished premises for the purposes of the business of a licensee who is a
taxable person, or if the lease is entered into on or after 31 December 1990, for the purposes of the
activity of a non-taxable person;

+ the landlord who rents property under a lease conferring a right in rem; and

+ the persons who rent land and buildings operating for agricultural use.

7.4 VAT free purchase regime

French VAT registered taxable persons performing intra-Community supplies and/or exports can purchase
or import goods free of VAT up to an amount corresponding to their intra~Community supplies and exports
of the previous year or the past 12 months, upon authorisation by the tax authorities. This quota is called
“contingent d’achats en franchise”. In order to benefit from this regime, the customer must provide its
supplier with a signed document certifying that the goods purchased are susceptible to be exported or
supplied to another EU Member State and making reference to the ‘achat en franchise’ license number
granted by the authorities.

When the quota is exceeded, the purchases made until the end of the year are subject to VAT unless an
extension of the quota is claimed. This VAT is recoverable under the general conditions.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

In France, there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B
services provided by foreign entities to taxable persons and legal entities established in France. This reverse
charge applies in all EU Member States and follows from Articles 44 and 196 EU VAT Directive.

8.
When taxable persons not established in France, invoice taxable persons registered for VAT purposes in
France for the domestic supply of goods or services, the French VAT due on the domestic transaction is
always shifted to the customer. The customer does not have to be established in France, but he must be VAT
registered in France in order for the reverse charge to apply. The customer self-assesses this VAT in its French
VAT return. The invoice issued does not show VAT.

1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)

The reverse charge mechanism is applicable when the supplier is not established in France. The reverse
charge even applies if the supplier is VAT registered in France.

On the invoice, reference should be made to the application of the reverse charge. The corresponding French
wording “Autoliquidation” is required on invoices subject to French invoicing rules. It is not compulsory
any more to refer to the VAT Code or to the EU VAT Directive provisions but for clarity purposes, a foreign
supplier could use the following text on the invoice: “Autoliquidation — Article 283-1 of CGI”.

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 Directive)

On the following supplies and/or services, the domestic reverse charge rules apply to:

+ operations of recycling goods and the new wastes of industry when the customer has a French VAT number ;

+ supply of investment gold (optional) ;

+ supply of CO, Emission Rights, certificates of guarantee of origin of biogas, electricity, renewable gas
and hydrogen, certificates of production of biogas and certificates of guarantee on capacities (on the latter,
change expected);

* electronic communication services which are out of scope of the tax mentioned in Article L.453-1 of
tax code on goods and services (CIBS) when the supplier is established in France and the customer is
established and VAT registered in France;

222 | France

--- Page 39 ---
+ supply of natural gas and electricity:
+ — from non-resident suppliers to taxable dealers established and VAT registered in France;
+ — from non-resident suppliers to French VAT registered acquirers using the goods for final consumption
in France;
+ from French established suppliers to French VAT registered customers, when used for purposes other
than consumption.

+ services connected to immovable property and consisting in construction work, including repair, cleaning,
maintenance, transformation and demolition services are subject to the reverse charge by the contractor,
VAT registered in France, who subcontracts such services within the meaning of Article | of Law No. 75-
1334 of 31 December 1975.

8.2 Joint and several liability

Under the French VAT rules, joint and several liability exists:

+ for businesses that form part of a VAT group. The representative of the VAT group bears the responsibility
regarding the amounts of VAT, late interests and penalties due resulting from reassessment procedures
followed in respect of the different members of the group. However, each member of the group remains
jointly liable for the payment of VAT, penalties and late interests (if required) due by the VAT group, up to
the amount that each member would owe if it was not part of the group;

+ for the French tax representative in case of payment failure (voluntary or not) of VAT, late interests by the
represented foreign company; and

+ for the acquirer of a good or service who knew or could not have ignored that he was participating in
fraud involving this good or service, due to the tax not paid upstream by a defaulting supplier (notably in
case of carousel fraud).

Joint and several liability also exists in other situations where, for example, there is a supply involving online
platform operators, an intra-Community acquisition when the acquirer is not established in France etc.

9. Recovery of input VAT

Asa general rule, input VAT must be deducted on a VAT return by 31 December of the second year following
the tax point. A special statement is compulsory for late deductions.

1 Non-recoverable input VAT

In tn general, the input VAT is not recoverable in case of:
goods or services used for more than 90% for non-business purposes;
+ accommodation expenditures for managers and employees;
+ vehicles for transportation of persons;
* — transport of persons;
+ deliveries of goods without payment or for a too low price (gifts not meeting the VAT deductibility
conditions, mentioned in paragraph 2.2. ete.);
+ services related to excluded goods (maintenance services on vehicles for the transport of passengers, etc.);
+ prohibited adverts; and
+ oil products except in the following cases:
* in case of oil products in a gaseous form and kerosene when used as fuel by the final customer:
+ 50% of the input VAT is recoverable if used for vehicles excluded from the right to deduction
(transport of passengers ete.); and
+ 100% of the input VAT is recoverable if used for vehicles not excluded from the right to deduction
(public transport of passengers ete.).
* incase of petrol products (gasoline), diesel oil and super ethanol E85, when used as fuel by the final
customer:
* 80% of the input VAT is recoverable if used for vehicles excluded from the right to deduction
(transport of passengers etc.); and
+ 100% of the input VAT is recoverable if used for vehicles not excluded from the right to deduction
(public transport of passengers etc.).

France | 223

--- Page 40 ---
9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT on a VAT return (VAT credit) can be carried over indefinitely or voluntarily claimed back
ona monthly, quarterly or annual basis, The refund can be requested on a monthly or quarterly basis if the
VAT credit amount is at least EUR 760. The refund can be requested on an annual basis if the VAT credit
amount is at least EUR 150.

9.3. Adjustment periods

The beginning of the adjustment period is the year during which the property has been acquired, imported,
completed, used for the first time or transferred between different business sectors.

+ immovable property - 20 years (19 years following the year of first use)
+ movable property - 5 years (4 years following the year of first use)

The adjustment applies on asset value.

94 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

EU taxable persons must file the refund request via the electronic portal of their EU Member State. The
requests must be filed before 30 September of the calendar year following the refund period.

The threshold for the refund is EUR 400 for a period of 3 months to a year and EUR 50 for a period of a
calendar year or the settlement of the calendar year.

An EU taxable person can file up to 4 quarterly refund claims (one for each quarter provided that the
minimum amount is met) and one yearly refund ctaim in case invoices were not included in the other refund
claims.

Copies of invoices or import documents for which the taxable amount equals or exceeds EUR 1,000 or EUR
250 for fuel costs must be submitted through the electronic portal together with the filing of the claim. If the size
limit is reached, the additional invoices can be, in principle, sent by email (st-tva.dinr@dgfip.finances.gouv.fr).

The tax authorities have 4 months to decide if they will refund the VAT or not (6 months in case of a first
request of additional information and 8 months in case of another request for additional information).

In case of a final decision of rejection, the time to appeal to the Administrative Court is 2 months from the
receipt of the decision extended by 2 additional months when the taxable person is established outside France.
In the meantime, the foreign taxable person can also make a claim to the tax conciliator at the following
address:

Le Conciliateur fiscal de la Direction des Impéts des Non-Résidents
10 rue du Centre

TSA 70025

93465 Noisy Le Grand Cedex

France

conciliateurfiscaldinr@dgfip,finances.gou,fr

There is no specific time limit to make an appeal to the tax conciliator.
The same VAT deduction limitations apply as for French taxable persons (see also paragraph 9.1).

appearing on these invoices or documents is equal to or greater than an amount of EUR 1,000, or EUR 250 if.
