10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

French invoicing rules apply to:

+ transactions carried out on the French territory or deemed so in application of Articles 258, 258B, 259,
259A, 259C and 259D of the VAT Code, excluding transactions carried out by a taxable person who has,
in another EU Member State, the seat of his economic activity or a fixed establishment from which the
transaction is performed, and for which the acquirer or recipient is liable for the VAT payment, except if the
taxable person gave the latter a mandate to issue invoices in his name and on his behalf (self-invoicing);

France | 225

--- Page 42 ---
* transactions for which the place of taxation is located in another EU Member State and which are carried
out by a taxable person who has, in France, the seat of its economic activity or a fixed establishment from
which the transaction is performed, and for which the acquirer or recipient is liable for the VAT payment
in the EU Member State in which the transaction is located, except if the taxable person gave the latter a
mandate to issue invoices in his name and on his behalf (self- invoicing); and

+ transactions whose place of taxation is located outside the BU and which are carried out by a taxable
person who has, in France, the seat of ils economic activity or a fixed establishment from which the
transaction is performed.

A VAT invoice must contain the following information:
+ date at which the invoice is issued;
+ invoice number (sequential and chronological);
+ VAT identification number under which the supply of goods or services is performed
+ VAT identification number of the supplier and VAI identification number of the customer in case of an
intracommunity supply;
+ VAT identification of the supplier and of the customer for supplies of goods or services subject to reverse
charge except article 194 of the EU Directive;
+ name and address of the supplier or service provider;
+ name and address of the customer;
+ billing address if it is different from the official addresses of the parties (e.g. ‘sold-to’ different than ‘bill-
to’);
+ purchase order number when it has been previously established by the buyer;
+ — operation’s date if it differs from the date of the invoice;
+ quantity and description of the goods or services;
* unit price without VAT;
+ reduction and discount directly related to the operation;
+ applicable VAT rate or exemption;
+ the total VAT amount, and for each rate, the total amount without VAT and the relating VAT;
+ reduction and discount not related to particular operations;
+ the reference to the relevant Article of the VAT Code or of the EU VAT Directive or to other information
indicating that the operation is exempted;
+ the indication for which the acquirer or recipient is liable for the payment of VAT (intra-Community or
domestic operations):
+ all the above-mentioned information, except as a simplification, for invoices for which the customer
established in another EU Member State must account for reverse charge VAT, the unit price without
VAT, the applicable VAT rate, the VAT amount and any reduction of price or discount; and
+ the mentioning of “Autoliquidation” (reverse charge). The administration does not require any longer the
mentioning of the provisions of the VAT Code or of EU VAT Directive justifying the application of such
regime but does neither prohibit it. It is recommended to include “Autoliquidation” with the reference to
the corresponding provisions to avoid any confusion regarding the nature of the transaction;
+ — the information necessary to determine whether there is a new means of transport; and
* areference to the fact that a margin scheme has been applied as well as a reference as to why the margin
scheme applied, e.g.:
+ margin scheme — second hand goods “Régime particulier — Biens d’ occasions”;
+ margin scheme — works of art “Régime particulier — Objets d’art”;
+ margin scheme — collector’s items and antiques “Régime particulier — Objets de collection et
d’antiquités”; and
+ tour operator margin scheme “Régime particulier - Agence de voyages”.

New invoicing requirements will enter into force from 1 September 2026:

+ SIREN number of the seller and the customer (if he has one);

+ delivery address if different from the billing address;

+ “VAT on debits” (if the supplier opted for the payment of VAT upon debits, i.e. in practice from the date
of issuance of the invoice). This mention will become mandatory while it is currently only recommended;
and

+ nature of the operation: delivery of goods and/or services.

226 | France

--- Page 43 ---
As a simplification, the following invoicing requirements are not compulsory on invoices with a net amount

of up to EUR 150 and corrective invoices modifying initial invoices when they referred to:

+ the VAT number of the taxable person under which he performed the supply of goods or services; and

+ incase of exemption, the corresponding reference to the provision of the VAT Code or of the EU VAT
Directive or any other reference that the operation benefits from a VAT exemption.

This simplification rule does not however apply to:

+ — distance selling;

+ intra-Community supplies of goods and means of transport; and

« — supplies of goods and services referred to in Article 289-0, I1-1° of the VAT Code (operations for which
the customer established in another EU Member State has to reverse charge VAT).

10.1.2 Time limits

In principle, the invoice must be issued each time a supply is made. However, the invoice can be issued
periodically on a monthly basis for several supplies in the same calendar month (when the operations are very
frequent).

Given the administrative management, it is allowed to issue the invoice a few days after the actual supply. For
exempt intra-Community supplies of goods referred to in Article 262 ter J of the VAT Code, exempt supplies
of new means of transport referred to in Article 298 sexies II of the VAT Code and supplies of services for
which VAT is due by the recipient in application of Article 196 of the EU VAT Directive, the invoice is issued
at the latest on the 15" day of the month following the one during which the chargeable event took place. The
invoice can be issued periodically for severat distinct supplies of goods or several distinct supplies of services
rendered to a single recipient for which VAT is due within the same calendar month. This invoice is then
issued at the latest by the end of this said month.

10.1.3 Curreney

‘The amounts on the invoice may be mentioned in another currency, provided that the amount of VAT to be
paid or regularized is stated in Euros.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate
When the elements used to determine the taxable amount are expressed in a currency other than Euro, the

exchange rate to be applied is the last exchange rate published by the European Central Bank, on the date on
which the VAT is due.

10.1.5 Self-billing

A prior invoicing mandate should be concluded between the supplier and his customer. However, this
invoicing mandate is not subject to a particular formalism, i.e. this mandate may be a tacit mandate. The
parties must be able to prove the existence of this invoicing mandate. As a consequence, it is recommended to
keep a proof in writing.

The factual circumstances sufficient to establish the existence of an implicit mandate may, for example, result
from the fact that the invoices in question expressly state that they were physically issued by the agent on
behalf and for the account of the supplier. The modalities of the invoicing mandate including its scope can be
freely defined by the supplier and his agent.

Any invoice issued in the name of and for the account of the supplier shall be subject to acceptance by the latter.
‘The parties are free to determine the modalities of acceptance. A procedure of tacit acceptance is allowed.

In order to avoid any ambiguity, it is advised to include on the invoice “invoice issued by A in the name
of and for the account of B”. When the material issuance of the invoices is assigned to the customer,
“Autofacturation” must be mentioned on the invoice.

When the invoicing mandate is concluded with a customer or an agent established in a country with which
a legal instrument relating to mutual assistance similar in scope to that laid down by Directive 2010/24/EU
of 16 March 2010 and Council Regulation Nr. 904/2010 of 7 October 2010 does not exist, some specific
conditions are required:

France | 227

--- Page 44 ---
+ the supplier must issue a prior and written mandate to his customer or to his agent;

+ the supplier must notify the existence of this invoicing mandate to the French Tax Authorities. This
notification is done in writing (either by letter or by email), with the reference to the name and address
of the customer or agent when this customer or agent issues on a regular basis the invoices in the name
of and for the account of the supplier. The criteria of “regular basis” are assessed in comparison to
the number of invoices or their periodicity. In practice, it is accepted that the notification is not filed
voluntarily by the supplier when the number of transactions invoiced does not exceed the threshold of 10
transactions per calendar year;

+ the supplier must be informed by his customer or agent of the place of establishment of the person
materially issuing the invoices and of any change in this place of establishment;

* invoices issued under this mandate must be issued as soon as the taxable event occurs; and

+ periodic invoices cannot be issued under this mandate.

10.1.6 Simplified invoices
A simplified invoice may be issued in several cases:

(a) Invoices for low amounts

The invoices containing a total net amount lower or equal to EUR 150 may omit the following items:
+ the French VAT registration of the supplier; and
+ the VAT mention referring to the VAT Code or the EU Directive article.

This simplification cannot be applied to the transactions for which:

+ the mention of the French VAT number is compulsory according to 2° of I of Article 242 nonies A of
Annex II to the VAT Code;

+ areference to the benefit of a VAT exemption measure is provided for by a provision of the VAT Code
other than 12 ° of I of Article 242 nonies A of Annex II to the VAT Code.

This simplification is not applicable to distance sales and (deemed) intra-Community supplies.

(b) Measures applicable to certain activities

+ motorway tolls
Billing arrangements for tolls paid on each pass, excluding those paid on subscriptions or through flow-
through cards operated by third parties, are permitted. The receipts issued at the toll gates are deemed
invoices if they mention all the information referred to in a) above and include a space reserved for the
information to be provided by the user. The customer identification should be mentioned by the customer.

+ restaurants
For invoices below or equal to EUR 150 excluding taxes, it is allowed that the customer identification
elements not be mentioned by the company issuing the invoice but that they be entered by the customer
himself in a reserved area. These invoices must mention the information referred to in a) above.

Under certain conditions, simplified invoices can also be issued in case of down payment invoices or in case
of amending invoices.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Information, documents, data, computer processing, information systems constituting the documented
and permanent controls put in place in order to establish a reliable audit trail between the invoice issued
or received and the supply of goods or services as well as documentation supporting their modalities

of performance, must be kept for 6 years. The administration with respect to electronic-, telecom- and
broadcasting services must be kept for 10 years.

The authenticity of the origin, the integrity of the content and the readability of the invoice must be guaranteed
from its issuance until the end of the storage period. Such obligations are also applicable for paper invoices.

228 | France

--- Page 45 ---
Paper invoices received or issued can be stored electronically or under paper form. When paper invoices are
stored in electronic form only, the conditions guaranteeing the identical reproduction of the invoice (image
and content) are the following: documented organization of the digital archiving process subject to in-house
business controls, availability, readability, integrity of the scanned invoices.

‘The integrity of the scanned invoices is ensured if below recommendations are notably complied with:
* Picture editing programs are forbidden;
» Incase the taxable person uses compression file, it should be operated without any loss;
+ Each scanned invoice must be stored under PDF or PDF A/3 (ISO 19005-3) format and be associated to:
- either a server seal based on a certificate compliant at least with the general referential of security
“RGS” at the level one star;
- ora digital impression:
-  oran electronic signature based on a certificate compliant at least with the general referential of
security “RGS” at the level one star;
- or any other equivalent secured method based on a certificate delivered by a certification authority
mentioned on a French Trusted List (Trust-service status List - TSL).
* Each file is date-stamped according to an in-house date stamping source in order to date the different
operations performed.

Electronic invoices must be stored in their original electronic format as the change of format does not
guarantee the integrity of the content of the invoices.

However, for management purposes, the taxable person is free to change the electronic format of the invoices
ifhe parallelly stores them in their original format (¢.g. a taxable person who receives invoices under XML.
format must store them in this format but can convert them also into PDF format for internal management
purposes).

Paper output invoices can be stored under one of the following options:

+ printing two paper invoices: one is sent to the client, the other is stored by the supplier; the invoice is
stored as paper version by the supplier;

* _ printing two paper invoices: one is sent to the client, the other is digitized by the supplier according to the
process depicted above to ensure the integrity of the scanned invoices; or

+ securing the files of invoices stored under PDF or PDF A/3 according to the process depicted above to
guarantee the identical reproduction of the invoice. The resulting secured pdf file is printed once for
transmission on paper to the client, the original pdf version is stored by the supplier.

Invoices issued by the operator or, in his name and on his behalf by his customers or by a third party, and all
invoices he receives, must be stored on the French territory when the storage is not conducted by electronic
means guaranteeing immediate, complete and online access to relevant information.

Scanned paper invoices and electronic invoices can be stored outside the French territory provided that:

+ The invoices are stored in the EU, each Member State being bound by Council Directive No. 2010/24/
BU of 16 March 2010 concerning mutual assistance for the recovery of claims relating to taxes, duties
and other measures and by Council Regulation (EU) No 904/2010 of 7 October 2010 on administrative
cooperation and combating fraud in the field of VAT. In such case, the accessibility of the invoices by
the French tax administration must be guaranteed from the seat or principal establishment of the audited
taxable person.

+ Ifthe invoices are stored outside the EU, the taxable person must ensure that either the non-EU country
concluded with France a mutual assistance convention equivalent to Council Directive No. 2010/24/
EU of 16 March 2010 and to Council Regulation (EU) No 904/2010 of 7 October 2010, or the non-EU
country is bound with France by a convention foreseeing a right of immediate access, download and use
of all data by electronic means.

France | 229

--- Page 46 ---
+ The taxable person must inform the competent local tax office of the place of storage outside France
(names and addresses of the persons in charge of the storage and periods of time involved) and of any
change in the place of storage.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

10.1.8.1 General rules

An electronic invoice is an invoice or a flow of invoices created, sent, received and stored in any electronic
format. The entire invoicing process must be electronic,

The recipient of the invoice will be considered as having received an electronic invoice and is only required
to store it in a dematerialized format.

Electronic invoices can be issued under 3 technical means:

+ asa structured message using agreed standards among the parties that ought to be readable by computer
and capable of being automatically and unambiguously processed (electronic data interchange (EDI)
procedure);

+ with an advanced electronic signature;

+ in any electronic format provided that documented and permanent controls are put in place in order to
establish a reliable audit trail between the invoice issued or received and the supply of goods or services.

The authenticity of the origin, integrity of the content and readability of an invoice sent by electronic means
must be ensured by the issuer and the recipient, from its issuance until the end of its storage period, at the
choice of the taxable person by using one of the above mentioned methods.

The recipient of an invoice is not bound by the securing method chosen by the issuer. The same taxable
person can use different securing methods depending on whether he acts as an issuer or a recipient. The
same taxable person can use concomitantly different methods (e.g. for different invoices or flows of invoices
directed to different customers),

The recipient must accept beforehand the use of the electronic transmission procedure. This acceptance
can be formally materialized or be tacit, for instance, when the acquirer or the recipient settles the invoice
received or in case a reasonable delay is granted to the recipient to require a paper invoice.

The issuer is free to choose the technical mode of transmission of electronic invoices (by email, as attachment
to an email, via a secured network or use of the EDI system, directly in the IT services of trading partners or
via platforms of services owned by third parties).

The choice of the file format is left free to each taxable person, subject to the fulfilment of the conditions of
the definition of the electronic invoice.

10.1.8.2 Mandatory e-invoicing

Electronic invoicing was supposed to become compulsory from 1 July 2024 for domestic B2B transactions

between VAT taxable entities established in France, but its implementation bas been postponed according to

the following timetable:

+ 1 September 2026: obligation for all companies to receive electronic invoices and obligation for large and
mid-cap companies (ETI in French) to issue electronic invoices;

+ 1 September 2027: obligation for small and medium-sized companies (PME in French) to issue electronic
invoices.

Contrary to what was expecied, the issue, transmission and receipt of electronic invoices will be carried out

only using a registered private platform (PDP), Invoicing data issued by taxable entities using a registered
private platform will be forwarded by the registered private platform directly to the tax authority.

230 | France

--- Page 47 ---
10.2 Compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

Under the standard regime, a taxable person must file monthly VAT returns. When the annual amount of VAT
due is less than EUR 4,000, the taxable person can file quarterly VAT returns.

Under the simplified regime and under specific conditions, a taxable person can file annual VAT returns,

10.2.2. Filing of VAT return and payment
Taxable persons, whether established in France or not, must file electronic VAT returns.

French taxable persons, non-BU taxable persons subject to tax representation must, in principle, file their VAT
returns according to a date provided by the authorities, mostly between the 21* and 24" day afer the end of
the reporting period

‘The taxable persons established in other EU Member States and non-EU taxable persons exempt from tax
representation must file the VAT return at the latest on the 19" day after the end of the reporting period, or on
the 24" day after the end of the reporting period when they carry out imports subject to VAT deferment, called
“reverse-charge on import” in France.

Anil return must be filed when no taxable operations have been made.

If the due date falls on a public holiday, Saturday or Sunday, the due date is postponed to the next business
day.

The e-payment of VAT by direct debit is mandatory for all taxable persons, including foreign taxable
persons holding a bank account in a bank established in the countries and territories which are part of the
jurisdictional scope of the SEPA schemes, and which opted for the SEPA B2B standard.

E-payment by direct debit must be ordered by the VAT return filing due date. The actual debit by the French
tax administration always takes place after this due date,

Taxable persons who do not meet the conditions of e-payment of VAT by direct debit can continue paying
VAT to the French tax administration by bank transfer. In such case, the funds must be credited on the bank
account of the French tax administration on the VAT return filing due date at the latest.

Transactions that do not require the filing of VAT returns in France:

French VAT registered taxable persons are not required to file VAT returns when they perform solely VAT
exempt intra-Community acquisitions referred to in Article 262 ter-II-3° of the VAT Code and/or VAT exempt
deemed intra-Community acquisitions in France when followed by supplies of goods or services subject to
reverse-charge by the recipient.

There is also a current practice from the non-resident tax office consisting in considering that the sole
performance of deemed intra-Community supplies from France to another EU country requires a VAT
registration but does not imply the obligation to submit VAT returns.

In above cases, French VAT registered taxable persons are only liable for the filing of Intrastat returns (if a
letter is received from the Customs authorities notifying them of their liability to file Intrastat returns— see
paragraph 10.4) and EC sales listing with the French customs authority.

10.2.3. European sales listing

‘The European sales listing named “état récapitulatif TVA” in French must be filed online on the customs?
website, on a monthly basis, only when intra-Community supplies from France ot simplified EU triangulation
are performed. If no transaction is performed, there is no need to file any nil EC sales listing.

France | 231

--- Page 48 ---
The following data must be filed in the EC sales listing:

+ the VAT identification number of the customer;

+ for each customer, the total amount (without VAT) of the supply in EUR;
+ — the amount of commercial regularizations, if any;

+ the regime code.

‘The due date for filing the retum is the 10" working day of the month following the end of the reference
period (including Saturdays).

A penalty of EUR 750 per retum can be imposed in case the EC sales listing has not been filed within the
deadline. This can amount to EUR 1,500 per month in the case of absence of filing following 30 days after the
receipt of a formal notice.

Each omission or inaccuracy in the declaration produced results in the application of a fine of EUR 15, up to a
maximal total of EUR 1,500.

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement
There is none.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

‘The tax assessment period in France is 3 years, e.g, operations conducted in 2025 can be assessed until 31
December 2028, It is extended until the origin of the VAT credit.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

Backdated VAT registrations are allowed in France in certain cases and upon acceptance by the authorities.
If VAT was due to the French State on a past period, a 5% and/or 10% penalty can be applied on the VAT
amount as well as interest for late payment.

In case of “bad faith” or occult activities, penalties may be higher.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return on time

The following penalties apply on the VAT amount:

* 10% if there is no notice of assessment or if the taxpayer submits the VAT return within 30 days from the
receipt of the first notice;

+ 40% if the taxpayer fails to submit the return within 30 days afier the receipt of the first notice; or

* 80% if the taxpayer fails to submit the return within the receipt of the second notice.

In case of zero-rated transactions, the French authorities can apply a penalty for failure to file VAT returns.
When the VAT returns which should have been filed do not include any element for the calculation of the VAT
due, i.e. when the operations which should have been reported are VAT exempted or lead to a VAT credit, the
applicable penalty is EUR 150 per lacking VAT return,

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

If the VAT is not paid on time (even if the return is filed on time), the penalty is 5% of the VAT amount due
and possibly 0.2% per month (interest for late payment). The payment is only recognized when the sums are
credited on the bank account of the tax office.

A5% penalty (based on the deductible VAT) for failure to reverse charge VAT in the corresponding VAT

return can be imposed. The French administration does not, however, apply this penalty in case of voluntary

and spontaneous regularization occurring before any tax notice. This regularization can be performed under

two practices:

+ the reporting is done in a corrective VAT return concerning the period during which the reverse-charged
transaction should have been declared; or

232 | France

--- Page 49 ---
+ the reporting is done in an ulterior VAT return subject to a clear mention of the elements related to the
reverse charged transaction.

Specific guidance applies for the regularization of certain types of transactions (e.g. importations).

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly
The French administration can apply the penalties of 5% and/or 10% of the VAT amount, and possibly late

payment interests, even in case of voluntary and spontaneous regularization occurring before any tax notice.
Mitigation can be negotiated on a case-by-case basis.

Tn other cases (e.g. in case of audit by the authorities, intention or negligence), the above-mentioned penalties
are applicable (see paragraphs 10.3.2 and 10.3.3).

10.4 Intrastat

This single report has been de-merged and the Intrastat called in French “Enquéte Mensuelle statistique sur
Jes Echanges de Biens Intra-Union européenne” (so-called EMEBD), i.e. statistical survey on intra-EU trade
in goods, must be filed online only in the event the company has received a notice letter from the French
customs authorities informing the company of the requirement to reply to the survey.

The letter informs the company about the flows of goods concemed: arrival, dispatch or both, along with the
period of time covered by this obligation.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

‘There is no official threshold to reach to become liable for the Intrastat/EMEBI returns filing, The liability
starts at receipt of the letter from the customs authorities (see section 10.4)

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

The information which needs to be reported in the Intrastat return are:

+ the nature of the movement of the goods (ie. arrival or dispatch);

+ the reference period (i.e. the reporting month);

+ the partner VAT ID number;

+ the commodity code of the goods;

+ the invoice value of the goods or the statistical value;

+ the net weight in kilograms of the goods without all their packaging:

+ the supplementary units (units of measurement other than kilograms, depending on the commodity code);

+ the country of destination (for dispatches) / country of consignment (for arrivals);

+ the country of origin within the meaning of customs regulations on origin;

+ the regime code (e.g. intra~Community acquisition, other arrival following processing etc.):

* the transaction nature code (e.g. purchase, sale, return of goods, repair etc.);

+ the means of transport, for instance “by road” or “by sea”; and

+ the French department (two-digit numerical code) of initial shipment of the goods for dispatches or actual
destination for arrivals.

Distance sales of goods dispatched from France to another EU Member State must be included in the EMEBI
return for dispatches.

Distance sates of goods dispatched from another EU Member State to France are not to be included in the
EMEBI return for arrivals.

A return is filled in for each type of flow (arrival or dispatch).

The due date for filing the return is the 10" working day of the month following the end of the reference
period (including Saturdays).

A penalty from EUR 75 to EUR 150 per return can be imposed in case the EMEBI has not been filed within
the deadline,

France | 233

--- Page 50 ---
A penalty of EUR 2,250 can also be applied in case of repeated refusals to comply with the deadlines or
to transmit the information and documents requested or no presentation to a summons of the customs
authorities.

An omission or inaccuracy in the declaration may result in a finding of non-filing and the application of above
fines.

10.4.3. “Déclaration Européenne de Services” (DES)

Taxable persons established in France must, trom the first euro, declare the supplies of services subject to the
general principle of Article 44 of the EU VAT Directive when the customer is a taxable person established in
the EU who will reverse charge the VAT on the supply in his country of establishment (Article 196 of the EU
VAT Directive),

The due date for filing the return is the 10" working day of the month following the end of the reference
period (including Saturdays).

‘The following data must be filed in the DES:
+ the VAT identification number of the customer;

+ for each customer, the total amount (without VAT) of the supply in EUR; and
+ the amount of commercial regularizations, if any.

‘The penalties are the same as the penalties applied for the EC Sales listing (see paragraph 10.2.3).
10.5 Additional compliance requirements

10.5.1 Real time reporting of taxable operations

E-reporting will be compulsory for mostly non-domestic B2B transactions (known as B2B International)
and B2C transactions, along with the e-invoicing reform (see paragraph 10. 1.8.2 above) according to the
following timetable:

+ 1 September 2026: for large and mid-cap companies (ETI in French);

+ 1 September 2027: for small and medium-sized companies (PME in French).

E-reporting will cover the transmission of transaction data to the tax authority, along with transmission of
transaction payment data, using a registered private platform (PDP).

Germany

‘The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented in Germany through the “Umsatzsteuergesetz
2005”, VAT is officially referred to as “Umsatzsteuer (USt)” or in common language as “Mehrwertsteuer
(MwSt)”. Besides that, the German Federal Ministry of Finance published the Administrative VAT Guidelines
referred to as “Umsatzsteuer-Anwendungserlass (UStAE)” which provide the official opinion of the German
tax administration and is updated regularly.

1. Taxable persons

A taxable person (Unternehmer) is defined in sec. 2 of the VAT Act as anyone who carries out a commercial
or professional activity independently, regardless of whether they have legal capacity under other regulations.
In other words, a taxable person is any independently operating person or entity that performs supplies for
remuneration on a continuous basis (or has the intention, supported by objective evidence, to carry out a
taxable activity for remuneration and independently and makes initial capital expenditure for this purpose).
Neither the legal form nor the legal capacity of the supplier is relevant.

Ll Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

In principle, all taxable persons have to register for VAT. Small businesses, however, enjoy a VAT exemption
according to sec. 19 of the VAT Act and do not have to register. This concession applies to all taxable persons
irrespective of their legal form. As from 2025, the exemption applies to small businesses established in
Germany if the annual turnover realized in Germany did not exceed EUR 25,000 (without VAT) during

the previous calendar year and as long as the tumover realized in Germany does not exceed EUR 100,000
(without VAT) in the current calendar year. Certain VAT-exempt supplies (mainly exempt supplies without
the right to deduct input VAT) do not have to be considered when determining whether the thresholds were
exceeded.

Where the relevant supplies are below the threshold, the taxable person is not obliged to charge VAT. On the
other hand, it cannot deduct any VAT incurred on the acquisition of goods or services. However, under certain
conditions the smal] business may be liable for VAT under the reverse charge scheme stipulated in sec. 13b of
the VAT Act (without input VAT deduction), This would result in an obligation to register for VAT and to file
VAT returns.

1.1.1 Voluntary registration

It is possible to register for VAT voluntarily before the relevant threshold is exceeded, even retroactively.
Small businesses may irrevocably declare to the tax office by the last day of February of the second calendar
year following the refevant tax period that he waives the application of the exemption. The waiver shall take
effect from the beginning of the tax period to which it is to apply. The waiver shall bind the taxable person for
at least five calendar years, The taxable person may revoke the waiver with effect from the beginning of the
following calendar year.

1.1.2 Provision of intra~-Community B2B goods and services

Where a domestic taxable person provides intra-Community supplies of goods and / or services to other
taxable persons which have to be declared in the VAT Information Exchange System (VIES), it needs to
apply for a VAT-ID no. (with the country prefix DE) separately with the Federal Central Tax Office. This

is necessary because taxable persons only get a national tax number for VAT purposes (with a different
structure and without the country prefix DE) and require an additional EU-VAT-ID no. for intra-Community
transactions.

From 2025, small businesses established in Germany who do not exceed the thresholds mentioned above have
the choice to apply the VAT exemption for small businesses for supplies in other EU Member States, too. The
taxable person will then have to participate in a specific reporting procedure as stipulated in sec. 19a of the
VAT Act. For this purpose, the taxable person will get a specific identification number for small businesses
with the annex “EX” from the Federal Central Tax Office under which the supplies in other EU Member
States have to be reported quarterly within one month after the end of each quarter of the year. Furthermore,

Germany | 235

--- Page 52 ---
the exemption only applies if the turnover according to Art. 288 of Directive 2006/112/EU (for all supplies
throughout the EU) did not exceed EUR 100,000 (without VAT) during the previous calendar year and as long
as this tumover does not exceed EUR 100,000 (without VAT) in the current calendar year. And last but not
least, the conditions for the VAT exemption for small businesses of the relevant other Member State must be
met as well.

1.2 _ Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

‘As from 2025, small businesses established in another EU Member State (irrespective of their legal form)
enjoy the same VAT exemption as domestic taxable persons according to sec. 19 of the VAT Act and do not
have to register. The exemption applies if the turnover according to Art. 288 of Directive 2006/112/EU (for
all supplies throughout the EU) did not exceed EUR 100,000 (without VAT) during the previous calendar
year and as long as this tumover does not exceed EUR 100,000 (without VAT) in the current calendar year.
Furthermore, a specific identification number for small businesses must be assigned to the taxable person in
the Member State where it is established.

If the VAT exemption for small businesses applies, the taxable person is not obliged to charge VAT. On the
other hand, it cannot deduct any VAT incurred on the acquisition of goods or services, not even in the VAT
refund procedure according to the Directive 2008/9/EC. However, the small business may be liable for VAT
under the reverse charge scheme stipulated in sec. 13b of the VAT Act (without input VAT deduction). This
would result in an obligation to register for VAT and to file VAT returns.

1.2.1 Voluntary registration

Itis possible to register for VAT voluntarily before the relevant threshold is exceeded, even retroactively. A
small business may irrevocably declare to the competent tax office in his Member State of establishment that
he waives the application of the exemption. The waiver shall take effect from the beginning of the quarter of
the year following receipt of the waiver. If the waiver is received by the authority during the last month of a
quarter of the year, it shall take effect as from the beginning of the second month of the following quarter. The
waiver shall bind the taxable person for at least five calendar years, The taxable person may revoke the waiver
with effect from the beginning of the following calendar year.

1.2.2 Registration procedure

When registering, the following information must be submitted:

+ ategistration form;

+ the articles of association;

= anexcerpt from the commercial register; and

+ acertificate of being a taxable person (i.e. VAT registration certificate from the country of establishment).

‘The registration form can be downloaded at the following link: https:/Avww,formulare-bfinv.de/ffw/content.do
Itnormally takes 2 to 4 weeks to register for VAT purposes.

‘The proper tax office is dependent on the location of the foreign business. For taxable persons established
in Germany, usually the tax office of the district in which the taxable person carries out its business is
competent. For foreign taxable persons, German VAT laws stipulate respective rules of competence,
depending on the country where the foreign taxable person is established. in principle, the Tax Office Berlin
International is responsible for foreign taxable persons. However, for taxable persons established in the EU

13 VAT grouping

When 2 or more taxable persons established or having a fixed establishment in Germany are closely bound
to one another by financial, economic and organisational links, those persons are treated as a single taxable
person for VAT purposes. The treatment as a VAT group is obligatory in Germany if the conditions are met
(sec, 2 para, 2 no. 2 of the VAT Act).

Financial links exist if more than 50% of the shares of the members are directly or indirectly held by the
same person(s). Organizational links exist if the members are under common management or where the
management of one member is actually subordinate to that of another. Economic links exist where the
activities of the members are mainly aimed at achieving the same economic goal or carried out for the benefit
of the other members of the group.

Only taxable persons can form part of'a VAT group, which excludes “pure” holding companies. VAT grouping
is limited to the German entities / fixed establishments of the group.

The VAT group must file one VAT return only, ie. a single VAT return, in the name of the head of the group.
If the head of the group is not located in Germany, another taxable person must be selected as the head of the
VAT group. The members of the VAT group are jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT debts of
the group. Nevertheless, each member of the group may apply for a separate VAT identification number.

Germany | 237

--- Page 54 ---
14 Fiscal representative

A foreign taxable person from an EU or a non-EU country does not have to appoint a fiscal representative for
registering for VAT purposes in Germany. It is possible to register directly for VAT purposes in Germany.

A foreign taxable person may appoint a fiscal representative according to sec. 22a of the VAT Act if it
provides only VAT exempt supplies and is not incurring any input VAT in Germany (i.e. does not have any
costs with German VAT). This avoids a VAT registration and filing of VAT returns since the exempt supplies
are reported under a specific VAT-ID of the fiscal representative.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

Germany has implemented an out of the scope of VAT rule for the sale of a business or a separated part of the
business in sec. 1 para 1a of the VAT Act (transfer of a going concern). It is essential that all important parts
of the business are transferred and that the transferee continues the business. The transferee must be a taxable
person. The transferee steps into the tax position of the transferor (i.c. takes over all transferor’s rights and
obligations related to VAT).

2.2 Supplies free of charge

The following supplies of goods free of charge shall be regarded as a supply for consideration and be subject

to VAT (sec. 3 para, 1b of the VAT Act):

+ the removal of an asset by a taxable person from his business for purposes other than those of its business;

+ the contribution of goods without consideration by a taxable person to its employees for their private use,
unless it is a gift for a special (personal) occasion of small value (up until EUR 60 net per gift);

+ any other contribution of goods without consideration, with the exception of gifts of small value (up until
EUR 50 net per year per recipient) and samples of goods made for the purpose of the business.

If it is already known at the moment of the acquisition of goods that these goods are to be used for rendering
supplies free of charge, the taxable person acquiring the goods is not entitled for an input VAT deduction. On
the other hand, in such a situation, the supply free of charge is not subject to VAT since the input VAT was not
deducted.

The following shall be regarded as a supply of service for consideration (sec. 3 para. 9a of the VAT Act):

+ the use of goods, for which input VAT was deducted, in full or partially, by a taxable person for purposes
other than those of its business or for the private use of its employees, unless it is regarded as a gift for a
special (personal) occasion of an employee of small value (up until EUR 60 net per gift);

+ the supply of a service without consideration by a taxable person for purposes not connected with the
business or for the private use of its employees, unless it is regarded as a gift for a special (personal)
occasion of an employee of small value (up until EUR 60 net per gift).

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

The importation of goods which will be subject to an immediate subsequent intra-Community supply is zero-
rated according to sec. 5 para. 1 no. 3 of the VAT Act (customs procedure 4200) if the importer:

* provides its German VAT-ID (or the VAT-ID of its fiscal representative);

* provides the VAT-ID of the acquirer in the other EU Member State; and

* provides proof that the imported goods are intended to be shipped to another EU Member State.

23.2 Postponed accounting on importation

Import VAT generally must be paid immediately, upon the importation for any goods from places outside
of the EU. Germany does not provide for a postponed accounting scheme, meaning that the import VAT is
declared in the VAT return. However, taxable persons enjoy an extension and have to pay the import VAT by

238 | Germany

--- Page 55 ---
the 10th day after import. Furthermore, in the event that the taxable person was granted a deferment account
in accordance with Art. 110 (b) or (c) of the Union Customs Code, the import VAT incurred upon importation
into Germany is only due on the 26th day of the second month following the month of import (sec. 21

para. 3a of the VAT Act). In principle, the taxable person must also provide security for the amount of import
duties and import VAT that are to be deferred. However, the security is not to be provided if the taxable
person is fully entitled to deduct input VAT.

3. Place of supply

31 Consignment and call-off stock

A foreign taxable person moving stock to Germany must, in principle, register for VAT purposes in Germany.
It will then have to report intra-Community acquisitions because of the transfer of own goods when
replenishing the stock and domestic supplies in Germany when the goods are pulled from the stock.

According to the jurisprudence of the Federal Fiscal Court with respect to consignment stocks, however, a
foreign taxable person will not have to register in Germany but rather provide direct supplies to Germany (at
the moment the goods are transferred to the stock) if a binding purchase contract is in place at the beginning
of the transport to the stock.

Furthermore, if certain conditions are met, the simplification scheme according to Art. 17a of the EU VAT
Directive which was transposed to sec. 6b of the VAT Act applies. The German VAT legislation is in line
with the relevant articles of the EU VAT Directive and stipulates that the supplier does not perform a transfer
of own goods followed by a domestic supply but rather an intra-Community supply to his customer at the
moment the goods are pulled from the stock,

3.2 Installation supplies

Certain supplies including installation (Werklieferung or Montagelieferung) are treated as supplied where
they are installed (sec. 3 para. 7 of the VAT Act). In Germany, a supply including installation is considered a
Werklieferung if the supplier uses his own good(s) for the supply which are not of minor importance for the
supply and the customer provides certain goods which become part of the final construction (sec. 3 para. 4 of
the VAT Act). A supply including installation is considered a Montagelieferung if the supplier only uses his
own good(s) for the supply. This distinction is relevant for the application of the reverse charge scheme (see
paragraphs 8.1.1).

If the goods are of minor importance, the supply is qualified as a service. Whether the goods used for the
Werklieferung or Montagelieferung are of minor importance or not does not depend on the value of the goods
Rather, what is relevant is if the goods form a dominant part of the supply. Only for the repair of movable
tangible goods where it is hard to determine the importance of the goods supplied, the Administrative

VAT guidelines allow a determination based on the value of the goods. If the goods used for the repair
amount to more than 50% of the total value of the supply, the repair is considered to be a supply of goods
(Werklieferung).

3.3 Distance sales

In principle, the place of supply of goods is where the transport commences (sec. 3 para. 6 of the VAT Act)
However, the place of supply shifts to where the transport ends for intra~Community distance sales once the
supplier exceeds the uniform EU threshold of EUR 10,000 (sec. 3c of the VAT Act). Turnover generated from
the supply of ‘electronically supplied services’, telecommunication services and broadcasting services are
included in the EUR 10,000 threshold.

Once the threshold is exceeded and distance sales to German customers are deemed to be carried out in

Germany, the supplier may either register directly in Germany or in its country of establishment via the One-
Stop-Shop to account for German VAT for its distance sales to Germany.

Germany | 239

--- Page 56 ---
It is possible to opt for taxation in the country of arrival (i.e. Germany) before the threshold is exceeded. It is
necessary to inform the German tax authorities. The option is binding for 2 years. If Germany is the country
of dispatch of the goods, it is necessary to inform the German tax authorities in writing if a taxable person
opts for taxation in the country of arrival before the threshold is exceeded.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

Under German VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rules have been implemented in line with the EU
VAT Directive in sec. 25b of the VAT Act.

Taxable persons A, B and C in the ABC transaction must be registered for VAT in different EU countries (i.e.
in countries 1, 2 and 3), The transport must be organized by either A or B. The goods must be transported
directly from A in country 1 to C in country 3.

The simplified triangulation rules can be applied if (among other conditions):

+ Germany is the country of arrival of the goods; and

+ the middleman (B) is not resident in Germany; and

+ the middleman (B) issues an invoice without VAT but referring to a simplified triangulation and the VAT
liability of the last acquirer (C).

Where Germany is the EU Member State of destination of the goods, the wording of the German legislation
does not preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the middleman (B) is VAT registered in Germany
or in the country of dispatch. It does preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the middleman (B) is
established in Germany.

In principle, the simplification is only applicable to transactions where 3 parties are involved (ABC
transaction). However, according to the Administrative VAT guidelines, the simplification is also applicable to
transactions where more than 3 parties are involved but only to the last 3 parties in the supply chain (the intra-
Community supply takes place between B and C).

35 Acquisition threshold

Where a specific taxable person (non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons exclusively engaged in VAT-
exempt activities, flat-rate farmers) established in Germany acquires goods from a taxable person in another
EU Member State, that acquirer is liable to report intra-Community acquisitions if the threshold of EUR
12,500 was exceeded during the previous calendar year or once this threshold is exceeded during the current
calendar year.

3.6 _ Effective use and enjoyment

3.6.1 Outbound transactions

For the following outbound services performed to a taxable person established in Germany, sec. 3a para, 8 of
the VAT Act (based on Art. 59a(a) of the EU VAT Directive) stipulates that these are taxable outside the EU if
the effective use and enjoyment of the services takes place outside the EU:

+ transport of goods, loading / unloading and similar services connected to transport of goods;

+ work on movable, tangible goods and evaluation of such goods; and

+ certain services relating to fairs, exhibitions and such travel services.

Furthermore, sec. 3a para. 7 of the VAT Act stipulates that the short-term rental of a railway vehicle, a motor
bus or a road vehicle intended exclusively to transport goods, the rental is deemed to be rendered outside the
EU:

+ if the supply is provided to a taxable person established outside the BU;

+ the vehicle is intended for his business; and

+ is used in the non-EU country.

3.6.2 Inbound transactions
For the following inbound services performed by a taxable person established outside the EU, where the
240 | Germany

--- Page 57 ---
supply would otherwise be regarded as made outside the BU, sec. 3a para. 6 of the VAT Act (based on
Art. 59a(b) of the EU VAT Directive) stipulates that these are taxable in Germany if the effective use and
enjoyment of the services takes place in Germany:

+ short-term renial of means of transport (B2B and B2C)

+ long term rental of means of transport (B2B and B2C); and

+ telecommunications and radio and television broadcasting (B2B and B2C).

‘This regulation, furthermore, covers the following services if rendered by a taxable person established outside

the EU to a legal person under public law established in Germany if used and enjoyed in Germany:

+ transfers/assignments of copyrights, patents, licenses, trade marks and similar rights as well as the
obligation to refrain from such rights;

+ advertising services;

+ services of consultants. engineers, lawyers, accountants and similar services;

+ translation services;

+ data processing;

+ provision of information;

+ banking, financial and insurance transactions;

+ supply of staff;

+ obligation to refrain from a business activity; and

+ hiring out of movable property, except all means of transport.

4, Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods
or services are supplied. For goods, this is the date when they are placed at the disposal of the acquirer.

A supply of goods involving their transportation is deemed to be carried out at the date the transport
commences. A supply of service is deemed to be carried out at the date the service is finally executed.

For intra-Community acquisition of goods, the VAT becomes chargeable on issue of the invoice, latest on the
15* day of the month following the acquisition if no invoice has been issued by that time.

For supplies subject to the reverse charge mechanism, the VAT becomes chargeable on issue of the invoice,
latest on the last day of the month following the supply if no invoice has been issued by that time. The VAT
on the purchase of a service, which falls under the main rules of Articles 44 and 196 of the BU VAT Directive,
is nevertheless due at the time the service is supplied.

If VAT was unduly charged, this VAT becomes chargeable on the date of issuing the invoice.

42 Advance payments

Where a payment is to be made on account before the goods or services are supplied, VAT becomes
chargeable on receipt of the payment.

4.3 Continuous supplies

Services ongoing over a period of more than 1 year, that do not give rise to statements of account or payments
in that period and for which the customer is liable to account for the VAT on the basis of Article 196 EU VAT
Directive, are regarded as being supplied on the expiry of each full calendar year up to the time the provision
of the services ceases.

4.4 Cash accounting

Upon request, the tax authorities may permit a taxable person to opt to apply a cash accounting scheme for
the payment of the VAT, in accordance with sec. 20 of the VAT Act, if:

Germany | 241

--- Page 58 ---
+ the total turnover in the previous calendar year did not exceed EUR 800,000;

+ the taxable person is exempt from the obligation to keep an accounting pursuant to Article 148 of the
General Fiscal Code; or

* to the extent the laxable person carries out supplies as a member of an independent profession within the
meaning of Sec. 18 para. 1 no. 1 of the Income Tax Act.

Where the scheme applies, output VAT is chargeable when the payment is received, and input VAT is
chargeable when the payment is made. It is not foreseen in the VAT legislation to mention ‘cash accounting”
on the invoice.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

In Germany, bad debt relief is available to taxable persons, provided they prove to the satisfaction of the tax
authorities that the amount not yet paid by the customer became a bad debt in the relevant period (sec. 17
of the VAT Act). The claim is made by reducing the tax base in the VAT return in the period of the debt
becoming uncollectable. The tax office may require evidence of uncollectability, but there are no guidelines
for this.

5.2 Cash discounts

In cases where the supplier offers a discount for settlement within a specific time limit, the taxable amount
shall be the amount after the discount is applied, meaning the actual payment.

Example:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
19% VAT EUR 190
Total to be paid EUR 1,190

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier must pay EUR 186 (EUR 190 less 2%)
in his VAT return. The customer can deduct EUR 186 in his VAT return, The supplier does not need to send a
credit invoice for the 2%.

5.3 Open Market Value

Where sole traders supply goods or services to their related parties and where corporate entities and

associations of persons supply goods or services to their sharebolders, proprietors, members, partner or

person related to such and if the consideration actually paid is lower than;

+ the acquisition or production costs (where goods are supplied);

+ the costs associated with the provision of a service, to the extent input VAT was deducted for these costs
(where a service is rendered),

then the costs are the taxable amount rather than the consideration paid (sec. 10 para. 5 no. | of the VAT Act).

However, in such scenarios, the taxable amount shall be limited by the open market value.

‘The same regulation applies to supplies of goods and services by a taxable person to its employees and their
relatives on the basis of the employment relationship (sec. 10 para. 5 no. 2 of the VAT Act).
54 Returnable packaging

Germany did not implement any of the options according to Art, 92 of the EU VAT Directive in the VAT Act.
However, the Administrative VAT Guidelines provide for extensive regulations on returnable packaging and
differ between different kinds of packaging:

242 | Germany

--- Page 59 ---
+ packaging required for the delivery of the goods, e.g. bottles: the amount to be paid for it is deemed to be
part of the taxable amount of the packed goods:

* packaging facilitating the transport. e.g. pallets, crates, containers: the amount to be paid for it is deemed
to be a remuneration for a separate supply of the packaging.

Once the packaging is returned to the supplier and the remuneration for the packaging is paid back to the
customer, the taxable amount for the initial supply is deemed to be reduced and the VAT can be recovered by
the supplier. If the customer is 2 taxable person, he must reduce the input VAT deduction accordingly. It is not
required to issue a credit note or any similar document.

6. VAT rates

61 Standard rate
‘The standard rate in Germany is 19% (sec. 12 para. 1 of the VAT Act).

6.2 Reduced rate

The reduced rate in Germany is 7%. Sec. 12 para. 2 of the VAT Act stipulates to which supplies it applies, for
instance:

+ the supply of food and beverages (except alcoholic beverages);

+ hotel accommodation;

+ books, magazines and newspapers;

* passenger transport; and

+ art.

6.3 Super-reduced and zero rates

Germany has not implemented a super-reduced rate or a zero rate.

7. Exemptions

TA Exemptions with the right to deduction

Germany zero-rates the following supplies and services (with the right to input VAT deduction) according to
sec. 4 no, 1 ~ 7 of the VAT Act

* exports and intra~-Community supplies of goods;
+ supplies relating to maritime traffic and aviation;
* cross-border transportation of goods related to import/export;

* supplies of monetary gold to central banks;

* supplies of goods under customs control or VAT warehousing arrangements;

+ the transport of goods under customs bond;

* — supplies to armed forces of the NATO and to armed forces of other EU Member States; and
+ — supplies to intergovernmental institutions and to diplomatic missions.

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

The intra-Community supply of goods is zero-rated according to sec. 4 no. | lit. b of the VAT Act under the
conditions of sec. 6a of the VAT Act and if the particular Recapitulative Statement was filed correctly.

Furthermore, proof is necessary that the goods have left Germany. Acceptable proof includes certain
bookkeeping records, the transport documentation and certain confirmations of the customer. If a freight
forwarder is involved, a copy of the invoice and a special confirmation of the freight forwarder are
required. However, the standard proof for intra~Community supplies is the so-called Gelangensbestatigung
(confirmation of arrival — entry certificate). The above rules can still be used in order to prove the transport

Germany | 243

--- Page 60 ---
besides the presumptions of transport as mentioned in Art, 45a Implementing Regulation which was also
transposed into national law.

7.1.2 Export supplies

The export of goods is zero-rated according to sec. 4 no. 1 lit. a of the VAT Act under the conditions of sec. 6
of the VAT Act.

Furthermore, proof is necessary that the goods have left the EU. The so-called Ausgangsvermerk
(confirmation of export) of the Automated Tariff and Local Customs Clearance System (ATLAS) is
considered to be the standard documentary proof. Altemative proof, such as bills of lading, airway bills or
freight forwarder certificates, is accepted only if an export has not been declared in the electronic ATLAS
procedure or if, in special cases, the electronic export procedure could not be completed as required.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Germany implemented VAT warehouse regulations. A permission from the tax authorities is necessary to
maintain a VAT warehouse. The warehouse can be used for the storage of specific types of predetermined
goods. In addition to the goods listed in Annex V of the EU VAT Directive, the following additional products
can be put into a VAT warehouse:

+ gold (CN code 7108);

+ pulp from wood (CN code 4703);

+ iron and steel products (CN code 7207 - 7212); and

+ non-ferrous metal (CN code 8101).

Anon-established trader is not required to register for VAT when placing goods in the VAT warehouse but
must register for VAT when selling goods in the VAT warchouse or releasing goods from a VAT warehouse.
When placing non-EU goods into a VAT warehouse, the importation is exempt for VAT purposes. This
exemption becomes final for the supplier of the goods if the goods are sold in the VAT warehouse and remain
under the arrangement. The release from the VAT warehouse arrangement triggers VAT liability, unless an
exemption (e.g. intra-Community supply, exportation) applies, In that case the non-established trader in
principle must register in order to declare the intra-Community supply. But if this is his only activity, he may
appoint a fiscal representative.

However, Germany decided to abolish the VAT warehouse regulations as from Ist January 2026 since these
were applied rarely and required extensive administrative efforts.

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse
Germany has not implemented excise warehousing.
7.1.3.3 Bonded (customs) warehouse

Bonded warehouses are possible in Germany but are covered by the Customs Code. There is, generally, no
influence on VAT if goods are placed in a bonded warehouse. If the goods are placed in a customs warehouse,
no import VAT applies as the goods are not released into free circulation but remain under customs control.
The sale within the warehouse is subject to a zero VAT rate under the condition that the goods are finally
cleared for customs. Import VAT is due when the goods are cleared for customs.

72 Exemptions without the right to deduction

There are various exemptions without the right to input VAT deduction in Germany (sec. 4 no. 8 — 29 of the
VAT Act). A few are mentioned below.
7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest

Certain hospital services, medical and paramedical care, cultural activities, ambulance services, services
rendered by undertakers, educational services, services of a cultural or social nature, some services connected
with sports, etc. are examples of exemptions in the public interest. Some exemptions, however, are granted

244 | Germany

--- Page 61 ---
on the condition that the supplier is a non-profit institution and/or on the condition that no distortion of
competition will occur as a result of the exemption.

7.2.2 Other exemptions

The other exemptions are financial and banking services, insurance services, management of collective
investment funds, postal services, non-commercial activities, letting of immovable property, the supply of
immovable property, services by religious, charitable organizations, etc.

7.3 Option for taxation

The taxable person may (among others) opt for taxation in case of certain financial transactions, insurance
transactions, sale and rental of real estate if the turnover is executed for another taxable person for the taxable
activity of the latter. In case of rental of real estate, the option is only allowed if the customer is exclusively
using or planning to use the real estate for turnovers allowing input VAT deduction.

8. Liability

81 Reverse Charges

In Germany there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B
services provided by non-resident suppliers to taxable persons and legal entities with a German VAT-ID
number established in Germany. This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and follows from Art.
44 and Art. 196 EU VAT Directive.

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism - non-resident suppliers (Art. 194 EU VAT Directive)

There is a reverse charge for services supplied in Germany as well as for supplies of goods including
installation (limited to Werklieferungen — not applicable to Montagelieferungen — see 3.2) in Germany
performed by a non-established taxable person (sec. 13b para. | and para. 2 no. | in conjunction with sec. 13b
para. 5 of the VAT Act). A mere VAT registration does not avoid reverse charge, i.e. the reverse charge also
applies if a non-established supplier is registered for VAT in Germany. On the other hand, reverse charge
applies irrespective of whether or not the recipient is VAT registered or resident in Germany. The recipient
may be obliged to register in order to fulfil the reverse charge obligation. The reverse charge scheme

for supplies of non-established taxable persons does not apply to certain passenger transport services, to
admission to conferences, exhibitions, fairs etc., to services of fair organization companies rendered to non-
established persons and to restaurant services aboard ships, planes and trains.

On the invoice, reference must be made to the application of the reverse charge scheme. The supplier must
use the following text on the invoice “Steuerschuldnerschaft des Leistungsempfiingers”. A remark in a foreign
language (for example: Reverse Charge") is possible.

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism - specific domestic rules (Art. 199 EU VAT Directive)

On the following supplies of goods and/or services the domestic reverse charge rules apply (sec. 13b para. 2
no. 2~ 12 of the VAT Act in conjunction with sec. 13b para. 5 of the VAT Act) :

+ supply of goods provided as security by one taxable person to another in execution of that security outside
insolvency procedure;

+ — supply of immovable property with an option for taxation;

+ supply of construction work, particularly supplies of goods including installation and supplies of services
in relation to immovable property aiming for the construction, repair, maintenance, alteration and
demolition of buildings and constructions to customers providing construction work on a regular basis;

+ supply of natural gas and electricity supplied by non-resident suppliers to “taxable dealers”;

+ supply of emission rights;

+ supply of old, used materials, scrap, waste and specific services;

+ cleaning of buildings;

+ supply of gold;

+ supply of mobiles, tablet computers, games consoles and integrated circuits if the value is not less than
EUR 5,000;

Germany | 245

--- Page 62 ---
+ supply of certain precious metals (like silver, platinum etc.) if the value is not less than EUR 5,000; and
+ telecommunication services to resellers.

Reverse charge always applies if the customer is a taxable person, regardless if he is established or VAT
registered in Germany or not.

A supplier must refer to the reverse charge scheme. The following text is to be mentioned on the invoice
“Steuerschuldnerschaft des Leistungsempfingers”. A remark in a foreign language (for example: Reverse
Charge“) is possible.

8.2 i nt and several liability

Under German law, managing directors of businesses can be held jointly and severally liable for the unpaid
taxes of the businesses they manage.

Joint and several liability also exists for businesses that form part of a VAT group for the VAT debts of each
member of the VAT group for the entire period that the relevant entity was part of that VAT group.

Joint and several liability also exists in situations of carousel fraud.

9. Recovery of input VAT

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

The input VAT is not recoverable in case of:

+ purchases being related to VAT exempt supplies not allowing input VAT deduction;

+ gifts for non-employees if the limit of EUR 50 per year and per recipient is exceeded; and

* expenses for guest houses, hunting and fishing, yachts and excessive expenses related to private living.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT is immediately recoverable and refunded by the VAT authorities.

9.3. Adjustment periods

+ immovable property - 10 years after first use;
+ movable property - 5 years after first use.

94 VAT refund to EU taxable persons

The claim must be submitted electronically via a portal of the country of residence and is submitted by the
local tax authorities to the Bundeszentralamt fiir Steuern (www,bzst.bund.dc), The minimum period is 3
months (mninimum amount: EUR 400), maximum period is one year (minimum amount: EUR 50). The claino
must be filed within 9 months after the end of the calendar year (30 September). After that date, filing of a
return is only possible in very limited circumstances (for example, in case of serious illness etc.).

Scanned invoices or import documents must be attached if the VAT amount is more that EUR 1,000 (fuel: EUR 250).

The deadline for refunding the outstanding VAT amount is 4 months and 10 working days after receipt of
the claim (ie, application), otherwise the refund is interest-bearing, The deadline is postponed in case the
refunding office needs additional information. After having received an assessment, the period for a possible
appeal is normally one month.

The special procedure is only applicable if the foreign taxable person is not obliged to register for VAT in
Germany. If the foreign taxable person is obliged to register for VAT in Germany, all input VAT incurred must
be declared in the VAT return,

For non-refundable VAT please refer to 9.1.

--- Page 63 ---
95 VAT refund to non-EU taxable persons

In case of a non-EU non-resident taxable person, a refund claim must be submitted electronically using the
Online-Portal of the Bundeszentralamt filr Steuern (BOP). Claimants will have to register first before using
the BOP. Detailed information is available at: http://www.bzst.de.

The minimum period is 3 months (minimum amount: EUR 1,000), maximum period is one year (minimum
amount: EUR 500). The claim must be filed within 6 months after the end of the calendar year (i.e. before
30 June), A submission of the claim on paper and a signature is not required anymore due to the registration
process prior to the electronic filing, After 30 June, filing of a claim is only possible in very limited
circumstances (for example in case of serious illness ete.).

All original invoices, original import documents and a certificate of taxable status must be sent to the
Bundeszentralamt flir Steuern by postal mail. These original documents must arrive before the 6 months
period elapses.
