10.

Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

A VAT invoice must contain the following information (sec. 14 para. 4 of the VAT Act):

date on which the invoice is issued;

invoice number (unique and sequential);

VAT identification number/tax number under which the supply is performed;

full name and address of the supplier;

full name and address of the customer and the VAT identification number of the customer in case of intra-

Community supplies and cross border reverse charges;

quantity and nature of the supplied goods or quantity and type of the supplied services;

date of supply of goods or services; in the case where an invoice is issued before the respective supply

is carried out, the date when the payment is fully or partially received, provided that this payment date is

known and is not identical to the invoice date;

taxable amount, split up according to the applicable VAT rates resp. applicable VAT exemptions, as well

as any discounts or rebates agreed beforehand unless they are not already accounted for in the taxable

amount;

applied VAT rate(s);

VAT amount;

reason for the application of an exemption, zero rate or reverse charge. For instance:

+ intra~-Community supply: “Tax exempt intra~Community supply” resp. “steuerfreie
innergemeinschaftliche Lieferung”;

+ export supply: “Tax exempt export supply” resp. “steuerfreier Export”;

+ reverse charge: “reverse charge” resp. “Steuerschuldnerschaft des Leistungsempfingers”;

name, address and VAT identification number of the fiscal representative (if applicable);

the information necessary to determine whether there is a new means of transport;

in case of self-billing the remark “Gutschrift”; and

an indication of the fact that a margin scheme has been applied.

Please note that the references are not required to be in German.

10.1.2 Time limits

For

supplies of goods including installation (“Werklieferung”) and supplies of services in connection with

immovable property, an invoice has to be issued within 6 months after the supply was performed. For supplies
to other taxable persons or to non-taxable legal persons an invoice has to be issued within 6 months after

248

| Germany

--- Page 65 ---
the supply was performed as well. For certain transactions, such as intra-Community supplies and supplies
subject to the reverse charge, the invoices must be issued within 15 days after the end of the month in which
the supply was rendered.

10.

The invoice may be issued in any currency. It is not necessary to mention an exchange rate or an equivalent in
Euro.

3 Currency

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate
For conversion of the invoice into a foreign currency either the actual bank rate must be used or, alternatively,

a monthly exchange rate published by the German Federal Ministry of Finance. The exchange rate of the
ECB may also be used.

10.1.5 Self-billing

‘Under German VAT legislation, self-billing is allowed in cases where it was agreed upon in advance. If the
recipient of the document protests it, then the document loses the character of an invoice. On the document, it
must be stated that it is a self-billing invoice (“Gutschrift” resp. “self-billing”).

10.1.6 Simplified invoices

It is possible to issue a simplified invoice if the total amount of the invoice is not higher than EUR 400, A.
simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ full name and full address of the supplier;

«date of issue;

+ quantity and nature of the supplied goods or the quantity and type of the supplied services;

+ total invoice amount including taxable amount and VAT amount for the supply; and

+ applicable VAT rate or reason for the application of an exemption.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices
Invoices need to be kept available for 8 years. Generally, invoices must be stored in Germany. After approval

by the tax authorities, invoices may be stored outside of Germany. Invoices may be stored electronically. In
case of electronic invoicing, it is obligatory to store the invoices electronically.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Invoices can be issued by electronic means, after acceptance by the recipient, provided that the authenticity of
the origin, the integrity of the content and the legibility of the invoice is ensured.

With effect from Ist January 2025 the regulations on electronic invoicing were extensively amended
Electronic invoices in a structured format (according to Directive 2014/55/EC and CEN standard 16931 or
interoperable with it) have to be issued under the following conditions:

+ supplier is a taxable person established in Germany;

+ customer is a taxable person established in Germany;

+ place of supply is deemed to be in Germany; and

+ supply is not VAT-exempt according to sec. 4 no, 8 ~ 29 of the VAT Act.

The ¢-invoicing (in a structured format) does no longer require the consent of the parties.

The e-invoice obligation applies from 1 January 2025, with transitional provisions only for the invoice issuer,
i.e. the obligation to issue invoices. Invoice recipients are obliged to accept the receipt of e-invoices, without
restriction from 2025 if they receive supplies from other taxable persons established in Germany.

The following transitional provisions apply to invoice issuers:

+ In 2025 and 2026, paper invoices and other electronic invoices, such as PDF (the latter still only with the
consent of the invoice recipient) will continue to be permitted, in addition to e-invoices in a structured
format;

+ In 2027, taxable persons with a total turnover of up to EUR 800,000 in the previous calendar year may
continue to issue paper invoices and other electronic invoices (the latter still only with the consent of the
invoice recipient) to their customers);

Germany | 249

--- Page 66 ---
+ In 2027, EDI invoices may also continue to be issued with the consent of the invoice recipient. EDI
invoices may only be used if they are compatible with thc CEN standard and both parties have agreed to
their use.

Simplified invoices are not subject to the e-invoicing regulations,
10.2 Compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

‘The standard VAT return period is a calendar quarter, Monthly VAT retums must be filed if the yearly VAT
liability exceeded EUR 7,500 in the previous calendar year. Additionally, a yearly (summarizing) VAT retum
must be filed.

No quarterly or monthly VAT returns but only annual VAT returns must be submitted if the annual amount of
VAT due is less than EUR 1,000, The VAT returns, in general, have to be submitted electronically.
10.2.2 Filing of a VAT return and payment

The VAT due must be received on the bank account of the tax authorities on the 10" day of the following
month. If this date falls on a weekend or a bank holiday, the VAT is due on the next business day (i.e. the VAT
for October 2024 must be received by the VAT authorilies by 11" November 2024). The same applies for the
VAT return.

An extension of one month may be granted against prepayment (1/1! of the last year’s VAT liability). Filing
can be done via htips://www.elsteronline.de/eportal/
10.2.3 European sales listing

European sales listings must be submitted on a monthly basis if the intra~Community supply of goods
exceeds EUR 50,000 in the current quarter or in any of the 4 preceding quarters. The European sales listings
for services can be submitted on a quarterly basis, regardless of the period for the intra-Community supply
of goods. The European sales listings must be filed, as a general rule, electronically. Filing can be done via
huips://www.elsteronline.de/eportal/. There is no threshold for filing the European sales listings.

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

There are no national recapitulative statements in Germany except for the yearly summarizing VAT return.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

In Germany the statute of limitation is 4 years (15 years in case of tax fraud). The statute of limitation starts at
the end of the year in which the tax return had been filed (ie. if the VAT return for 2021 was filed in 2022, the
period ends on 31 December 2026).

In order to avoid that the deadline never ends if the taxable person fails to file a return, there is the fall-back
position that the limitation period starts automatically after 3 years (i.e. if no VAT return is filed for 2021, the
limitation period ends on 31 December 2028). After that date, assessments or amendments are no longer possible.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or an EC Sales List on time

If the VAT return is not filed on time, the penalty may amount to up to 10% of the tax due, with a maximum
of EUR 25,000, The penalty is at the discretion of the tax authorities.

If the EC Sales List is not filed on time, the VAT authorities can issue a penalty of 1% of the tax base to be
declared in the listing with a maximum of EUR 2,500.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

Tf the VAT is not paid on time, the VAT authorities will assess a penalty of 1% of the outstanding amount per
month,

250 | Germany

--- Page 67 ---
10.3.3 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

Interest will be assessed on outstanding amounts as well as on refunds. The rate is 0.15% per month, Interest
period starts 15 months after the end of the respective catendar year (ie. for 2024 interest will be paid starting
from | April 2026).

10.3.4 Offence penalties

The VAT law allows penalties up to BUR 50,000, In case of tax fraud, the penalty may be up to 5 years prison
ora fine, The interest rate is 0.5% per month,

10.4 Intrastat

Intrastat returns must be submitted to the Statistical Central Office (Statistisches Bundesamt). A monthly
return is required which is due on the 10th working day of the following month at the latest.

All taxable persons who are registered for VAT in Germany (and were assigned a German VAT number) are
generally required to file Intrastat returns if they perform intra-Community supplies or acquisitions (including
transfer of own goods) unless the threshold mentioned below is not exceeded. This also applies to distance
sales. Only sales to private persons in other EU Member States where the customer is responsible for the
transport, do not have to be reported. Ifa fiscal representative was appointed, the obligation to file Intrastat
returns is shifted to the fiscal representative.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
The Intrastat threshold is for the arrivals EUR 3,000,000 and for the dispatches EUR 1,000,000 per year.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

The information which needs to be reported (depending on arrival or dispatch) is:
+ VAT number of the supplier;

+ reference period (i.e. the reporting month);

+ EU Member State of dispatch or destination;

+ State of dispatch or destination;

+ country of origin;

+ nature of the transaction (e.g, purchase, sale, return of goods, repair etc.);

+ means of transport, for instance “by road” or “by sea”;

+ HS Code (8-digit harmonized commodity code according to Combined Nomenclature (C'N));
+ statistical value in Euro;

+ VAT ID number of the customer;

+ quantity of the goods (i.e. net weight in kilograms);

+ other measure for certain goods (liters, m? or number of pieces); and

* invoice value of the goods in Euro.

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

There are no additional requirements.

Greece

Value Added Tax (VAT) was introduced in Greece in 1986 (and came into force as of Ol January 1987)
through law 1642/1986 and is currently regulated by law 5144/2024 “Kedrxac @épov [Ipooti@épevng
Ablac” (“Value Added Tax Code”), Greek VAT legislation transposes into domestic law the provisions of
the European Union (EU) VAT Directive. VAT is referred to in Greece as “@dpog poo 0éjevnc Aziac” or
“OILA”.

1. Taxable persons

1.1 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Domestic taxable persons, engaging in economic activity, have the obligation to register with the tax
authorities and acquire a tax registration number, There is no separate registration for VAT purposes or a
separate VAT number, as the tax registration number acquired covers all taxation purposes. In this respect,
there is no threshold for taxable persons as to their obligation to be registered with the tax authorities.

If, however, a specific business falls under a special VAT scheme or engages in VAT exempt activities, or
activities outside the scope of VAT, this is declared to the tax authorities upon its registration, so that its VAT
obligations, if any, are observed by the tax authorities accordingly.

Greek VAT law provides for the small enterprises scheme with a threshold of EUR 10,000. Still however the
persons that are eligible for the scheme are required to VAT register.

The tax registration is made with the central tax administration. The documents that need to be provided for
the tax registration vary, depending on the legal type of the domestic taxable person.

Additionally, domestic taxable persons need to submit certain other forms which must be completed as well
for the registration,

The forms may be downloaded at the following link: https:/tinyurl.com/3sv9vhrm

1.2 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

Foreign taxable persons that perform taxable transactions in Greece for which a VAT registration is required,
have the obligation to VAT register, without any threshold or exception being applicable. The VAT registration
of EU entities is made electronically, through submission by email of the required form to the designated tax
office at the e-mail address doyaathinon@ 1836.syzefxis.gov.gr

‘The aforementioned form can be downloaded at the following link: hitps://tinyurl.com/3sv9vhrm

The tax office subsequently verifies the declared information with the data recorded with the VAT Information
Exchange System (VIES) and, having confirmed that this information is accurate, issues the VAT number and
communicates it to the applicant by email. On average, the VAT number is issued within two weeks of the
electronic submission of the required form.

In the case of non-EU taxable persons, Greek VAT registration is made through the appointment of a fiscal
representative. EU taxable persons who need to VAT register in Greece have the option, but are not required,
to appoint a fiscal representative, if they do not want to proceed with the above direct registration. To this end,
the foreign business needs to send:

= a power of attomey to the (legal or natural) person that will be appointed as the fiscal representative;

+ its articles of association; and

+ acertificate of good standing.

Greece | 253

--- Page 70 ---
All these documents must be notarized and apostilled. The fiscal representative submits them, together with
their translation in Greek and certain tax forms filled in by the fiscal representative, to the fax office that is
competent for the fiscal representative.

These tax forms can be downloaded at the following link: ht(ps://tinyurl.com/3sv9vhrm
On average, the VAT number is issued within two weeks of the submission of the above documents.

13 VAT grouping
VAT grouping is not possible under Greek VAT law.

1.4 Fiscal representative

Taxable persons established in non-EU countries that carry out taxable transactions in Greece for which
they become liable for VAT (and for which the reverse charge mechanism is not applicable), are in principle
required to appoint a fiscal representative in order to fulfil their Greek VAT obligations.

On the other hand, taxable persons established in EU Member States that carry out taxable transactions in
Greece for which they become liable for VAT (and for which the reverse charge mechanism is not applicable)
have the option to appoint a fiscal representative, or to proceed with a direct registration. In all cases where

a fiscal representative is appointed, the representative is liable to the Greek State for the fulfilment of his
principal’s Greek VAT obligations. For the procedure of the appointment of a fiscal representative see
paragraph 1.2.

By way of exception from the above, non-EU taxable persons are not required to appoint a fiscal
representative in case they operate under a special license for the deferral of VAT on import (see paragraph
2.3.2.1) in which case they may proceed with a direct registration like EU taxable persons.

In the event the EU or non-EU taxable person, who has the requirement to VAT register in Greece and
account for Greek VAT, does not proceed with such registration, then the Greek customer becomes liable
to the Greek State to account for the VAT due that is not charged and paid by the foreign supplier, if such
customer is a taxable person. However, this will not be the case, if the EU or non-EU taxable person is
considered to have a fixed establishment in Greece,

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

According to Greek VAT legislation, the transfer of a going concer falls outside the scope of VAT.

This applies to the transfer of a business in its totality, sectors or parts of a business, with or without
consideration, or in the form of contribution to existing or new legal persons, unless the transferor or the
transferee is engaged exclusively in VAT exempt activities which do not allow the deduction of the input VAT.
‘The transferee is required to continue carrying out the transferred business.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

The application by a taxable person of goods forming part of his business assets for his private use or for that

of his staff, or their disposal free of charge for purposes other than those of his business, is treated as a supply

of goods for consideration, where the VAT on those goods or the component parts thereof was deductible.

However, the application of goods for business use as samples or as gifts of small value shall not be treated as

a supply of goods for consideration. Gifts of small value are defined as goods:

+ whose purchase price, or in the absence thereof, their market value, excluding VAT, does not exceed EUR
10 per person

+ the goods offered do not constitute part of a successive series of offers to the same person;

+ the total value of the goods offered does not exceed in one fiscal year 1% of the gross revenues of the enterprise; and

284 | Greece

--- Page 71 ---
+ the goods offered are not of the same kind as the goods the enterprise sells, produces or manufactures.

Samples are goods offered by an enterprise to its clients and to the public, without consideration, for
promotional purposes, provided that:

+ they are the same with the goods sold, manufactured or produced by the enterprise

+ bear the mark “free sample”; and

+ they are offered in reasonable quantities so that they become known and their quality is assessed.

2.3 Importation

2.3.1. Importation followed by an intra~Community supply

It is possible to apply an exemption on the importation of goods into Greece (with the exception of excise
goods), if it is followed by an intra-Community supply (within 5 days). In such case the importer must be a
taxable person registered with VIES. If the importer is established in another EU Member State or established.
in a non-EU country, he needs to appoint a fiscal representative in Greece or, under certain conditions, acquire
a VAT number under the direct registration procedure (this latter option is only available for EU established
importers).

The exemption is applicable provided that the customer who is established in the other EU Member State is a
taxable person registered with VIES. Furthermore, the respective invoices need to be submitted to the relevant
customs authorities, as well as the transport contract or transport documents proving the transport of the
goods to the other EU Member State. Subsequently the importer needs to submit to the customs authorities
the European Sales Listing and Intrastat return where the intra-Community supply has been declared.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

2.3.2.1 Import VAT deferral scheme

Foreign taxable persons with no establishment in Greece are allowed to perform imports into Greece without
paying VAT upon importation at the customs office. The tax is assessed on the respective customs documents
but it is accounted for by the importer through the filing of the respective periodical VAT return. To this end, a
VAT registration is required. By way of exception, non-EU taxable persons which qualify for this scheme are
not required to appoint a fiscal representative but they can proceed with a direct registration like EU taxable
persons.

The application of the above simplification is subject to strict conditions, among which, the realization of an
annual yolume of imports of at least EUR 40 million and the export or intra~Community supply of at least
90% of the statistical value of the imported items on an annual basis. In case of groups of companies, the
aforementioned requirements are taken into consideration for all the companies of the group as a whole.
Special licensing is provided in this respect, subject to the aforesaid conditions and other standards (e.g.
creditworthiness) being met. The license may include all the group companies that will qualify for this
simplification,

2.3.2.2 Other simplified procedures allowing postponed accounting of VAT on import

Greek corporate entities or natural persons that engage in transactions which fall within the competence of
customs authorities and need to be declared to them, are allowed to apply for simplified procedures on import
of goods from non-EU countries. Instead of paying the duties and VAT every time an import is made, the duties
and VAT of the whole month can be declared cumulatively until the 5" day of the month following the month
of importation, This total amount must subsequently be paid ultimately on the 16" day of that month. For
deduction purposes the VAT thus paid can be included in the VAT return of the period during which it was paid.

The aforementioned simplified procedures may apply following the issuance of a relevant authorization by

the competent customs authorities, Such authorization is subject to a guarantee and the fulfilment of the

following criteria;

+ appropriate record of compliance with customs requirements of the applicant, the persons in charge of the
applicant company or exercising control over its management and the person responsible in the applicant
company for customs matters;

Greece [ 255

--- Page 72 ---
+ proven financial solvency; and
+ satisfactory system of managing commercial, accounting and where appropriate, transport records.

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

Greece has implemented the call-off stock simplification in accordance with Directive 2018/1910 without any
deviation.

In this respect, the movement of goods to Greece shall not be treatcd as an intra-Community transaction and

therefore the taxable person making the transfer of the stock will not declare an intra~Community supply

in the EU Member State of dispatch and an intra~-Community acquisition in Greece (for which it would be

required to obtain a VAT number in Greece, before the application of the simplification in question) at the

time it transfers the stock.

Instead, the taxable person making the transfer will declare an intra-Community supply in the EU Member

State of dispatch and the acquirer in Greece will declare an intra-Community acquisition, only at the time

the acquirer withdraws goods from the stock located in Greece. Thus the obligation for the supplier lo VAT

register in Greece will be avoided, provided that the below conditions for the call-off stock simplification are
met: both the supplier and the intended acquirer are taxable persons for VAT purposes;

+ the supplier has not established his business and he does not have a fixed establishment in Greece (VAT
registration of the supplier in Greece does not amount to establishment, therefore it allows the application
of the call-off simplification);

+ the goods are transported by the supplier or by a third party on his behalf from another EU Member State
to Greece with the intention of being supplied in Greece and after arrival to an intended customer;

+ the intended acquirer’s identity and VAT number are known by the supplier at the time when the transport
begins;

+ the acquirer is entitled to take ownership of the goods in accordance with an existing agreement;

+ the supplier should record the transfer of the goods in a special register, including the identity and the
VAT number of the acquirer; and

+ the supply to the acquirer should be made within 12 months from the arrival of the goods in Greece.

No simplification is available under Greek VAT law for consignment stock, Accordingly, non-Greek suppliers
need to VAT register in Greece and account for the Greek VAT due on the intra-Community acquisition of
goods in Greece and their subsequent local sale,

3.2 Installation supplies

When goods are supplied from abroad to Greece and the foreign supplier undertakes their assembly or
installation in Greece, then such a transaction is treated as a single domestic supply of goods subject to

Greck VAT. The foreign supplier is liable to register for VAT in Greece and charge Greek VAT on the supply,
without the reverse charge mechanism being applicable. There is no formal definition in the VAT Law as to
what constitutes installation or assembly. According to the guidelines of the Greek Ministry of Finance, the
aforementioned treatment applies in the event the goods are supplied to the customer after their installation/
assembly in Greece, and a single price is set for the goods and the installation/assembly, regardless of whether
the price is paid in lump sum or partially.

3.3 Distance sales

In case non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons exclusively engaged in exempt activities and flat-rate
farmers, who have in common that they are not liable for payment of VAT on their intra~Community
acquisitions of goods and private individuals, all of which are established in Greece, purchase goods, other
than new means of transport, or goods supplied after assembly or installation, with or without a trial run, by
or on behalf of the supplier, from a supplier in another Member State, the supplier must charge Greek VAT,
unless the total value, exclusive of VAT, of supplies of telecommunications, broadcasting and electronic
services or supplies of distance sales of goods does not in the current calendar year exceed EUR 10,000,

256 ! Greece

--- Page 73 ---
or the equivalent in national currency, nor did it do so in the course of the preceding calendar year. Where.
during a calendar year, the threshold of EUR 10,000 is exceeded, Greek VAT applies as of that time. Suppliers
can opt anyway for the place of supply to be Greece, and in such case the option shall cover 2 calendar years

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

Under Greek VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rules have been implemented in line with the EU
VAT Directive. The simplified triangulation rules can be applied if (among other conditions):

* Greece is the country of arrival of the goods;

+ the middleman (B) is not registered in the country of dispatch; and

+ the middleman (B) is not established in Greece.

Consequently, where Greece is the EU Member State of arrival of the goods the wording of the Greek
legislation precludes the application of the triangulation rules if the middleman (B) is VAT registered in the
country of dispatch, The middleman (B) must mention on his invoice that this is a triangular transaction. In
case Greece is the country of arrival of the goods, the simplification cannot be applied when 4 parties are
involved.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the intra~Community acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons
engaged in exempt activities with no right to deduct input VAT and flat-rate farmers ftom suppliers
established in another EU Member State is EUR 10,000 for Greece.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Greece has implemented the effective use and enjoyment rule provided in Article 59a(a) of the EU VAT

Directive for the following services:

* transport of goods supplied to taxable persons; when the place of supply of such services is situated
within Greece, it will be considered as situated outside the EU when the transport services take place
exclusively outside the EU; and

+ short-term hiring of professional pleasure boats to taxable or non-taxable persons; when the place of such
services is situated within Greece, it will be considered as situated outside the EU when the boats are used
outside the EU;

Furthermore, Greece has implemented the effective use and enjoyment rule provided in Article 59a(b) of the

EU VAT Directive. In this respect, for the following services for which the place of supply is situated outside

the EU, Greece will be considered the place of supply (and, therefore, Greek VAT will be applicable) if the

effective use and enjoyment of these services takes place within Greece:

+ short-term hiring of means of transport to taxable or non-taxable persons and long-term hiring of means
of transport to non-taxable persons, to the extent the use of such means of transport takes place in Greece;

+ _ hiring out of movable tangible property to non-taxable persons, to the extent the use of such goods takes
place in Greece; and

+ telecommunications services, radio and television broadcasting services and electronically supplied
services to non-taxable persons, to the extent the recipient of the services is in Greece at the time such
services are supplied.

4, Chargeable event and chargeability of VAT

44 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods or
services are supplied.

When however the obligation exists to issue an invoice, VAT becomes chargeable on the date of issuing the
invoice, or on the date the invoice should have been issued. The VAT on the purchase of a service, which

Greece | 257

--- Page 74 ---
falls under the main rules of Articles 44 and 196 of the EU VAT Directive, is nevertheless due at the time the
service is supplied.

For intra-Community supplies of goods, VAT becomes chargeable when the invoice is issued and latest on the
15® day of the following month,

4.2 Advance payments

VAT does not become chargeable in case of advance payments, with the exception of advance payments that
correspond to services that fall within the main rule of Article 44 of the VAT Directive for which their place of
supply is Greece.

4.3. Continuous supplies

For supplies of services for which the consideration has been agreed to be paid in instalments, VAT becomes
chargeable when each instalment is due.

For supplies of services which last for more than one year and fall under the main rule of Srticle 44 of the
VAT Directive, VAT becomes chargeable at the end of the calendar year provided that no payments have
become due during the year.

4.4 Cash accounting

VAT is due to the tax authorities, when actually received from the customer in case of suppliers with an
annual turnover up to EUR 2 million, Cash accounting is optional and does not apply to exempt and zero rate
transactions, imports and intra-Community acquisitions of goods, transactions to which the reverse charge is
applicable, retail transactions and transactions whose place of supply is outside Greece.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief
No bad debt relief is available under Greek VAT law.

However, the Greek Supreme Administrative Court has ruled that this is contrary to BU VAT law and the
taxable persons have the right to the refund of VAT on bad debts, in case the customer has been declared
bankrupt, has entered a rehabilitation procedure or has been placed under special administration, in
accordance with the Greek Insolvency Code.

Tn response to the above judgement of the Supreme Court, Greek tax administration issued guidelines which
only allowed bad debt relief for the rehabilitation procedure of a specific Greek retailer and its suppliers,
because this specific retailer’s case was examined by the Supreme Court. Although the Supreme Court ruled
in a general manner that VAT should be recovered when one of the aforementioned instances occur, Greek tax
administration limited the bad debt relief only to a specific case.

Nevertheless, in line with the aforementioned judgement of the Supreme Court, the Dispute Resolution
Directorate (which reviews administrative appeals) has also ruled that VAT should be refunded when the
customer has been declared bankrupt or has entered a rehabilitation procedure.

5.2 Cash discountsAssume that the supplier sends the following invoice:
“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
24% VAT EUR 240
Total to be paid EUR 1,240

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”.

258 | Greece

--- Page 75 ---
Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier must issue a credit invoice to the
customer for the amount of the discount plus the corresponding VAT. Accordingly, the supplier will deduct
the VAT charged with the credit invoice from the output VAT he will declare in the month the credit invoice is
issued. The customer will deduct the VAT charged with the credit invoice from the input VAT he will declare
in the month the credit invoice is issued.

5.3 Open market value

In case of supplies of goods or services between affiliated persons, the taxable amount is the open market
value in the following cases:

+ where the consideration is lower than the open market value and the recipient of the supply does not have
a full right of deduction;

+ where the consideration is lower than the open market value and the supplier does not have a full right of
deduction and the supply is VAT exempt; and

* where the consideration is higher than the open market value and the supplier does not have a full right of
deduction.

5.4 Returnable Packaging

In case of goods which are supplied in returnable packaging, the value of the goods and the value of the
packaging should be reflected separately on the fiscal document, in case they are subject to different VAT
rates. In this respect, the taxable person has the option not to impose VAT on the value of the packaging upon
its supply and its return, If however, the packaging is not returned, VAT is due on the value of the packaging.

6. VAT rates

6.1 Standard rate

The standard VAT rate in Greece is currently 24%.

6.2 Reduced rate

‘The reduced rate is 13% and is applied to certain goods and services that are specifically stated in the VAT
law. This rate is applied to, for example:

* certain food products;

+ the supply of water;

+ helmets for motorcycles;

* hotel accommodation; and

* restaurant services (except alcoholic beverages).

A super-reduced rate of 6% is applicable, among others, to:
* certain pharmaceutical products;

+ books;

* newspapers;

+ magazines;

+ theatre tickets; and

+ electricity and natural gas.

A second super reduced rate of 4% applies to services for adjusting public buildings or private ones serving
the public interest to the needs of persons with disabilities.

The above rates are reduced by 30% (i.e. 16%, 9% 4% and 3% respectively) when they apply to transactions
occurring in the Aegean islands of Leros, Lesbos, Samos, Kos, Chios. The reduction applies for as long as
there are immigration centers in these islands, The reduction does not apply to means of transport and tobacco
products.

Greece | 259

--- Page 76 ---
Te Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with right to deduction (zero rate)

Greek VAT law provides for a zero rate on certain transactions while at Lhe sume time the deduction of the

input VAT incurred for these transactions is allowed. These transactions include amongst others:

+ exports and intra-Community supplies;

+ supplies and hiring of certain categories of vessels and aircrafts, supplies of goods for the fuelling and
provisioning thereof, supplies of goods incorporated or used therein, and certain related services:

+ supply of goods under diplomatic and consular arrangements, supply of goods and services to
international bodies and their members;

+ transport of persons by sea or air from Greece to abroad and vice versa;

* services related to exports of goods;

+ — supplies of goods under suspending customs regimes or VAT warehousing arrangements;

+ supplies of goods and services to taxable persons, European and international organizations, embassies
and charitable organizations, which further donate them to specific public or non-profit entities to cover
refugees’ needs;

+ supplies of goods and services to taxable persons, which further donate them to specific public bodies and
entities to cover the needs of affected persons from extreme natural phenomena; and

+ supplies of goods and services to a donor, who is using such supplies in the context of a donation
agreement with a public sector body, provided the donation agreement is ratified by law or approved by
the Minister of Finance and the competent Minister as the case may be, depending on the type of donation
and the value of such supplies does not exceed the amount of the donation,

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

In relation to intra~Community supplies conducted by Greek suppliers, the latter should possess the
documents prescribed in Council Implementing Regulation 2018/1912, in order to substantiate the exemption
(zero rate). The Greek VAT Act does not require an invoice when goods are transferred to another EU
Member State. However, in practice, taxable persons issue pro-forma invoices.

7.1.2 Export supplies

In regards to exports conducted by Greek suppliers, in order to substantiate the exemption (zero rate), the

latter must have:

+ the customs export declaration;

+ the sales invoice;

+  aprintout of the electronic message of the customs office of export confirming the completion of the
respective export (i.e. message with code number “IE599”); and

+ bank documents verifying that the consideration has been paid.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Greek VAT law provides for the operation of VAT warehouses. In this respect, an exemption from VAT is
provided for the supplies and intra-Community acquisitions of goods that are intended to be placed in VAT
warehouses and the supply of related services, as well as the supplies of goods and related services when the
goods remain in VAT warehouses. VAT warehouses are operated by persons who are specifically licensed

for such operations. Furthermore, persons other than the above operators may be licensed to receive goods
that are intended to be placed in a VAT warehouse. There are many types of goods that can be placed in a
VAT warehouse (see also Annex V of the EU VAT Directive) which are specifically stated in Greek VAT law.
Further types of goods may be eligible by a decision of the Greek Minister of Finance. There are various
procedural and bookkeeping obligations that need to be kept for the proper placement of the goods in the VAT
warehouse and the proper application of the VAT exemption.

VAT becomes payable at the time the goods are released from the VAT warehouse. The operator of the
warehouse or the taxable person who has placed the goods in the warehouse is liable to pay the VAT due. If
the release of the goods from the warehouse is made by another taxable person, then this person is liable for
the VAT due. The operator of the warehouse is jointly liable for payment of the VAT.

260 | Greece

--- Page 77 ---
7.1.3.2 Special VAT warehouses for non-EU and domestic goods

Greek law provides for a special VAT warehousing scheme allowing the suspension of VAT on non-EU and
domestic goods that are intended for export, zero-rated intra-Community supply or supply to vessels and
aircrafts (to the extent such supply qualifies for an exemption from Greek VAT). The application of the said
scheme, which allows the suspension of VAT on imported goods that are stored in the VAT warehouse and
the domestic sale of stored goods requires, among others, the issuance of a special license in the name of the
warehouse keeper and the payment of an amount of EUR 600 (corresponding to administrative expenses for
the audit of the VAT warehouses) on an annual basis. Furthermore, a financial guarantee of at least 30% of
the amount of VAT that corresponds to the value of the stored goods is required. It is noted that the afore-
mentioned special warehousing scheme does not apply to excise goods.

7.1.3.3 Excise warehouses

There are no special warehouses for excise goods. Excise goods may be stored in a bonded warehouse
(paragraph 7.1.3.4).

7.1.3.4 Bonded (customs) warehouse

Greek VAT law provides for the operation of bonded (customs) warehouses, among others for goods subject
to excise duties. There are various types of such warehouses for the operation of which a relevant license

is required by the customs authorities. Such a license is granted upon provision, among other things, of a
guarantee by the operator of the warehouse. For the placement of the goods in the warehouse, a customs
declaration is filed with the proper customs authorities. The proper operation of the bonded (customs)
warehouse is closely monitored by the customs authorities and provided that such operation is compliant with
all customs requirements, the sale of goods in a bonded warehouse is zero-rated.

Furthermore, the supply of services that are closely related to goods that are placed in bonded warehouses is
exempt from VAT.

7.2 Exemptions without right to deduction

Greek VAT law provides for the exemption of certain supplies which are specifically enumerated therein,
without entitling the person supplying them to deduct the input VAT incurred for these supplies.

Exemptions in the public interest

More specifically, the following are such exemptions:
the supply of hospital and medical care, and diagnostic services and the supply of goods and services
closely related thereto, undertaken by State bodies. The services provided by thermal bath and spring
facilities are also included. The exemption in question applies also to private entities, provided that they
are non-profit and such exemption does not cause distortion of competition;

+ medical care supplied in the exercise of the medical professions, as well as the supply of services of
psychologists, midwives, nurses and physiotherapists, speech and occupational therapists;

+ services supplied by dental technicians and the supply of dental prostheses by dentists and dental
technicians;

+ services supplied by unions or associations of persons to their members, whose activities are exempt from
or outside the scope of VAT, to the extent these services are directly necessary for the exercise of their
activity and are provided against contribution to the joint expenses;

+ the supply of services and goods closely linked to welfare and social security, as well as to the protection
of children and young persons by legal persons governed by public law or other organizations or
institutions recognized by the State;

+ the transportation of sick or injured people with vehicles especially designed or adapted for this purpose,
under the condition that a relevant approval has been provided by the proper public authority;

+ the provision of education in general and the supply of goods and services closely related thereto by
public educational entities or by other persons that have been recognized by the proper authority; and

+ the supply of services closely linked to sport or physical education by non-profit legal persons to persons
taking part in sport or physical education.

7.3 Option for taxation

In relation to all exempt transactions above, the option for taxation exists only in relation to commercial
leases of buildings. To this end, the lessor must submit a relevant application to the tax office prior to entering
into the lease agreement, when it concerns a new building or at any time if the building is not new. The
standard VAT rate applies to leases that are subject to VAT.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charge

In Greece there is the obligatory reverse charge rule for general B2B services provided by non-resident
suppliers to taxable persons and legal entities with a Greek VAT ID number established in Greece. This
reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and follows from Art. 44 and Art. 196 EU VAT Directive. The
reverse charge applies also in Greece for the supply of natural gas and electricity supplied by non-resident
suppliers to “taxable dealers”,

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)

In case a non-resident supplier effects taxable supplies of goods or services in Greece, the reverse charge
mechanism is not applicable and the non-resident supplier needs to VAT register, regardless of whether the
customer is a Greek or foreign taxable or non-taxable person. However, if the non-resident supplier does not
VAT register, then the customer becomes liable to account for the Greek VAT due, provided that the customer
is established in Greece and he is a taxable person. Namely, although the non-resident supplier is required to
VAT register in the first place, in case he does not do so, the reverse charge mechanism becomes applicable
as a consequence, The application of the reverse charge mechanism in such a case is obligatory and concerns
all transactions for which the non-resident supplier should have VAT registered in Greece and did not do so.
In case however the non-resident supplier has not VAT registered and therefore the reverse charge becomes
applicable, as per the practice of Greek tax authorities, he will not be able to recover any VAT incurred in
Greece through the procedure of Directive 2008/9/EC.

In addition to the above, the reverse charge mechanism is also applicable in case of a domestic supply of
goods that have been imported in Greece by a supplier which holds an import VAT deferral license (or

belongs to a group that holds such license), as explained in paragraph 2.3.2.1 above. In such case, the invoice
of the supplier must make the following reference: “Application of reverse charge mechanism. Decision POL
1126/2013. Import VAT deferral license No

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism — specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

Greek VAT law provides for the application of the reverse charge mechanism to domestic transactions in the

following instances:

+ supply of used material, used material which cannot be re-used in the same state, scrap, industrial and
non-industrial waste, recyclable waste and incompletely processed waste;

+ transfer of greenhouse gas emission allowances;

+ domestic supply of goods imported by persons holding an import VAT deferral license referred to in
paragraph 2.3.2.1 above. See also paragraph 8.1.1;

* technical projects performed for Greek public authorities and State-governed organizations, which are
taxable persons with the right to deduct input VAT, with the exception of major projects falling within the
scope of article 100 of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council of
17 December 2013 and article 39 of Council Regulation (EC) No 1083/2006 i.e. projects funded by the
European Regional Development Fund and the Cohesion Fund for which the total eligible cost exceeds
EUR 50,000,000 and in the case of projects promoting sustainable transport and removing bottlenecks in
key network infrastructures where the total eligible cost exceeds EUR 75,000,000;

+ the supply of goods and services to taxable persons established in Greece whose activity consists of the
transport of natural gas through pipelines, to the extent that such goods/services are exclusively intended
for the construction of the said pipelines; and

+ the supply of mobile phones, game consoles, tablets and laptops to taxable persons established in Greece
who have the right to deduct input VAT.

Greece | 263

--- Page 80 ---
8.2 Joint and several liability

Jointly liable for VAT debts are the executive presidents, directors, general directors, administrators,
managing directors and liquidators and any other persons having managing pawers, provided that:

+ the above persons had the above capacities either during the operation of the legal entity or at the time of
its dissolution, termination or merger, or during its liquidation;

* the debts became overdue during their office; and

«the debt was created due to the fault of the above persons.

In case of VAT assessments made following an audit, jointly liable are the persons for which the first and third
of the above conditions are fulfilled, in the fiscal year to which the VAT assessment refers.

For non-Greek taxable persons who are VAT registered in Greece, through the appointment of a fiscal
representative, the latler is jointly liable for the VAT debts of the foreign taxable person.

9 Recovery of input VAT

o1 Non-recoverable input VAT

Input VAT is not recoverable in case of

+ purchase, importation or intra~Community acquisition of tobacco products;

+ purchase, importation or intra-Community acquisition of spirits or alcoholic beverages, provided that the
aforementioned goods are used for the purposes of non-taxable transactions;

* reception, entertainment and hospitality expenses;

+ accommodation, food, beverage, transport and entertainment expenses for the staff or the representatives
of a company; and

+ purchase, importation or intra-Community acquisition of private use passenger cars up to 9 seats,
motorcycles and motorbikes, vessels and aircrafts that are used for recreational or sports purposes and
expenses in relation to petrol, repair, maintenance, leasing and circulation thereof in general (VAT is
deductible, however, when the aforementioned transport means are intended for sale, leasing or transport
of persons against a fare).

92 Excess input VAT

Taxable persons who are registered in Greece for VAT purposes are entitled to apply for the refund of any
excess input VAT at any time, including immediately after the filing of the VAT return in which the relevant
excess input VAT has been declared.

Following the filing of the VAT refund claim by the taxable person, the tax administration performs a risk

analysis, in order to determine if the refund claims for which the refund should be made without a prior audit

and those refund claims for which an audit should be performed prior to the refund. Furthermore, for amounts

of refund exceeding EUR 300,000, the prior approval of the Minister or Deputy Minister of Finance is

required. The following taxable persons are considered to raise low risk and therefore their refund claims are

processed with priority and usually without prior audit:

+ taxable persons who have an Authorised Economic Operator license; and

+ taxable persons who perform zero rated transactions and-or services subject to the reverse charge which
turnover exceeds 50% of the total turnover.

In any case, according to the Greek Tax Procedures Code, if the refund of taxes, including VAT, is made after
90 days from the filing of the refund claim, the taxable person is entitled to interest.

9.3 Adjustment periods

+ immovable property - 3 years following the year of first use;
+ movable property - 5 years following the year of first use.

264 | Greece

--- Page 81 ---
However, in the case of immovable property which is leased and for such lease the option has been exercised
to be subject to VAT, the adjustment period is 10 years from the year of first use.

Certain services consisting in the right to use patents, drawings, templates, trademarks and similar rights also
qualify as capital goods according to Greek VAT law, to the extent they are used for more than one accounting
period.

94 VAT refund to EU taxable persons

‘Non-resident EU taxable persons may apply for the refund of VAT incurred in Greece under the conditions
and requirements of Directive 2008/9/EC. More specifically, the said taxable persons may file a relevant
refund claim with the designated website of the tax authorities of their country of establishment. The latter
will forward electronically the application to the Greek Independent Authority for Public Revenue, which
notifies the applicant electronically of the date of receipt of the refund claim.

‘The VAT refund claim must be accompanied by an electronic file (of maximum size SMb), containing
scanned copies of the relevant invoices, with a minimum value of EUR 250 for invoices concerning fuel
expenses and EUR 1,000 for invoices concerning all other expenses.

The aforementioned refund claims of EU taxable persons must be filed by 30 September of the calendar year
that follows the refund period (which shall not be more than one calendar year or less than 3 calendar months;
it may be less than 3 months only if it concerns the remainder of a calendar year). VAT is not refunded in case
of claims that are submitted after the lapse of the deadline, In case of refund claims filed for a period of one
calendar year, the requested VAT cannot be lower than EUR 50. In case of refund claims filed for a period of
less than one calendar year, the requested VAT cannot be lower than EUR 400.

In the event the refund claim is duly filed, the Greek Ministry of Finance must issue a decision within a
deadline of 4 months, starting from the date the claim is received. The said deadline may be extended under
conditions but, in any case, it cannot exceed 8 months from the date of receipt of the claim. Subsequently,
in the event the refund claim is approved, the refund must take place before a deadline of 10 working days,
starting from the expiration of the deadline set for the Greek Independent Authority for Public Revenue to
decide on the claim.

In case the refund claim of an EU applicant is rejected, the latter may file an administrative appeal for the
re-examination of its claim. The said appeal must be filed with the Dispute Resolution Directorate of the
Independent Authority for Public Revenue within 60 days after the notification of the rejection, or after the
deadline for the issuance of a written decision on the claim, as the case may be. The Directorate should issue
a decision within 120 days after the filing of the said appeal. In case of a rejecting decision, or if no written
decision is issued before the deadline, the EU taxable person is entitled to file a judicial appeal (before the
administrative courts) within a 90 day period starting from the notification of the rejecting decision or from
the lapse of the deadline for the issuance of a decision.

Finally, see paragraph 9.1 for the Greek VAT which is not refundable.

95 VAT refund to non-EU taxable persons

In the case of non-resident non-EU taxable persons, the refund of VAT incurred in Greece is also possible to
the extent the reciprocity principle is fulfilled. Until now, the Greek Ministry of Finance has issued circulars
explicitly providing that the VAT is refunded to taxable persons established in Switzerland, Norway and

the UK. In practice VAT may be refunded to taxable persons in any other country as well, provided that the
reciprocity principle is fulfilled. To this end, a relevant (hard copy) application must be filed with the Greek
Ministry of Finance. The application must be accompanied by the original invoices and a certificate of the
competent authorities of the applicant’s country of establishment, stating that the latter is a taxable person for
VAT purposes in that country.

In the event the refund claim is filed by another person, a Power of Attorney for his appointment must also be
submitted together with the refund claim.

Greece | 265

--- Page 82 ---
‘The refund claims of non-resident non-EU taxable persons, must be filed by 30 September of the calendar
year that follows the refund period (which shall not be more than one calendar year or less than 3 calendar
months). It may be less than 3 months only if it concerns the remainder of a calendar year. VAT is not
refunded in case of claims that are submitted after the lapse of the deadline.

In the event the refund claim is duly filed, the Greek Ministry of Finance must issue a relevant decision
within a deadline of 4 months, starting from the date the claim was received. The said deadline may be
extended under conditions but, in any case, it cannot exceed 8 months from the date of receipt of the claim.
Subsequently, in the event the refund claim is approved, the refund must take place within a period of 10
working days, starting from the expiration of the deadline set for the Greck Ministry of Finance to decide on
the claim.

the re-examination of its claim. The said appeal must be filed with the Dispute Resolution Directorate of the

deadline for the issuance of a written decision on the claim. The Directorate should issue a decision within
120 days after the filing of said appeal. In case of a rejecting decision, or if no written decision is issued
before the deadline, the non-EU applicant is entitled to file a judicial appeal (before the administrative courts)
within a 90-day period starting from the notification of the rejecting decision or from the lapse of the deadline
for the issuance of a decision.

Finally, see paragraph 9.1 for the Greek VAT which is not refundable.

10. Admi

istrative obligations

10.1 Invoicing

10.1.1 Invoice content

Greek tax law does not provide for the issuance of separate commercial and VAT invoices but requires the

issuance of a single invoice. Invoices issued in Greece must mention the following details:

+ the date of issuance of the invoice;

+ the sequential number of the invoice;

+ the full details of the supplier and the customer (name or trade name, address, tax registration (VAT)
number);

+ the type and quantity of the goods sold or the description of the services rendered;

+ the measurement unit as well as the price per unit;

+ the date when the supply of the respective goods or services was realized or completed, to the extent such
date does not coincide with the date of issue of the invoice;

+ the price of the goods or the service remuneration per VAT rate or per VAT exemption;

* discounts or rebates granted;

+ the applicable VAT rate;

+ the VAT amount payable, unless a special VAT scheme is applicable, according to which such reference is
not required;

+ where the invoice is issued by the recipient of the respective goods or services the reference “self-billing”
should be inserted (in Greek “Avt6-tyLoAdynoy”);

+ where the customer is liable to pay the VAT due (by application of the reverse charge mechanism) the
reference “reverse charge” should be inserted (in Greek “Avtiotpogn emBépvvon”);

+ where a VAT exemption is applicable, reference should be made to the relevant provision of Greek VAT
law or the EU VAT Directive providing for such exemption; for instance for intra-community supplies
the reference in Greek is “AmaAAGooetai and MTIA Gp8po 33 tov Kade PIA” while for exports the
reference in Greek is “AzakAécoerar and PITA dépOpo 29 tov Kaduca DILA”;

+ where a special VAT scheme is applicable, reference should be made on the invoice that this scheme
is applicable; for instance, the reference in Greek for travel agents scheme is “KaBectig nepiupiov-
TakiSuotuxé mpaxtopeta” and for second hand goods is “KaGeotac nepWapiov- Metazerpicpéve oad”;

266 | Greece

--- Page 83 ---
+ incase of intra-Community supply of a new means of transport, reference should be made to the
characteristics according to which the means of transport qualifies as new; for instance, in case of a
vessel, the date of such vessel’s entry into service and the hours it has sailed;

+ in the event the invoice is issued by a foreign entity in respect of taxable transactions in Greece for which
itis liable for Greek VAT, a reference must be made to the VAT number that has been acquired in Greece;
and

+ when a fiscal representative is liable for VAT, the invoice must mention the full details and tax registration
number of the representative.

The invoices can be issued in any language, but the tax authorities have the right to ask for a translation.
‘Therefore, an invoice issued by a Greek company could be in English and, therefore contain (e.g.) the
reference “reverse charge” in English.

10.1.2 Time limits

The time limits for the issuance of invoices depend on the type of transactions that are being invoiced.
Indicatively:

* in the event ofa supply of goods or services, the relevant invoice must be issued by the 15 day of the month
that follows the supply, the dispatch of the goods or the completion of the services, as the case may be; and

+ inthe event of a supply of goods or services that is continuous, the invoice is issued by the 15" day of the
month that follows the date when part of the consideration becomes due.

10.1.3 Currency
Invoices may be issued in any currency, however the amount of VAT due must be expressed in EUR.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate
Greek tax law refers to Article 91 of the EU VAT Directive, as regards the conversion to EUR of the amount of

VAT expressed on an invoice in a different currency. In this respect, the exchange rate used is that published by
the Central European Bank and no notification needs to be made to this end by the taxable person.

10.1.5 Self-billing
Under Greek tax legislation, self-billing is allowed, provided that a relevant agreement is in place between the
2 parties, which may be proved by any suitable means. In the event of self-billing, the relevant invoices to be
issued must fulfil all the conditions that apply to regular invoices according to Greek legislation.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ the date of the issuance of the invoice;

+ the full details of the supplier;

+ description of the goods or services supplied; and

+ the amount of VAT due or the information necessary for its calculation.

The simplified invoice is allowed to be issued when the amount invoiced does not exceed EUR 100.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Accounting books and fiscal documents, including invoices, must be retained for a period of at least 6 years
from the year of reference. However, in cases specifically provided in the law, the period may be extended
e.g. when information has been requested from another country or if the taxable person files an amending tax
return in the last year of the retention period.

If tax litigation is pending before the administrative courts, the accounting books and fiscal documents must
be maintained throughout the period during which the tax litigation is pending and one year after the issuance
of the irrevocable court decision.

Invoices should be stored in paper or in electronic form. Invoices may be stored in Greece or abroad. In any
case, the taxable person has the requirement to give access to the stored invoices to the tax authorities, within
the deadlines set by the tax authorities and without unreasonable delay.

Greece | 267

--- Page 84 ---
10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Electronic invoicing is allowed under Greek lax law, provided that the authenticity of the origin, integrity of
the content and the readability of the invoices are guaranteed by any suitable means. The use of electronic
invoices is subject to the acceptance of the customer.

10.2 VAT compliance

10.2.1. VAT return period

Greek established taxable persons that maintain double entry accounting books are required to file VAT
returns monthly. Greek established taxable persons that maintain single entry (revenues-expenses) accounting
books, are required to file VAT returns quarterly. Foreign taxable persons that are VAT registered in Greece
are also required to file VAT returns quarterly. VAT returns may only be filed electronically and should be
filed regardless of whether there is VAT payable, credit VAT balance, or the returns are nil.

10.2.2 Filing of a VAT return and payment

VAT returns should be filed by the last working day of the month that follows the respective VAT return
period, as defined in paragraph 10.2.1 above.

VAT due can be paid in lump sum by the last working day of the month when the VAT return should be filed.
Alternatively, to the extent the amount of VAT due exceeds EUR 100, it can be paid in 2 equal instalments.
‘The first one must be paid by the last working day of the month of filing of the VAT return and the second one
by the last working day of the following month. The website through which VAT returns are filed is:
Attps://tinyurl.com/4294ttdd

10.2.3 European sales listing

The European sales listing must be submitted on a monthly basis, by the 26% day of the following month,
only however, for those months in which intra-Community transactions have taken place and regardless of the
value of the intra-Community transactions. More specifically, the European sales listing is filed to declare the
realization of:

+ intra-Community supplies of goods;

+ intra-Community acquisitions of goods;

+ — supply of (reverse charge) services to taxable persons established in different EU Member States to the
extent that the services provided are subject to VAT in the EU Member State of the recipient; and

+ receipt of (reverse charge) services from taxable persons established in different EU Member States to the
extent that such services are subject to VAT in Greece.

The website for the filing of the European sales listing is: huips://tinyurl.com/4294ttdd
10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

Taxable persons are not required to file annual lists of their suppliers and customers.
10.2.5 Tax assessment period

For VAT purposes, the tax assessment period is in principle 6 years (starting from the end of the year of
reference). By way of exception from the above rule, the said period may bc extended up to 11 years, as the
case may be, when special conditions apply.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration
Late registration triggers a penalty of EUR 100.
10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

The late filing of VAT retums, when no VAT is payable, triggers a fixed penalty of EUR 100. This fixed
penalty is also applicable in case of late filing of the European sales listing.

268 | Greece

--- Page 85 ---
In case VAT is payable and the VAT return is filed with delay, then a fixed penalty of EUR 250 or EUR S00 is
imposed, depending on the type of accounting books maintained.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

If the VAT is not paid on time to the tax authorities, then interest is calculated on the amount of the VAT due
from the next day following the deadline for the payment of the VAT. The interest rate is 8.76% annually
(0.73% monthly).

10.3.4 Default penalties for VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly
In case an assessment is made by the tax authorities in the context of an audit, and the taxable person is found

to have filed inaccurate VAT returns and as a result he has paid less VAT than the VAT actually due or he has
deducted or received a refund for a higher amount of VAT, a penalty of 50% of the difference is imposed.

Furthermore, the interest mentioned in paragraph 10.3.3 is also applicable.

In case an audit has been initiated, until the 10" day from the issuance of the preliminary audit report, the
taxable person has the right to file VAT returns (for the first time or amending to previously filed ones). In
such case the above penalty is reduced by 50 % thereof, while the interest mentioned in paragraph 10.3.3 is
also applicable.

In case the taxpayer accepts the VAT assessment after the final audit report has been issued and before the
deadline expires for the filing of an administrative appeal, the penalty is reduced by 40%. In case the taxpayer
accepts the VAT assessment after the issuance of a decision on the administrative appeal (or the expiration

of the deadline for such issuance) and before the expiration of the deadline for filing a judicial appeal, the
penalty is reduced by 30%, whereas if the acceptance is made after the filing of the judicial appeal and before
the first hearing set, the penalty is reduced by 25%.

10.3.5 Offence penalties

In case of non-filing of a VAT return, where the VAT return that was not filed would give rise to the obligation
for payment of an amount of VAT (i) a penalty equal to 50% of such amount or (ii) a fixed penalty of EUR
250 or EUR 500 (depending on the type of accounting books maintained) is applicable, whichever of the two
is higher, Also the interest mentioned in paragraph 10.3.3 is applicable.

In case of failure to issue fiscal documents or issuance/receipt of inaccurate fiscal documents with respect to
transactions that ate subject to VAT, a penalty is imposed equal to 50% of the corresponding VAT or of the
respective difference. This penalty cannot be lower, per tax audit, than EUR 250 or EUR 500 (depending on
the type of accounting books maintained).

10.4 Intrastat

Intrastat returns are filed on a monthly basis, by the 26" day of the following month, however only for the
months in which intra-Community supplies or acquisitions have taken place (including distance sales) and
to the extent that the value of such transactions exceeds the thresholds mentioned below in paragraph 10.4.1,
either in the previous year or within the year of reference. Such returns are filed electronically with the
Hellenic Statistical Authority (EL.STAT),

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
The Intrastat threshold for 2025 is EUR 200,000 for arrivals and EUR 90,000 for dispatches.
10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

Intrastat returns should include the following information in relation to the arrivals and dispatches:
+ the BU Member State of departure/destination;

+ the nature of the transaction;

+ the mode of transport;

+ the 8-digit combined nomenclature (CN) code of reported goods;

* net weight of reported goods;

Greece | 269

--- Page 86 ---
* invoiced value of the goods;
statistical value of the goods;

+ the VAT number of the customer; and
+ the country of origin.

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

Taxable persons who are subject to local accounting requirements have to provide VAT and accounting
information to the myDATA public portal. The myDATA system is an ¢-accounting system which also
includes reporting obligations to be fulfilled either periodically or on a real-time basis. It requires taxable
persons to provide a set of data to a public platform, which are then used to maintain online tax ledgers.
This system covers all transactions (B2B, B2C and B2G) and both domestic and international transactions.
‘Taxable persons with a turnover lower than EUR 30,000 or issuing less than 50 invoices per year are able to
manually upload invoice data on the myDATA portal.

i. Taxable Persons

Ll Registration threshold & SME scheme for domestic taxable persons

Taxable persons carrying out business activities must register for taxes in Hungary. There is no threshold for
registration. The general tax number is used as a VAT number except in intra~Community transactions, where
an additional EU VAT identification number must be shown.

Once a company, ic. taxable person, is registered at the Court of Registry, it receives its tax number
automatically. Nevertheless, a registration form detailing its tax status must be filed with the tax office local to
the domicile of the company. The EU VAT identification number must be requested on this form.

The possibility for a VAT exemption exists for small businesses if their supplies are under the threshold of
HUF 18 million (approximately EUR 45,375)', however, they still must register for VAT purposes.

‘The registration forms can be downloaded at: hitps://tinpurl.com/yhkuyf2z

1.2 Registration threshold & SME scheme for foreign taxable persons

Foreign taxable persons must register for ‘all taxes’ in Hungary. It is not possible to register for VAT only.
However, it is possible to indicate on the registration form if the taxable person is not liable for corporate
income tax or it is not liable for VAT. There is no registration threshold for foreign taxable persons that need
to register for VAT purposes in Hungary.

When registering, the following information must be provided:

+ aregistration form;

= accompany registration document not older than 30 days issued by the competent foreign authority, for
example company house (original or copy, no need to notarize);

+ evidence of registration as a taxpayer issued by the competent foreign authority: the original foreign tax
registration confirmation;

+ specimen signature or notarized copy of the passport (if it includes the signature) of the signatory officer.
Specimen signature must be 1) original, 2) notarized and 3) of the foreign company’s signatory officer
who is authorized to represent the Company as per the company’s registration document.

Only EU-based taxable persons or those that have a fixed establishment in the EU, can request direct
registration in Hungary. Non-EU taxable persons are required to appoint a fiscal representative when
registering for taxes (see also paragraph 1.4).

Foreign taxable persons should submit their registration form at a special tax office: NAV Kiemelt Ad6z6k

From 1 January 2025, the so-called SME scheme (Special Scheme for Small Enterprises) was introduced,
which allows taxable persons established in the EU to opt for an exemption in a Member State where they
are not established. ‘The application of the scheme is subject to certain conditions, by registering only in the
Member State of establishment. A taxable person who has established its business in another EU member

For information about the rules on cross-border exemption, please visit the NAV website (nay.gov.bu) at
