Accordingly, the foreign taxable person may opt for the exemption (known as individual exemption) in Hungary
if, both in the current and the preceding calendar year, his consolidated turnover does not exceed the EU thresh-
old of EUR 100 000 and his domestic turnover does not exceed the domestic threshold of HUF 18 000 000.

A taxable person who opts for the individual exemption (SME scheme) has an obligation to declare and pay

tax in cases where he cannot act as an exempt person. This is the case, for example, where:

+ he sells goods used as capital goods in his business;

+ permanently transfers intangible assets or rights used in his business;

+ he supplies new means of transport to another EU Member State;

+ he sells new immovable property under construction or new immovable property, or building plot, even if
not considered as capital assets;

+ certain free of charge supplies, provided that the input tax relating thereto is wholly or partly deductible;

+ for certain cross-border transactions subject to the reverse charge;

+ he charges VAT on the invoice despite being tax exempt;

+ he is liable as a guarantor for the payment of the tax in the event of non-payment of the consideration;

+ for intra-Community acquisitions of goods with a value exceeding ZUR 10,000 (or you may opt to pay
below this threshold), and for intra-Community acquisitions of excisc goods and new means of transport
irrespective of the threshold; or

+ he imports goods.

In addition to the above-mentioned supplies, certain VAT exempt and transactions that fall under a special
agricultural scheme do not count for the individual exemption threshold.

13 VAT grouping

Taxable persons:

* who (that) have established their business inside the domestic territory or, in the absence of such a place
of business, have their permanent address or they usually reside inside the domestic territory; and

+ who (that) are considered affiliated,

may together enter into a group taxation arrangement.

A group taxation arrangement may be established subject to the state tax authority’s permission, granted upon,

an application submitted in writing and containing the express and unanimous understanding of all members

concerned. The group taxation arrangement is not available to any taxable person who holds membership

in another group taxation arrangement or has submitted an application to establish another group taxation

arrangement, where this application is pending the definitive decision.

“Affiliated company” is defined in the Corporate Income tax law. The Corporate Income law defines affiliated

company as follows:

+ the taxpayer and the person in which the taxpayer has a majority control - whether directly or indirectly -
according to the provisions of the Civil Code;

+ the taxpayer and the person that has majority control in the taxpayer - whether directly or indirectly -
according to the provisions of the Civil Code;

+ the taxpayer and another person if a third party has majority control in both the taxpayer and such other
person - whether directly or indirectly - according to the provisions of the Civil Code, where any close

272 | Hungary

--- Page 89 ---
relative holding a majority control in the taxpayer and the other person shall be recognized as third
parties;

+ anon-resident taxable person and his domestic business establishments, furthermore, the domestic place
of business of a non-resident taxable person and the person who maintains the relationship defined under
Paragraphs a)-c) with the non-resident taxable person;

+ the taxpayer and its foreign business establishment, and the taxpayer’s foreign business establishment and
the person who maintains the relationship defined under Paragraphs a)-c) with the taxpayer;

+ the taxpayer and another person if between them dominating influence is exercised relating to business
and financial policy having regard to the equivalence of management.

For the purposes of the VAT grouping, taxable persons shall not be treated collectively as affiliated
companies if the sole reason for their being recognized as affiliated is that the Hungarian State or a municipal
government has majority control in them, whether directly or indirectly, as a third party in accordance with
the Act on the Civil Code (hereinafter referred to as Civil Code). Municipal governments and any company
in which a municipal government has majority control, whether directly or indirectly, in accordance with the
Civil Code shall not be collectively recognized as affiliated.

‘The members of a VAT group keep their own tax numbers. However, they will receive one common separate
VAT number for the group. The members of a VAT group do not charge VAT on their supplies to other
members of the same VAT group. A VAT group files one VAT return monthly. The members are jointly and
severally liable for payment of the VAT debts of the group.

14 Fiscal representative

Taxable persons from EU Member States do not have to appoint a fiscal representative but they can do it
voluntarily. Taxable persons from non-EU countries are obliged to appoint a fiscal representative in order to
register and carry out business in Hungary. A fiscal representative should have a share capital of HUF 150
million (approximately EUR 384,000) and must have no fiscal debt. The fiscal representative must perform
every tax obligation on behalf of the foreign taxable person. He is jointly and severally liable for these
liabilities.

2 Taxable transaction

21 Transfer of business

‘The transfer of a line of business (transfer of a going concern) is considered to be out of the scope of VAT,
provided that the acquirer purchases the line of business for the purpose of carrying on the business and

both the transferor and transferee are VAT registered in Hungary and parties do not have a status that can
jeopardise the right of VAT deduction (for example they are subject to the SME scheme). The transferee
should undertake joint and several liability within the period of limitation. ‘Line of business’ shall mean an
existing unit of a company that is capable to function independently and to carry out economic activities on a
long term using its own assets.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

Supply of goods for consideration also means where a taxable person permanently disposes of goods forming
part of his business assets - without any compensation - for his own or his employees’ private use, more
generally, for purposes other than those of his business, or if supplied to others free of charge, where the VAT
on such goods or on its component was wholly or partly deductible by the taxable person.

Some other transactions are also considered to be taxable supplies even if made without consideration, if the

related input VAT was deductible, for example:

+ the application by a taxable person for the purposes of his business of goods produced, constructed,
extracted, processed, purchased or imported in the course of such business, where the VAT on such goods,
had they been acquired from another taxable person, would not be wholly deductible;

Hungary | 273

--- Page 90 ---
+ the application of goods by a taxable person for the purposes of a non-taxable area of activity, where the
VAT on such goods became wholly or partly deductible upon their acquisition or upon their application in
accordance with point.

The supply of goods for consideration shall not cover:

+ charitable donations; and

+ the supply of samples of goods and goods of small value free of charge by the taxable person for his
business purposes.

“Samples of goods” shall mean any component part or small quantity of a product that carries specific
characteristics of that product, whose sole purpose is to demonstrate the product in question, and that is not
suitable for long-term use or for other objectives due to its physical substance or value.

“Small value” shall mean anything of value worth less than HUF 5,000, where the value is to be determined
relying on the open market value of the goods provided - inclusive of VAT - prevailing at the time when
provided.

“Charitable donation” shall mean goods and services made available to public-benefit organizations for
supporting their activities performed in the public interest provided for in the relevant legislation, or to
educational establishments supporting their activities specified in the charter document, and, furthermore,
to the ecclesiastical legal entities that satisfy the requirements to support the activities described therein,
provided that:

* the true contents of the documents pertaining to the transaction in question and the actual circumstances
of such transaction confers sufficient proof that such action does not serve or only seemingly serves the
public purpose or public interest, the activities of educational establishments specified in the charter
document, or the objective defined in the relevant legislation in the case of ecclesiastical legal entities;

* such donation does not result in any financial advantage for the donor, its members (shareholders), its
executive officers, any member of its board of directors or board of supervisors, its auditor, or close
relatives of such persons or private individual members (shareholders), whereby any reference to the
name or to the activities of the donor shall not be regarded as financial advantage; and

* the donor has a certificate made out by the public-benefit organization, educational establishment or the
ecclesiastical legal entity that contains the name of the donor and the recipient, their registered address
or home address, tax number, or in the absence of a tax number their unique identifier and the objective
supported.

Supply of services for consideration also means where a taxable person temporarily disposes of goods
forming part of his business assets - without any compensation - for his own or his employees’ private use,
more generally, for purposes other than those of his business, or if transferred to others free of charge, where
the VAT on such goods, or on its component, was wholly or partly deductible by the taxable person. Supply of
services for consideration shall also mean the supply of services carried out free of charge by a taxable person
for his private use or for that of his staff or, more generally, for purposes other than those of his business,
where the VAT on such services was wholly or partly deductible by the taxable person.

Supply of services for consideration shall not cover the provision of charitable donations. Charitable
donations are out of scope of VAT. However, the definition of such donations is very restricted.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

It is possible to apply an exemption on the importation of goods into Hungary if it is followed by an intra-

‘Community supply. Certain conditions must be met in order to apply the exemption (so-called “Procedure 42”):

+ the goods must be transported out of the country within 30 days (this deadline can be extended by 30
days);

+ asecurity must be provided;

274 | Hungary

--- Page 91 ---
+ the goods may not be used in Hungary; and
+ the importer or its representative has a VAT registration number.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

Generally, import VAT is levied by HTA in Hungary. Under the general rules import VAT is due at the time of
the import. This import VAT is deductible in the VAT period of the import.

Import VAT deferment license. Under special circumstances taxable persons can apply for a license to avoid
payment of VAT at the time of importation but pay and reclaim import VAT in the Hungarian VAT retum.

‘The conditions for applying the deferment of VAT upon importation are as follows:

+ the applicant is registered for tax (VAT) in Hungary;

+ the applicant has a clean record with the customs authorities; and

+ the volume of the exempt sale of goods with the right to recover VAT (for instance in case of exports or
intra-Community supplies) is at least 67% of the total supplies in the year but with a minimum of HUF
10 billion (approximately EUR 25,200,000); or

+ the volume of the tax exempt sale of goods in the current year is at least HUF 20 billion (approximately
EUR 50,400,000); or

+ the applicant has obtained an authorized economic operator (AEO) license or is authorized to apply the
simplified procedure.

Import through an indirect customs representative. If the importer appoints an indirect customs
representative, the latter will become liable to pay import VAT. If the indirect customs representative has an
import VAT deferment license, he pays import VAT in his own VAT return (deferred import VAT payment).

Subject to further conditions, the importer can also assign the right to deduct import VAT to the indirect
customs representative. In this case the indirect customs representative with the deferment license can pay
and recover import VAT in his own VAT return even if the importer does not have the import VAT deferment
license. From | March 2025 the conditions for the indirect customs representative being able to self-assess
and deduct import VAT became stricter and include the requirement of reliable taxpayer status for the indirect
customs representative and reliable or non-risky taxpayer status for the importer. If the importer is not
considered risky, but also not considered a reliable taxpayer, the indirect customs representative must perform
additional checks on the importer as prescribed by the HTA.

HTA grants businesses “reliable taxpayer status” after three years of tax compliant operation in Hungary.
Certain non-compliant activity may result in the “risky taxpayer” status.

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

The following joint conditions must be fulfilled in order to avoid the VAT registration of a foreign supplier in

Hungary:

+ both the supplier and the intended acquirer are taxable persons;

+ the goods are transported from one EU Member State to another with a view to being supplied there at a
later stage and after arrival to an intended acquirer;

+ the supplier has not established his business nor does he have a fixed establishment in the EU Member
State to which the goods are dispatched;

+ the supplier records the dispatch of the goods in its register and indicates the VAT identification number of
the intended acquirer in his European sales listing;

+ the intended acquirer is identified for VAT purposes in the EU Member State to which the goods are
transferred;

+ the intended acquirer’s identity and VAT identification number are known by the supplier at the time when
dispatch begins;

Hungary | 275

--- Page 92 ---
+ the intended acquirer is required to keep regularly updated quantitative records on the goods intended to
be sold to him from the call-off stock and indicate the VAT identification number of the supplier in his
European sales listing; and

+ asa time limit, the goods can remain no longer than 12 months under the call-off stock arrangements.

By applying the simplification, the supply of the foreign taxable person should be treated as an intra-
Community supply (without being followed by a domestic sale in Hungary thal would otherwise trigger the
need for VAT registration), while the purchase by the Hungarian customer is to be considered as an intra-
Community acquisition.

3.2 Installation supplies

The supply of goods with installation is treated as supplied where the goods are assembled or installed. In
Hungary, there is no formal definition of the notion ‘supply with installation or assembly’.

On the supply of goods with installation or assembly, the reverse charge may apply if the supplier is not
established and the customer is registered for VAT purposes in Hungary (see also paragraph 8.1.1).

33 Distance sales threshold

Distance selling refers to the sale of goods to private individuals in another EU Member State, where the
goods are transported from one Member State to another by or on behalf of the supplier. Hungary has a EUR
10,000 annual threshold for distance sales.

To simplify VAT compliance, businesses can use the One-Stop Shop (OSS) scheme. By registering for OSS
in one EU Member State, businesses can report and pay VAT on all their EU distance sales through a single
return, avoiding the need to register in multiple Member States. Once the threshold is exceeded or if the
business opts into the OSS scheme, the VAT rate applied is the rate of the Member State where the goods are
delivered. If distance sales to a Member State do not exceed the threshold, the supplier charges VAT at their
‘own country’s rate, unless they choose to opt into the OSS scheme.

OSS returns must be submitted on a quarterly basis. OSS filing should be done in Hungarian Forints (HUF),
The ECB exchange rate valid on the last day of the quarter should be used. Payments must be made in HUF
too.

Under the special arrangement for postal and courier services, the postal operator will declare and pay the
amount of VAT collected monthly. Postal operators making use of the special arrangements will be subject to
record-keeping obligations. The records to be held by them will contain all the taxable transactions, including
cases where the consignment was rejected by the customer. Under this simplified import VAT payment scheme,
a flat rate of 27% is applied to all cases, regardless of the actual VAT rate applicable to the imported goods.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

The triangulation simplification allows for the party participating in the intra-Community chain supply
of goods to be exempted from the obligation to register for VAT in Hungary and charge Hungarian VAT.
The simplification usually involves three parties, party A (supplier who dispatches the goods), party B
Gniemnediary party) and party C (party buying goods from B).
The transaction must involve parties VAT registered in different EU member states;
+ The goods must be transported directly from A to C either by A or by B (in which case B must not make
use of its VAT number of country A if they have one there too);
+B must not have an establishment in the country where transport ends;
+B purchases goods for the purposes of reselling them to C; and
+ C must use the VAT ID of the country where transport ends.

Although it is rare, triangulation simplification also applies to chain transactions with four or more parties,
provided that the supply with intra-Community transport is the one before the last supply, i.e. in case of
ABCD, the B-C supply must be the supply with transport.

276 | Hungary

--- Page 93 ---
3.5 Acquisition threshold

‘The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons engaged in exempt
activities and flat-rate farmers from suppliers established in another EU Member State is EUR 10,000.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment
Hungary has implemented the following effective use and enjoyment rule,
The place of supply of short- and long-term B2B and B2C rental of means of transport (including railway

coaches) by a non-EU service provider to a person in Hungary, or by a Hungarian service provider to a non-
EU person, is where the effective use and enjoyment takes place.

4. Chargeable event and chargeability

41 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods
or services are supplied.

4.2 Partia{ fulfillment and advance payments

If the supply of goods or services is divisible and the supply is delivered partially, the date of the partial
fulfillment becomes the date of supply.

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the service is
completed, provided that the payer has the legal obligation to pay the money before the delivery and pays an
amount which is or will become part of the consideration. This is the case when the price has been totally or
partially received, before the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied. To the
extent that any such payment is received in advance, an ‘actual’ tax point arises.

43 Continuous supplies

If the parties agreed in a periodical settlement or payment or the consideration of the transaction is assessed
for a certain period (together ‘periodic supply of goods and services’), the VAT shall be assessed (paid and
declared) based on the specific rules of periodical settlement. Examples of periodic supplies include situations
where the fee is determined over the period of time, e.g. monthly, quarterly, annual subscription or other fees,
rents, etc. and situations where the settlement is done with respect of supplies made in a specific period, for
example, “all bread rolls baked by the bakery for the supermarket in March”.

In this case, the tax point date shall be determined based on the following special rules:

+ the iast day of the period, if none of the special rules described in the next points applies;

+ the invoice date, if the invoice date as well as the due date is before the last day of the invoicing period;

+ the due date, if the due date is later than the last day of the invoicing period, if the due date is not later
than the 60%day following the last day of the invoicing period; or

+ the 60 day following the last day of the period, if the due date is later than the 60" day following the last
day of the invoicing period.

4.4 Cash accounting

A taxable person may opt for the cash accounting scheme provided that the below conditions are fully met
This means that VAT becomes payable by the supplier only when the respective payment was effected by the
customer. This also means that the recipient of the goods/services may only deduct the VAT charged in the
invoice if the financial settlement has been completed.

Small-sized businesses according to the terms of the respective act, may only opt for the cash accounting if
they fulfil the following conditions:

Hungary | 277

--- Page 94 ---
+ the place of establishment is in Hungary (for lack of such, the domicile or habitation);

* do not fall under liquidation or voluntary dissolution procedures;

* did not opt for the tax exemption; and

* the net value of sales revenue on the supply of services and goods did not exceed HUF 125 million in the
previous year nor is likely to exceed this threshold in the given year. When calculating this threshold, not
only domestic but all transactions must be considered.

5) Taxable amount

SA Bad debt relief

Bad debt relief is available in Hungary for VAT purposes under certain strict conditions.

The taxable amount may be reduced subsequently by the amount of consideration in whole or in part,

exclusive of VAT, shown under bad debts, under the following cumulative conditions:

+ the taxable person and the person to whom the goods or services are supplied are acting as independent
parties;

+ the taxable person notifies the (axable person to whom the goods and/or services are supplied in writing
in advance, except if the person to whom the goods and/or services are supplied dissolved without
succession;

+ the taxable person is not adjudicated in bankruptcy, liquidation or involuntary de-registration proceedings
at the time the tax return affected by the bad debt is submitted;

+ at least one year has lapsed since the due date of the payment of consideration for the supply of good and/
or services underlying the accounts claimed as bad debts, except where the term of limitation for civil
claims relating to supplies of goods and/or services underlying the accounts claimed as bad debts is less
than one year; and

+ the consideration payable for the supply of good or services underlying the accounts claimed as bad debts
was not and cannot be recovered otherwise.

The advance written notice to be sent to the supplier shall include at least the following information:

+ the reason for accounting the consideration charged as bad debt;

+ the serial number of the invoice where the consideration is unpaid;

* amount of the bad debt (showing separately the amount of consideration or part of the consideration and
the amount of VAT charged); and

* a statement that the taxable person will subsequently deduct the amount of consideration referred to in
Paragraph c), exclusive of VAT, from the taxable amount.

Enclosed with the tax return, the taxable person shall disclose the reason for accounting the amount as bad
debt to the tax authority using the form prescribed for that purpose. The form must include the number

of the invoice for the supply of goods or services on which the taxable amount is reduced, the amount of
deduction, the name and tax number of the taxable person to whom the goods or services are supplied, and
an explanation why the consideration payable for the supply of the goods or services claimed as bad debt was
not and cannot be recovered otherwise.

If the supplier of goods or services recovers the consideration accounted as bad debt in whole or in part after
it was deducted from the taxable amount, the taxable amount may not be reduced subsequently by up to the
amount of consideration or part consideration recovered, exclusive of VAT. Enclosed with the tax return the
taxable person shall disclose the amount of the consideration or part of the consideration recovered to the tax
authority using the form prescribed for that purpose, exclusive of VAT, including the name and tax number of
the taxable person to whom the goods or services were supplied.

Where the consideration for the supply of goods and/or services is written off as bad debt, and the person

to whom the goods and/or services are supplied is not a taxable person, unless otherwise provided for, VAT
amount reduction shall be applicable where the taxable amount is reduced

278 | Hungary

--- Page 95 ---
5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
27% VAT EUR 270
Total to be paid EUR 1,270

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier must pay EUR 264,60 in its VAT return.
The customer can deduct EUR 264,60 in its VAT return. Therefore, the supplier has to issue a ‘modified
invoice’

5.3 Open Market Value

In lieu of the consideration, the open market value shall be applied as the taxable amount if the supply of

goods or services takes place between non-independent parties:

+ where the consideration charged is unreasonably higher than the open market value of the goods or
services in question and the supplier does not have a full right of deduction;

+ where the consideration charged is unreasonably lower than the open market value of the goods or
services in question and the recipient of the supply does not have a full right of deduction; or

+ where the consideration charged is unreasonably lower than the open market value of the goods or
services in question when the supplier does not have a full right of deduction and the supply is subject to
an exemption.

‘This rule shall not apply where the consideration is determined by a binding statutory provision, or if the
consideration is to be determined on the basis of the relevant legal regulations.

54 Returnable packaging

As regards the costs of returnable packing material, Hungary includes them in the taxable amount and take
the measures necessary to ensure that this amount is adjusted if the packing material is in fact retuned (VAT
base reduced).

Special rules apply to certain packing materials such as plastic bottles, where there is a prefixed packaging
fee

times a week).

Tt Exemptions

WW Exemptions with the right to deduct VAT

Hungary applies an exemption with the recovery of input VAT for the following supplies and services:

* — exports and intra~Community supplies of goods;

+ goods supplied to travelers resident outside the EU under retail export schemes or through tax-free shops;

+ services in the framework of inward processing on behalf of principals resident outside the EU;

* _ services of intermediaries relating to transactions carried out outside the EU;

* sea vessels and aircraft and related goods and services;

+ supplies made to customers with (diplomatic) tax immunity;

* supplies of monetary gold to central banks;

+ transport of goods to and from Madeira and the Azores as well as the transport of goods between those
islands;

* goods under customs control or VAT warehousing arrangements;

+ international passenger transport by seagoing vessel or airplane;

* provisioning with foodstuffs of seagoing vessels, with the exception of vessels for inshore fishing;

* — services to goods under customs control; and

+ services provided in a VAT warehouse.

TAL Intra~Community supplies

‘To qualify as VAT exempt, intra-Community supplies must be accompanied by proof thal the goods have

left Hungary. For the exemption to apply, a transport document or other credible proof (e.g. warehouse

documents, customer declarations) or documents described in the EU VAT Implementing Regulation must be

available. Suppliers must retain: 2 documents from list A; or 1 from list A plus | from list B:

+ List A: Signed CMR road transport document; Bill of Lading; Airfreight invoice; courier invoice.

+ List B: Insurance policy for the transport of the goods; official documentary proof or arrival of the goods;
or receipt by operator of receiving warehouse in country of arrival

If the customer picks up the goods, the supplier may apply the exemption if he is in possession of the

following:

* a written statement from the customer, stating that the goods have been dispatched or transported by the
cusomer, or by a third party on behalf of the customer, and identifying the Member State of destination of
the goods; that written statement shall state: the date of issue; the name and address of the acquirer; the
quantity and nature of the goods; the date and place of the arrival of the goods; in the case of the supply
of means of transport, the identification number of the means of transport; and the identification of the
individual accepting the goods on behalf of the acquirer; and

+ at least two items of non-contradictory evidence referred to above

‘The customer shall furnish the vendor with the written statement referred above by the tenth day of the month
following the supply.

7.1.2 Export supplies

To qualify as VAT exempt, exports supplies must be accompanied by proof that the goods have left Hungary
(within 90 days).

280 | Hungary

--- Page 97 ---
For export supplies, in order to apply the tax exemption, a certificate from the authority that exited the
territory of the Community is required, which authentically certifies the fact of the exit of the product from
the territory of the Community (“Confirmation of Exit”).

If the goods do not leave the customs border within 90 days but all other conditions of an export are fulfilled,
the exporter can reduce the VAT paid on the sale, if the product leaves the customs territory within 360 days.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Hungary has introduced the VAT warehouse. Goods from non-EU countries (i.e. customs goods) and excise
goods cannot enter the VAT warehouse. The sale of goods is VAT exempt if the goods are transported to a
VAT warehouse or are Jocated in a VAT warehouse. The intra-Community acquisition of goods in a VAT
warehouse is also exempt.

A guarantee or a deposit must be provided for the operation of a VAT warehouse. Export of goods from the
warehouse must be proven by customs documents. The supply of goods to other EU Member States must
be proven by transport documents. If there is lack of sufficient proof, the tax office will withdraw the VAT
amount from the guarantee or deposit

The supplier does not have to be registered in Hungary for the purpose of a supply of the goods stored in a
VAT warehouse to an EU Member State. The condition is that the supplier enters into an agreement with the
warehouse keeper whereby the EU VAT identification number of the latter is used for the intra~-Community
supply.

7.1.3.2 Bonded (customs) warehouse

Goods which have not been cleared for free circulation can be stored in bonded (customs) warehouses. Sales
with respect to goods located within a bonded warehouse are exempt. Also, services performed on the goods
are exempt from VAT. Import VAT is payable upon taking the goods out of the bonded warehouse, the owner
of those goods is liable for the VAT.

72 Exemptions without right to deduction

7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest

Postal services, certain hospital services, medical and paramedical care, cultural activities, ambulance
services, services rendered by undertakers, educational services, services of a cultural or social nature, some
radio and audio-visual services, services of non-profit organizations, some services connected with sports, etc.
are exempt of VAT.

Most of these exemptions, however, are granted on detailed conditions relating to the legal status or license of
the supplier, the purpose of the activity of the supplier, and many other conditions.

7.2.2 Other exemptions

Other exemptions are financial and banking services, insurance services, management of collective
investment funds, certain games of chance, postal and telecommunication services, non-commercial activities
of public radio and television broadcasting organizations, letting of immovable property, the supply of
immovable property, services by employers or employees’ organizations, or by political, religious, charitable
organizations, etc.

Some of these exemptions are granted on certain conditions.

7.3 Option for taxation

Regarding the supply and letting of certain immovable property (defined by the VAT law) , the supplier may
opt for taxation for VAT purposes. This requires notification to the tax authorities. Opting for VAT taxation
may, depending on the taxpayer’s decision, pertain to all types of immovable properties that are being let

or only toa restricted scope of such properties: immovable properties other than residential buildings. If

Hungary | 281

--- Page 98 ---
a taxable person chooses either of these options, it remains binding until the end of the S® calendar year
following the year when the choice is made.

The supplier may limit the option of taxation regarding the sale of all of his immovable properties or all
lettings of them. The option is not available regarding single transactions. However, it is possible to decide
differently regarding residential properties and other properties.

Itis not possible to opt for financial services.
8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charge

In Hungary there is the reverse charge for general B2B services provided by non-established taxable to
Hungarian VAT registered taxable persons in Hungary. This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States
and follows from Articles 44 and 196 EU VAT Directive.

For the reverse charge to apply

+ the supplier should not be established in Hungary (they could be VAT registered),

+ the recipient must be VAT registered in Hungary (it is sufficient to be registered, no need to be
established).

If neither the supplier nor the recipient is VAT registered, the reverse charge is not applicable, and the supplier
should register for VAT.

8.1.1 General reverse charge - non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)

Domestic reverse charge applies in the case of a supply of goods with installation or assembly and services
of which the place of supply is Hungary by a non-established supplier. Hungary did not introduce a reverse
charge for the supply of goods other than the supply of goods with installation or assembly and the supplies
mentioned in chapter 8.3.

For the reverse charge to apply

+ the supplier should not be established in Hungary (they could be VAT registered); and

+ the recipient must be VAT registered in Hungary (it is sufficient to be registered, no need to be
established).

If neither the supplier nor the recipient is VAT registered, the reverse charge is not applicable, and the supplier
should register for VAT.

The following supplies of goods fall under the reverse charge mechanism in Hungary if the place of the
supply is deemed to be in Hungary and the supplier is not established in the Hungary, but the recipient is
registered for VAT purposes in Hungary:
supply ofa newly built immovable property subject to registration in the land registry;
+ supply of goods with installation or assembly:
* supply of gas through a natural gas system situated within the territory of the Community or any network
connected to such a system, the supply of heat or cooling energy through heating or cooling networks,
and the supply of electricity.

In case of the supply of goods with installation or assembly, the reverse charge is applicable if the supplier
is not established in Hungary but the customer is registered for VAT purposes in Hungary. On the invoice,
reference must be made to the application of the reverse charge. The supplier must use the following text on
the invoice: “forditott adézés” (translation: reverse charge).

282 | Hungary

--- Page 99 ---
The following services fall under the reverse charge rule in Hungary if the place of the supply is deemed to be
in Hungary and the supplier is not established in Hungary, but the recipient is registered for VAT purposes in
Hungary:

* services relating to immovable property;

+ transport of passengers;

+ admission to cultural, artistic, scientific and similar events;

* — short-term hiring of vehicles; and

* — services of restaurants.

‘The supplier must use the following text on the invoice: “forditott adéz4s” (translation: reverse charge).

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism - specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

On the following supplies and/or services reverse charge rules apply if both the supplier and the customer are

VAT registered in Hungary:

+ supply of a building;

+ certain supply of construction services, including repair, cleaning, maintenance, alteration and demolition
services in relation to immovable property;

+ supply of staff engaged in certain construction related supplies;

+ supply of immovable property with an option for taxation;

+ supply of old used materials, scrap, waste;

+ supply of goods provided as security by one taxable person to another in execution of that security;

+ supply of an asset with a market value exceeding HUF 100,000 (approximately EUR 250) under an
insolvency or similar procedure;

+ sale of greenhouse gases (CO2 Emission Rights);

+ sale of certain agricultural products by a taxable person not falling under the special rules of the flat-rate
scheme for farmers; and

+ certain iron and steel products which are listed in Annex 6/A of the VAT Law.

The supplier must use the following text on the invoice: “forditott adézés” (translation: reverse charge).

8.2 di nt and several liability

Joint and several liability applies in the following cases:
+ VAT warehouse operator for the inventory loss and for unresolved tax obligations; and
+ onward supply of goods or services (including recharges) between VAT registered affiliated parties.

9. Recovery of input VAT

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

The input VAT is not recoverable on:

* passenger cars, petrol, other fuel for passenger cars, products for passenger cars;

* — services necessary to operate passenger cars (up to 50%);

* motorcycles exceeding 125 cc, yachts and similar sports or entertainment vehicles;
* restaurants and catering- food and drinks, entertainment;

+ construction/building or purchase of housing real estate;

+ taxi, parking, tolls;

+ telecommunication services (up to 30%); and

+ services related to the maintenance and the operation of passenger cars (50%).

VAT is generally recoverable on the above-listed products and services if they are purchased for the purpose
of reselling or recharging.

Hungary | 283

--- Page 100 ---
9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT is generally recoverable and refunded by the VAT authorities. Llowever, if the refund
does not exceed certain amounts, the refund is carried forward. This amount differs depending on the actual
frequency of VAT reporting:

+ HUF | million (approximately EUR 2,500); for monthly VAT filers;

+ HUF 250,000 (approximately EUR 640); for quarterly VAT filers; and

+ HUF 50,000 (approximately EUR 130); for yearly VAT filers.

Excess input VAT is refunded by the tax office within 20 to 75 days depending on the status of the taxpayer
(reliable, risky or general), the amount of the refundable VAT and whether the purchase invoice has been settled.

9.3 Capital goods - adjustment periods

+ immovable property - 20 years following the month of first use, and
+ movable property - 5 years following the month of first use.

9.4 VAT refund for foreigners

9.4.1 VAT refund to EU taxable persons

The refund application must be submitted electronically through the portal of the tax authority of the country
in which the claimant is established. The claim can be submitted in Hungarian, English, French or German.

Annual minimum limit (for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year) is
EUR 50. Interim minimum limit (for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least 3 months)
is EUR 400,

Minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year, unless it is the
remainder of the year. Maximum period for making a claim is 1 calendar year. Deadline for making the claim
is 30 September of the year following the year over which the claim is made. No application is accepted after
the deadline.

Supporting documents (e.g. copies of invoices) are required in the following cases:
+ the taxable base regarding fuel exceeds the equivalent of EUR 250; and
+ the taxable base regarding other supplies exceeds the equivalent of EUR 1,000.

The tax authorities must issue a decision on the refund claim within 4 months of receipt of the claim. The
period in which the authorities must make a decision will be extended to 6 months or maximally to 8 months
if the authorities request additional information. The tax authorities usually ask for a proof of payment from
the bank that the supplies were paid by the claimant, the contracts or agreements/orders and a description of
what the business purpose of the purchase was.

Ifa refund is granted, it will be processed within 10 business days and paid to the bank account number
provided to the tax authorities. Time limit for appeals is 15 days following the decision. If this appeal is
unsuccessful, the claimant can initiate proceedings before the national court (within 30 days after a receipt of
the decision on appeal).

The input VAT is not recoverable in case of:

+ supplies of goods and services that are not used for business purposes;

+ supplies and services acquired or imported in connection with an exempt business activity;

+ supplies of goods and services where the VAT law regulates the non-recoverable VAT (see paragraph 9.1);
+ VAT charged erroneously or incorrectly; and

9.4.2 VAT refund to non-EU taxable persons

VAT is refunded to non-EU taxable persons if the reciprocity condition is fulfilled in this matter. Switzertand,
Liechtenstein, Norway, Serbia, Turkey (with some limitations) and the UK fulfill the criteria of such
reciprocity with Hungary,

Annual minimum limit (for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year) is
EUR 50. Interim minimum limit (for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least 3 months)
is EUR 400.

Minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year, unless it is the
remainder of the year. Maximum period for making a claim is 1 calendar year.

Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoicing

10.1.1 Invoice content

Invoices shall contain the following information:

the date of issue;

a sequential number, which uniquely identifies the invoice;

the tax number under which the taxable person supplied the goods or services;

the customer’s following information:

* tax number, under which the customer received a supply of goods or services in respect of which he is
liable for payment of value added tax, or

+ tax number, under which the customer received a supply of goods as referred to in Section 89, or

* the first eight digits of the tax number - or group identification number in connection with a group
taxation arrangement - under which the taxable person registered in the domestic territory received
a supply of goods or services, provided that the supplier of the goods and/or services has established
his business in the domestic territory, or in the absence of such a place of business, has his permanent
address or usually resides in the domestic territory;

the full name and address of the taxable person and of the customer;

description of the goods supplied, and - at the discretion of the taxable person required to issue the invoice

- the relevant tariff heading used in this Act, furthermore, the quantity of the goods or the description

of the services rendered, and ~ at the discretion of the taxable person required to issue the invoice - the

relevant TESZOR’15 code, used in this Act, furthermore, the extent and nature of the services rendered, if

it can be expressed in some unit of measurement;

the date referred to in Paragraphs a) and b) of Subsection (1) of Section 163, if other than the date of issue

of the invoice;

the words “pénzforgalmi elsz4molis” (“cash accounting”) when using the special taxation scheme defined

under Chapter XIII/A:

the taxable amount, the unit price of goods exclusive of VAT or the unit price of services exclusive of

VAT, if it can be expressed in some unit of measurement, and any discounts or rebates if they are not

included in the unit price;

the VAT rate applied;

the VAT amount payable, except where, in accordance with this Act, such a detail is excluded;

the words “énsz4mlazas” (“self-billing”) where the customer receiving a supply of goods or services

issues the invoice;

in the case of an exemption, reference to the applicable provision of law or to the VAT Directive, or any

other reference indicating that the supply of goods or services is exempt;

the words “forditott adézis” (“reverse charge procedure”) where the customer is liable for payment of

VAT;

in the case of the supply of a new means of transport made in accordance with the conditions specificd

in Section 89, the characteristics as identified in Point 25 of Section 259 pertaining to the new means of

transport;

in connection with the activities of tour operators governed under the special provisions of Chapter XV,

the words “kiilénbézet szerinti szabalyozas - utazdsi irodak” (“margin scheme - travel agents”);

where one of the special arrangements applicable to second-hand goods, works of art, collectors”

items and antiques is applied under the special provisions of Chapter XVI, the words “kiilénbézet

szerinti szabalyozas - hasznalt cikkek” (“margin scheme - second-hand goods”); “kiilénbézet szerinti

szabalyozas - miéalkotésok” (“margin scheme - works of art’) or “killénbézet szerinti szabalyozas -~

gylljteménydarabok és régiségek” (“margin scheme - collector’s items and antiques”) respectively; and

where a financial representative is involved, the name, address and tax number of the financial

representative.

Hungary has introduced the real-time reporting obligation as of | July 2018. The invoices must be issued
by qualified invoicing software that is capable of communicating with the HTA by electronic means in
a pre-defined way. This means that businesses are required to register their invoicing software and the

286 | Hungary

--- Page 103 ---
communication termination point and all data communication between the company and the HTA is done via
this electronic communication protocol. The taxable persons issuing such invoices will be obliged to report
them immediately (within 5 minutes from the moment the invoice is generated in the invoicing software),
without human intervention electronically to the HTA in a predefined way in xml format.

Affected transactions: all supplies of goods and services, either B2B or B2C, including advance payments
related to these supplies, except distant sales reportable in OSS subject to VAT in another EU member
state. This includes supplies of goods or services subject to reverse charge, exempt supplies such as intra-
Community or exports.

Affected suppliers: VAT registered businesses (this includes non-established VAT registered foreign
businesses as well as taxable persons subject to the exemption for SMEs).

Failure to comply with this obligation may result in a significant penalty of up to HUF 500,000
(approximately EUR 1,300) per each undeclared invoice.

10.1.2. Time limits

An invoice must be issued not later than on the 8" day after the day on which the goods were supplied or
services were rendered.

10.1.3 Currency

The invoice can be issued in any currency. However, the VAT amount in Hungarian Forint (HUF) must be
shown on the invoice.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

The VAT base and VAT amount should be converted to Hungarian Forint on the day when the VAT becomes
due or on the day of the supply. The conversion can be made according to the daily exchange rate of one of
the banks established in Hungary, or, if the taxable person opts for it, the Hungarian National Bank (HNB)
or the European Central Bank (ECB). The taxable person must report to the tax authority if it opted for using
the exchange rates of the HNB or the ECB. The same exchange rate source must be used for calculating the
taxable base of all transactions. The taxable person may not change its decision before the end of the second
calendar year following the year of the change.

10.1.5 Self-billing

Under Hungarian VAT legislation, self-billing is allowed if there is an agreement between the two parties (for
instance a contract or a written agreement).

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice must contain the same information as a normal invoice with the exception of the
following:

consideration should include the total amount payable without breaking it down to net amount and VAT
amount, for example “the total amount payable HUF 100”

VAT rate embedded in the total payable amount, i.e. calculated from the top, in case of the 27 % rate, is 21,26
%, but the VAT amount may be omitted on the simplified invoice.

Simplified invoicing is applicable only in certain cases listed in the VAT Act. The gross amount should not
exceed EUR 100,

The taxable person can opt to issue receipts instead of invoices if its customer pays the consideration,
inclusive of VAT, by the time of supply at latest in full, without requesting an invoice from the taxable person.

Receipts must contain the following details:

+ the date of issue;

+ a sequential number, which uniquely identifies the receipt;

+ name, address and VAT number of the taxable person issuing the receipt; and
+ the total sum payable for the supply of goods or services, inclusive of VAT.

Hungary | 287

--- Page 104 ---
10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices need to be kept available for the period until the right to claim the VAT elapses. It is usually +1
years, ITowever, in the case of self-corrections the deadline starts from the day of the correction

‘The invoices must be kept in original or as certified copies. Electronic invoices must be kept electronically.
Paper invoices may also be kept electronically.

The place of storage of the invoices is not restricted, but it must be reported to the tax office if the place of
storage is not the seat of the company. The documents must be made available within 3 days if requested by
the tax authorities.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

The authenticity, the integrity of data content and the readability of the electronic invoices must be ensured.
All business procedures that provide a reliable audit trail between the invoice and the supply are sufficient
for this purpose. Advanced electronic signature and electronic data interchange (EDI) are mentioned in the
law as examples. The recipient of the invoice must consent to the electronic invoicing. The supplier and the
recipient of the invoice issued with EDI must conclude an agreement about the method of applying and using
the EDI. It is not required for the electronic invoice to be structured, i.e. pdf invoice is acceptable if it fulfills
the requirements mentioned above. Electronic invoices must be issued from the software capable of real time
reporting to the HTA.

10.2 VAT compliance

10.2.1. VAT return period

The standard VAT return is to be filed every quarter. The VAT return must be submitted monthly if the

net payable VAT exceeded HUE | million (approximately EUR 2,500) in the second year preceding the

year in question. Also, if during the current year, the net payable VAT in a quarter exceeds HUF I million
(approximately EUR 2,500), the taxpayer must change to monthly filing, A yearly VAT return must be filed

if the net payable VAT did not exceed HUF 250,000 (approximately EUR 640) in the second year before the
year in question, except if the taxpayer has an EU VAT identification number. The yearly filing taxable person
must change to more frequent filing periods whenever it exceeds any of the above-mentioned thresholds
during the year.

Monthly VAT returns must be submitted in the year of VAT registration and in the subsequent year.

In principle, a domestic business is required to submit electronic VAT returns. A foreign company which is
registered only for VAT purposes can submit paper VAT returns if it does not have an EU VAT identification
number,

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment
The VAT due must be received on the bank account of the VAT authorities on or before the 20" day of the

month following the VAT return period (i.e. the VAT due over January 2025 must have been received by the
VAT authorities on 20 February 2025). The same deadline applies to the VAT return.

The Hungarian VAT return (form XX65 where the first two digits represent the last two digits of the current
year) is very complicated and changes every year.

VAT returns can be submitted in the following ways:

+ “Classic” VAT return submission electronically by applying ANYK reporting software to compile and
submit the 65 VAT return form and A60 IC Sales and Purchase Listing;

+ e-VAT system, web based —on the HU Tax Authority’s e-VAT system user interface, One can approve or
modify the draft of the VAT return prepared by the HU Tax Authority; and

+ e-VAT system, the so called Machine to Machine (M2M) interface.

‘The VAT return can be downloaded here:

Access to the ¢-VAT system is here:
https://eafa.nav.gov.hu

Ifthe taxable base or the VAT amount changes after the VAT return has been submitted (for example certain
invoices were not reported or modification invoices were issued) according to the main rule, the respective
VAT return shalll be self-revised. This means that the respective VAT return shalll be re-submitted with the
correct figures. If self-revision results in additional payable VAT, self-revision interest is payable

If the reporting period has been closed by the tax audit, re-opening of such period may be requested from the
HTA, if new facts or circumstances have emerged since the tax audit.

In certain cases self-revision is not necessary. There is no need for self-revision, for example, if the amount
of the deductible input VAT changes (e.g. the taxpayer received a modification invoice from its supplier with
amended input VAT).

10.2.3 European sales listing
Form XXA60 (where the first two digits represent the last two digits of the current year) must be submitted
if the taxable person has performed an intra-Commmnity supply of goods, an intra-Community acquisition of
goods, or if it has supplied or received general B2B services to/from a taxable person established in another
EU Member State (see also paragraph 8.1).

Generally, the form must be submitted with the same frequency as the VAT return. However, even if the
VAT return is submitted quarterly, the form must be filed monthly if the intra-Community supply of products
exceeds EUR 100,000 ina given quarter or in any of the preceding quarters.

Form XXA60 includes separate pages for intra-Community sales, intra~Community acquisitions, incoming
services and supplied services. No threshold exists for the reporting: all transactions must be reported.

The European sales/acquisition/service listing can be downloaded at: https://tinyurl.com/yhkanvf2z

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

‘The national recapitulative statement (also known as itemized report) was introduced on I January 2013
in Hungary. The form is an addition to the VAT return. Its number is XX65A, followed by “M” pages per
customer or supplier.

From 1 July 2020, the previous threshold of HUF 100,000 was eliminated, thus the data of all approved
incoming supplier invoices for which the company intends to exercise the right to deduct VAT must be
provided to NAV on the M-sheet in the domestic recapitulative statement.

On the M-sheet all incoming invoices issued by domestic taxable persons, if the taxpayer exercises the right
to deduct tax in connection with the invoices. It is very important to emphasize that according to the new
tules, the supplier invoices for which the customer of the invoice is required to pay VAT are also included in
the scope of the M-sheet, as he still exercises the right to deduct tax (reverse charge taxation).

Thus, from 1 July 2020, the NAV online invoice report covers all domestic supplies of goods and

services (except for intra-Community supplies), independently on whether there was output VAT or these
transactions were subject to VAT or exempt from VAT. This will enable NAV to prepare draft VAT returns for
taxable persons. The recapitulative statements extend to all supplier invoices of the company so that NAV can
ensure that the companies do exercise the right to deduct VAT based on the invoice issued by another
domestic taxable person and certified to NAV.

The customer is required to include the invoice in the period when the VAT charged on the invoice is
reclaimed by giving the following information:

* the VAT identification number of the supplier/customer;

+ basis and amount of VAT;

+ invoice number: and

+ date of the supply.

Hungary | 289

--- Page 106 ---
The form can be downloaded together with the VAT return form.

Taxpayers filing their VAT return through the e-VAT system are not required to file the domestic recapitulative
statements.
10.2.5 Tax assessment period

‘The tax assessment period in Hungary is 5 calendar years after the year of filing the VAT return, For example,
Q4 2024 is to be filed in January 2025. This means that the assessment period is 31 December 2025 + 5 years=
31 December 2030.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration
There is no penalty for a late registration, however late payment interest may be charged on the delayed VAT
payment,

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

If the VAT return or the European sales listing is not filed on time, the maximum penalty is HUF 500,000
(approximately EUR 1,300). Usually, it is HUF 100,000 (approximately EUR 260) for the first offence.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the VAT authorities

If the VAT is not paid on time, the VAT authorities calculate an interest penalty from the day it should have
been paid until the day it is paid. The interest rate is twice the “prime rate” which is established by the
National Bank of Hungary. The late payment penalty can be calculated here: htips://iinyurl.com/8fmkm3jw
10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

Ifthe VAT is not reported and not paid correctly, or reclaimed incorrectly, the tax authority is entitled to levy a
penalty up to 50% of the underpaid tax. Penally interest (see paragraph 10.3.3) up to 3 years is also due in this
case.

If, before a VAT audit is started by the tax authority, the taxable person self-corrects its VAT liability and pays
the tax and the self-correction interest (if applicable), the tax authority is not entitled to levy tax penalties or
interest fines,

When a self-correction is made by the taxable person, no penalty but a self-correction interest is payable,
which is equal to the “prime rate” established by the National Bank of Hungary.

When an error is detected by the taxable person in any of its earlier VAT returns (back to 5 years), the
correction must be made by the re-filing of that particular VAT return. The interest penalty is payable from the
due date of that VAT return until the actual payment of the difference.

10.3.5 Offence penalties
The penalty can be 200% of the unpaid tax if it is due to gross negligence. If the act was intentional, the case

can incur criminal penalties as well.
10.4 Intrastat

In Hungary, separate Intrastat returns must be submitted to the Central Bureau of Statistics, Kézponti
Statisztikai Hivatal (KSH).

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
From 2025 the data reporting thresholds are as follows:

+ 400 million HUF for arrivals; and
+ 160 million HUF for dispatches.

--- Page 107 ---
A special regulation applies to businesses reporting movement of goods for certain toll manufacturing
transactions. If the business is obliged to report data in one direction, it must also report its turnover in the
other direction — regardless of the threshold.

Businesses whose total value of their inbound reported in Intrastat or ~ in the absence of an Intrastat report
of intra-Community purchases or sales in their VAT retums for a maximum of 12 months exceeds the
applicable special threshold, are also required to complete the “statistical value” section of the questionnaire.

The special thresholds for 2025 are:

* 6 billion HUF for arrivals; and
* 15 billion HUF for dispatches.

Starting from 2025, the following companies are exempted from Intrastat arrival reporting obligation:

* ifthe intra~Community arrivals do not exceed 3.5 billion HUF; and

+ achieved excellent data quality (certification from the Statistical Office) based on the comparison of the
Intrastat arrival report they provided in the previous year and the data received from other Member States
within the framework of the MDE (microdata exchange)

Treland

The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented through the VAT Consolidation Act 2010 and
related VAT Regulations. VAT is referred to in Ireland as “Value Added Tax” or “VAT”.

1; ‘Taxable persons

dd Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

A taxable person is required to register for Irish VAT purposes where its annual turnover exceeds, or is likely
to exceed, the following thresholds:

+ BUR 42,500 for supplies of services;

+ BUR 42,500 in any continuous 12-month period for persons supplying goods liable at the reduced or
standard rates which they have manufactured or produced from zero rated materials;

+ EUR 85,000 for supplies of goods;

+ EUR 85,000 for persons supplying both goods and services where 90% or more of the turnover is derived
from supplies of goods (other than of the kind referred to at (b) above); and

+ EUR 41,000 in any continuous 12-month period for persons making intra-Community acquisitions of
goods into Ireland.

In addition, the following taxable persons are required to register for Irish VAT purposes:

* persons disposing of interests in immovable goods, where the disposal is not exempt from VAT;

+ landowners that allow non-established mobile traders or non-established promoters of events onto
their property for periods of less than 28 days have obligations to report certain information to the Irish
Revenue Commissioners (“Irish Revenue”). Failure to do so can make them accountable persons and
liable for the VAT due by the mobile trader; and

* — persons in receipt of taxable services from suppliers established outside of Ireland;

+ farmers and fisherman who elect to be accountable persons or who haye taxable turnover in excess of the
registration thresholds from other activities.

Taxable persons established for VAT purposes in Ireland are not required to register for Irish VAT if their
level of tumover does not exceed the appropriate threshold. However, they may elect to register for Irish VAT.
Voluntary VAT registration is effected in the same manner as the mandatory VAT registration process.

Please note that in Irish VAT legislation, a taxable person is defined as a person who independently carries
on a business within the EU or elsewhere. An accountable person is a taxable person who engages in the
supply within Ireland of taxable goods or taxable services and is accountable for and liable to pay the tax
charged in respect of such a supply. Legislation deems others to be accountable persons (those engaged in
intra~Community acquisitions, VAT groups, electing to register, etc.). Effectively, an accountable person is
someone who is entitled or required to register for VAT in Ireland.

For information regarding the completion of a registration application and details on what documentation
needs to be provided for VAT registration see also paragraph 1.2.

Irish VAT registration forms can be downloaded at: https.//tinyurl.com/ykzay3vr

Different forms apply to individuals and partnerships and to companies. There are separate forms for non-
established persons and entities see paragraph 1.2.1

1.2 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

Non-established persons supplying taxable goods or services in Ireland are obliged to register and account for
Irish VAT regardless of their level of turnover (i.c. no threshold applies).

The EU VAT changes as regards special schemes for small enterprises have not been implemented into Irish
law. However, Irish Revenue guidance confirms that Irish established traders may make a formal application
to them in Ireland if they wish to avail of the scheme in other EU Member States.

To register for VAT, taxable persons must complete a form TRI for private individuals, partnerships and co-

of the minutes from a Board of Directors meeting (for a company) or a signed letter from an individual/
partnership to evidence their intention to make taxable supplies of the property.

Where a taxable person is registering on the basis of being in receipt of reverse charge services from abroad,

evidence such supply. Non-established traders are generally required to provide evidence of a contract with an
Irish customer to demonstrate the requirement to register.

No other documents are required to be filed with the registration application, unless specifically requested
by the Irish Revenue. Examples of such information would be a certificate of VAT registration in another
Member State and an extract from the trade register to include details of directors and shareholders,

Intending traders are entitled to register for VAT purposes prior to making any taxable supplies. Evidence
of intention to make taxable supplies will be required. Irish VAT registration forms can be downloaded at:

circumstances, the paper-based system must be used. A full list of the exclusions can be found at the above
link.

Irish Revenue introduced a two-tier VAT registration system in June 2019. Traders applying for an Irish
VAT number must specify whether a domestic only VAT number is required or an intra-EU VAT number.

A domestic only VAT number will generally issue in real-time. Additional information is required (ie,

13 VAT grouping
VAT grouping is possible in Ireland.

Taxable persons may apply to be VAT grouped where they are closely bound by economic, organisational
and financial links and where it is necessary or appropriate for the effective and efficient administration
and collection of VAT, It is a condition of VAT grouping that all members be established in Ireland for VAT
purposes.

VAT grouping is optional and a taxable person may choose whether to apply for VAT grouping, where the
conditions are fulfilled. However, it should be noted that Irish Revenue are entitled to put a VAT group in
place between entities that satisfy grouping requirements, although in practice this is relatively rare.

VAT grouping is applied for by submitting completed Form VAT 5? for the remitter and Form VAT 53 for
the other group members (non-remitters) to the remitter’s local Irish Revenue district and an application for
granting of a VAT group is ultimately at the discretion of Irish Revenue. The VAT grouping registration is
made in the name of a representative member known as a ‘VAT group remitter’ who is responsible for filing
the single group VAT return and for making and receiving payments to / from Irish Revenue for the VAT
group. A least one member of the VAT group is required to be an accountable person for Irish VAT purposes.

Members of the VAT group will file one single VAT return in the name of and under the VAT number of the
VAT group remitter. However, invoices for taxable supplies of goods or services supplied by each member
of the VAT group to third party customers should be issued in the name of and under the VAT registration
number of the entity making that supply. Supplies among members of an Irish VAT group are disregarded for
VAT purposes and, therefore, VAT invoices do not need fo be issued. The exception to this is the supply of
immovable goods among VAT group members and the supply of non-deductible items, which would include
food, drink, accommodation, employee personal services, petrol, car hire etc.

VAT group members are held jointly and severally liable for the VAT liabilities of the group for the period

during which they are VAT grouped. This liability for the period of VAT grouping remains even after

members have exited the VAT group. Applications for the amendment or disbandment of a VAT group must

be submitted to Irish Revenue by the VAT group remitter in writing within 30 days of the end of the tax year

in which any of the below listed changes occur:

+ amember of the VAT group ceases to be established in Ireland;

+ there is no longer at least one accountable person present in the VAT group; or

+ there has been a significant change to the financial, economic and organisation links between the VAT
group members.

Irish Revenue may cancel a VAT group registration by issuing notice in writing to each member of the VAT
group with effect from the date specified on such notification.

The Irish VAT grouping provisions allow the inclusion of non-taxable persons in a VAT group. This
position was challenged by the European Commission in the ECJ case of C-85/11 (Ireland v the European
Commission). The Irish provisions were confirmed as being in line with the EU VAT Directive

1.4 Fiseal representative

The concept of fiscal representation has not been transposed into Irish law. Therefore, non-established
taxable persons are neither required nor allowed to appoint a fiscal representative in Ireland. However, an EU
established intermediary must be appointed when seeking to avail of the import one stop shop regime (IOSS).

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

VAT relieving transfer of business provisions have been implemented in Irish VAT legislation. The relief
applies where there is a transfer of ownership of goods to an accountable person of a totality of assets of a
business, or part thereof, which constitute an undertaking capable of being operated on an independent basis.
The relief applies whether the business is trading or has ceased to trade. The transfer of goodwill or other
intangible assets of a business in connection with the transfer of a business, or part thereof, by an accountable
person to a taxable person that carries on a business in Ireland or by a person that is not an accountable person
to another person also qualifies for relief.

Legislation provides that the parties to a business transfer, that falls within the scope of the relief, are entitled
to reclaim VAT on deductible costs that are directly related to the transfer of the goods, where the transfer
would be VATable in the absence of transfer of business relief. Concessionally, the Irish Revenue will allow
VAT recovery on costs related to the transfer of goodwill or intangibles in accordance with the usual VAT
deduction rules. There are some exceptions to this where property is also transferring as part of the business
and the capital goods implications of such transfer should also be considered from an Irish VAT perspective

2.2 Supplies free of charge

‘A supply free of charge shall be deemed to have been effected for consideration in the course or furtherance
of business for Irish VAT purposes except in the following circumstances:

+ agift of goods made in the course or furtherance of business (otherwise than as one gift forming part of a
series of gifts made to the same person) and the cost to the donor does not exceed EUR 20;

+ the gift in reasonable quantity to the actual or potential customer consists of industrial samples which are
nol ordinarily available for sale to the public;

+ advertising goods such as mirrors, glasses, beer mats bearing the donor’s name supplied to a hotel or pub
ete; or

+ replacement goods supplied in accordance with warranties or guarantees on original goods.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

‘The zero-rate of VAT may apply where goods are imported from outside the EU into Ireland and at the
time of importation those goods are already consigned to another EU Member State. On arrival in the other
EU Member State, the arrival of the goods must be treated as an intra-Community acquisition, There are a
number of conditions to be fulfilled:

+ Irish Revenue must be satisfied as to the authenticity of any particular case;

+ the importer must be VAT registered in Ireland;

+ the customer must be VAT registered in the other EU Member State;

+ the importer/agent has certain obligations regarding the onward supply; and

+ the goods must be dispatched within one month of clearance.

2 Postponed accounting on importation

Postponed accounting for VAT on imports applies in Ircland with effect from 1 January 2021. Although

this was introduced as a consequence of Brexit, it applies to all imports from third countries. VAT is to be
accounted for in the VAT3 return (in the T] and T2 boxes) of the trader and applies where the trader is VAT
registered in Ireland. A simultaneous input VAT deduction can be taken where so entitled. A new box has
also been added to the VAT3 return -P AJ wv hich will include the net value of the imports (net value plus
carriage, insurance and freight). Traders who do not qualify for postponed accounting or who no longer
qualify will be notified accordingly by Irish Revenue. If a trader does not wish to use postponed accounting,
import VAT will be payable in the normal manner.

--- Page 113 ---
‘There is a VAT deferment scheme for the importation of goods which allows accountable persons to defer
payment of import VAT to the 15" day of the month following import. The trader should already be approved
for a comprehensive guarantee. An authorisation number is required before the trader can apply for a deferred
payment authorisation. The application for a customs deferred payment authorisation is made using the

Furthermore, goods that are imported into the Shannon Free Zone and Ringaskiddy Free Port may be zero
