13. VAT grouping
Art. 11 of the EU VAT Directive 2006/112 has been implemented in Italy.

As an alternative to VAT grouping, the VAT Law provides for the possibility offered to the companies
belonging to the same group, to compensate VAT liabilities with VAT credits among the companies included
in the VAT group (Italian VAT group settlement).

13.1 VAT grouping
VAT group members must be taxable persons established in the territory of the Italian State.

The possibility to join the VAT group is not allowed for:

* seats and fixed establishments located abroad;

+ entities subject to voluntary liquidation, seizure by order of the court; and

* entities subject to bankruptcy, arrangement before bankruptcy, compulsory administrative liquidation,
extraordinary administration.

In order to join the VAT group, two or more taxable persons established in Italy must be bound to one another
by financial, economic and organizational links. The said conditions must occur jointly.

‘The financial link is determined by the existence, already since the 1* July of the year preceding the one in
which the option is carried out, of shared control, defined as more than 50% of the shares of the members
being directly or indirectly held by the same person(s).

The economic fink is based on the activity carried out and occurs by reason of the same core business, or activities
complementary or interdependent or activities carried out for the benefit of one or more of the other members.

The organizational link is represented by the legal coordination according to the civil law or by the
coordination between the management.

In principle, ifa financial link is present, the other links are presumed to be present unless a tax ruling is filed
with the Tax Authority in order to demonstrate that they do not exist.

The option for the VAT grouping is filed electronically by the representative of the VAT group (i.e, the

controlling company):

+ from | January to 30 September with application of the regime starting with the year following the one in
which the option is filed; or

Italy | 315

--- Page 132 ---
+ from 1 October to 31 December with application of the regime starting with the second year following the
one in which the option is filed.

‘The option requires that the group and its members conform to the above-mentioned conditions in all 3 of the
areas specified.

Failure to conform by the group or by one or more of its members results in the disbanding of the group.

New members, originally excluded due to the lack of conformance to the conditions, join the VAT group as
from the year following the one in which the conditions are met. In this case, the option must be filed within
90 days from the date in which the conditions came into existence.

The option is valid for 3 years and automatically renewed unless it is revoked.

Intra-group transactions are not considered as supplies of goods or services for VAT purposes, while
transactions carried out with third parties by the members are deemed to be carried out by the VAT group.

The members of the VAT group are jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT debts of the group,
penalties and interest.

1.3.2 Italian VAT group settlement

The ‘Italian VAT group settlement’ is an option (not a requirement) that can be put in place by companies
belonging to the same group, if the relevant conditions are met. Specifically, a controlling company and one or
more of its controlled subsidiaries (whereby the holding company must hold more than 50% of the voting rights
of the subsidiary from | July of the taxable year prior to the one in which the grouping takes place) can elect to
compute the amount of VAT due by ‘consolidating’ the monthly VAT amounts of the individual companies.

In other words, in each period, the amount of VAT due will be the net sum of debits and credits of the
companies that are elected to be part of the group. VAT credit carried forward and accrued before joining the
VAT group cannot be used.

The members of a VAT group are not considered a single taxable person. Each member retains its own VAT
registration number and carries out all the fulfilments provided for by the law (such as filing its own VAT
return, issuance of invoices with respect to intra-group transactions as well as with respect to transactions to
non-group members)

A VAT group is formed by the parent company, although there is no requirement to include all eligible
subsidiaries,

Alll the companies belonging to an Italian VAT group are fully, jointly and severally liable for the VAT
payments. The election must be made by the controlling company through the annual VAT return to be filed
in the tax period in which the VAT group settlement applies. The Italian tax authorities approved that an EU
company registered for VAT purposes in Italy can be part of'a VAT group.

‘Pure’ holding companies, not being taxable persons, cannot be a member of the VAT group. ‘Pure’ holding
companies are not considered to be taxable persons, unless the purpose of the possession of shareholdings
is to perform financial activities, to influence the activity of the controlled companies or to coordinate or
intervene in the management of the said companies.

14 Fiscal representative

A fiscal representative is mandatory for non-EU taxable persons when registering for VAT purposes. A fiscal
representative is jointly and severally liable with a client for payment of the VAT and penalties and in general
for any obligation arising from the Italian VAT law.

316 | Italy

--- Page 133 ---
In case of a fiscal representative appointed by a taxable person who is not resident in the EU or in one of the
States adhering to the European Economic Area, the inclusion in the VIES archive is subject to the issuance
of an appropriate guarantee,

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

‘The transfer of a business (or part thereof) is not considered to be a supply of goods subject to VAT.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

‘The VAT Law provides for both free supplies of goods and destination of goods for private use or for
purposes other than business purposes.

In particular, the following operations are deemed to be supplies of goods:

+ the free supplies of goods except those whose production or trade is not within the scope of the enterprise
activity if their unit cost is not higher than € 50 and those for which no tax deduction, as provided for by
Art. 19, has been effected at the time of purchase or import, even if according to the option provided for
by Art. 36-bis; and

+ the use of goods for either private purposes or for other purposes than business purposes by the taxable
person. This also applies if this use is the result of cessation of the activity except for those goods for
which tax has not been deducted at the time of their purchase.

The VAT Law provides for free supply of services too.

In particular, the free supply of services with a value higher than € 50, even if effected for the private use of
the taxable person or free of charge for purposes other than those of his business, is subject to VAT if input
VAT was deducted on the goods and services used for this service free of charge.

The above rule does not apply to the supply of food in canteens and transport, teaching, educational and
recreational services, sanitary and social assistance in favour of the staff, and advertising in favour of
institutional activities of non-profit bodies and for messages spreading, representations, images or public
interest communications requested by the State or by Public Bodies.

2.3 Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

The VAT Law provides that the payment of VAT due on importation of goods released for free circulation

in Italy is suspended when the goods are transferred to another EU Member State. In order to qualify for the

suspension, the importer must provide to Customs the following:

+ VAT identification number;

* VAT identification number of the customer established in another EU Member State; and

+ upon request from the Customs, suitable evidence proving the effective delivery of the same goods to
another EU Member State.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

Italy has not implemented an import VAT postponed accounting scheme. The VAT must be paid at the time of
importation.

Italy | 317

--- Page 134 ---
3: Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

Italy has implemented the simplifications provided for call-off stock both for intra-Community acquisitions
and intra-Community supplies of goods as from | December 2021

If the relevant conditions are met, no intra~-Community acquisition of goods occurs in case of transfer of
goods by a VAT registered taxable person from one EU Member State to Italy in order to be therein supplied,
ata later stage and after arrival, to another taxable person who is entitled to take ownership of those goods in
accordance with an existing agreement between both taxable persons,

3.2 Installation supplies

The supply of goods delivered from another EU Member State to be assembled or installed in the territory
of the State by the supplier or on his behalf is considered a local supply of goods. Italian legislation does not
provide for a formal definition of the notion of ‘supply with installation or assembly’.

In case the customer is a taxable person established in Italy, VAT is due by the latter also in case the non-
resident supplier has an Italian VAT number. For the application of the reverse charge mechanism, see also
chapter 8.

33 Distance sales

The new e-commerce VAT rules in force as from | July 2021 have been implemented in Italy.

‘The distance selling threshold for sales to private consumers is equal to EUR 10,000.
Distance sales of goods as well as electronically supplied services, telecommunication and broadcasting
services have to be included in the said threshold.

Once the threshold is exceeded the supplier may either register directly in Italy or in his country of
establishment via OSS to account for Italian VAT for their distance sales to Italy.

A threshold of EUR 150 per consignment is provided for the application of the special scheme regarding
distance sales of imported goods.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

‘The simplified EU triangulation rules implomented under Italian VAT legislation comply with the EU VAT
Directive.

Taxable persons A, B and Cin the ABC transaction must be registered for VAT in different EU countries (i.e.
in Countries 1, 2 and 3), The goods must be transported directly from A in Country 1 to C in Country 3.

The simplified triangulation rules can be applied if (among other conditions):

+ Italy is the country of arrival of the goods;

+ the middleman (B) is not established in Italy; and

+ the middleman (B) issues an invoice without VAT but referring to a simplified triangulation and the VAT
liability of the last acquirer (C).

Where Italy is the EU Member State of arrival of the goods, the wording of the Italian legislation does not
preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the middleman (B) is VAT registered in Italy or in the
country of dispatch. In principle, the simplification is only applicable to transactions where 3 parties are
involved.

318 | Italy

--- Page 135 ---
35 Acquisition threshold

‘The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons engaged in exempt
activities and flat-rate farmers from suppliers established in another EU Member State is EUR 10,000.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

The effective use and enjoyment rules of Article 59a EU VAT Directive, have been implemented under the
Ttalian VAT Law with respect to the hiring of a means of transport.

In particular, in case of short-term hiring, the place of supply will be Italy if the means of transport is put at
the disposal of:

+ acustomer in Italy and the means of transport is used within the EU; or

* acustomer outside the EU and the means of transport is used in Italy.

The above rule is applicable in the case of any customer (both taxable and non-taxable persons).

In the case of long-term rental of means of transport, the place of supply is Italy for B2C long-term rental of
means of transport other than pleasure boats when rendered to:

customers who reside in Italy and use the means of transport in the EU; or

* customers who reside outside the EU and use the means of transport in Italy.

In case of long-term rental of pleasure boats, the place of supply is Italy for B2C long-term rental of pleasure

boats when the boat is put at the disposal of:

+ acustomer in Italy where the service is actually provided by a supplier established in Italy and the boat is
used in the EU;

+ acustomer in a non-EU country by a non-EU supplier established in the same non-EU country and the
boat is used in Italy;

+ acustomer who is an Italian resident in a country other than the country where the supplier is established,
and the boat is used in the EU; or

+ anon-EU customer in a country other than the country where the supplier is established, and the boat is
used in Italy.

Use and enjoyment rules are also applicable to B2C telecommunications and broadcasting services.
‘Telecommunications and broadcasting services provided to an Italian customer are subject to Italian VAT if
the service is used and enjoyed in the EU.

4, Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles
Supplies of immovable properties shall be deemed to be effected at the date on which the agreement
concerning the transfer is signed.

Supplies of goods shall be deemed to be effected at the time of delivery or forwarding.

Supplies of services shall be deemed to be effected when payment is received wholly or in part in respect of
the amount paid.

In case of Article 44 services, the services are deemed to be carried out when the services are completed. In
case the same services are supplied continuously, they are deemed to be carried out at the date of accrual of
the consideration.

If before the above events take place, the payment is made wholly or in part, the transaction shall be deemed
to have been carried out at the date of payment, but only in respect of the amount paid. The same services,
which are supplied continuously over a period of more than 1 year and which do not give rise to statements of

Italy | 319

--- Page 136 ---
account or payments during that period, shall be regarded as being completed on expiry of each calendar year
until such time as the supply of services comes to an end.

4.2 Advance payments

If before the delivery of goods takes place, an invoice is issued or payment is made wholly or in part, the
transaction shall be deemed to have been carried out at the date of issuance of the invoice or of payment, but
only in respect of the amount invoiced or paid.

Regarding the services, if the invoice is issued before the payment is carried out, the transaction shall be
deemed to have been carried out at the date of issuance of the invoice.

43 Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies (e.g. supplies of electricity for a certain period of time) of goods shall be deemed to be
effected at the date of payment.

44 Cash accounting

‘The cash accounting scheme is applicable to taxable persons with a VAT tumover in the previous year lower
than EUR 2,000,000 with respect to supplies of goods and services to taxable persons acting as such.

According to the cash accounting scheme, output VAT becomes due when the taxable person (the supplier

of goods or services) receives payment for his supply of goods or services (in any case, after | year from the
year the transaction is deemed to be carried out). On the other hand, VAT deduction is postponed until the
VAT on the goods or services supplied to the taxable person has been paid to its supplier. The cash accounting
scheme is not applicable to:

+ supplies to private consumers;
* transactions subject to another special regime (such as the margin scheme);
* transactions subject to the reverse charge;

+ intra-Community transactions, exports and other zero-rated transactions; and
+ imports and the release of goods from a VAT warehouse.

The option to use the cash accounting scheme must be communicated to the tax authorities in the annual
VAT return for the tax period in which the scheme has first been applied. The option for the cash accounting
scheme is then compulsory for at least 3 years unless the threshold of EUR 2,000,000 is exceeded.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

According to the VAT Law, the supplier of goods or services is allowed to recover the output VAT on sales
invoices, some of which are unpaid, (whether totally or partially) if specific conditions are met.

Under the relevant rules, the VAT (relating to unpaid receivables) can be recovered as from:

+ the date in which the purchaser is subject to one of the bankrupicy proceedings; or

+ the date of the decree approving the restructuring agreement referred to in Art, 182-bis of R.D, no. 267 of
142; or

» the date in which the recovery plans referred to in Art. 67 of R.D. no. 267 of 142 is published in the
Register of Enterprises.

The bankruptcy proceedings that are considered relevant in order to recover VAT are the following:

+ bankruptcy (so-called ‘fallimento’);
+ compulsory administrative liquidation (so-called ‘liquidazione coatta amministeativa’);

320 | Italy

--- Page 137 ---
+ adjustment of creditors’ claims (so-called ‘concordato fallimentare’);
+ arrangement before bankruptcy (so-called ‘concordato preventive”); and
+ bankruptcy in the course of the arrangement (so-called ‘fallimento nel corso del concordato preventivo’).

In presence of proceedings for insolvency, a receivable shall be considered as definitively unpaid in the

following cases:

«incase of garnishment, when the minutes drawn up by the bailiff states the lack of goods or credits to be
attached;

* in case of attachment on movable goods, when the minutes drawn up by the bailiff states the lack of
goods to be attached or the impossibility of access to the domicile or impossibility of finding; and

+ in case the auction for the sale of the foreclosed property has failed three times, it is decided to interrupt
the proceedings being too onerous.

When the above conditions occur, the credit note can be issued within the deadline for filing the Annual VAT
return relating to the year in which the condition occurred. The relating VAT can be deducted within the
deadline to file the annual VAT return relating to the year in which the credit note has been issued.

+ For example, if the judgment declaring the debtor’s bankruptcy is dated 10 March 2025, the supplier can
proceed as follows: the credit note is issued during 2025 and VAT can be deducted at the latest in the
annual VAT return to be filed for 2025 (i.e, within 30 April 2026, deadline for filing the VAT return);

+ the credit note is issued during the first four months of 2026 (i.e. within 30 April 2026, deadline for filing
the VAT return relating to 2025) and VAT can be deducted during 2026 or at the latest in the annual VAT
retum to be filed for 2026 (i.e, within 30 April 2027, deadline for filing the VAT return).

5.2 Cash discountsAssume that the supplier sends the following invoice:
Supply of goods EUR 1,000
22% VAT EUR_220
Total to be paid EUR 1,220

According to the conditions agreed between parties, if payment is received within the next month, a 5%
discount is applied.

‘The Italian law provides that the issuance of a credit invoice relevant for VAT purposes and the consequent
reduction of the taxable base represents an option of the supplier. In this example, if the condition agreed by
the parties is met (i.e. the payment is received within the next month), the supplier can reduce the taxable
base of the original supply by issuing a credit invoice (so-called ‘nota di credito’) of EUR 50 plus VAT.

5.3 Open Market Value

The open market value applies to the following:

+ exchanges of goods and services;

+ transactions between related parties if certain circumstances occur;

+ in the case of a separation of activities; and

+ putting company cars and mobile phones at the disposal of employees in case the consideration agreed is
Tower than the open market value under certain circumstances.

In case of supplies of goods by way of a discount or a premium, the open market value must be indicated in
the invoice.

54 Returnable packaging

Packaging and recipients for which the return is expressly provided for, are not included in the taxable
amount.

In any case, their amount must be separately reported in the invoice.

Italy | 321

--- Page 138 ---
Packaging for which there is only the possibility of return but not an obligation, as well as packaging that is
not returned within the agreed time limit, shall be subject to VAT.

6. VAT rates and exemptions

6.1 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate is 22%.

6.2 Reduced rate

The reduced VAT rate is 10% and applies to, among others:

+ supplies of certain food and non-alcoholic drinks;

* catering;

+ hotel accommodation;

+ supplies of pharmaceutical products for human or veterinary medicine;

+ supplies of gas and electricity for domestic use;

+ some oil products;

+ theatre and cinema products;

+ certain substantial repair work on real estate; and

> letting of residential real estate carried out by building and renovation firms.

6.3 Super-reduced rates

The super-reduced VAT rate is 4% and primarily relates to the supply of basic foodstuff, books and other
printed material and sales of new property under certain circumstances.

The reduced rate of 5% applies to educational and social and health services rendered by social co-operatives
and consortia thereof Lo certain categories and supply of some anti-Covid goods.

7. Exemptions

TA Exemption with the right to deduction

The VAT Law provides for 2 different types of exempt transactions: those giving the right to recover input,
VAT and those limiting the right of deduction.

The first type of transaction is related to the international traffic of goods and can be summarized as follows:
+ (direct, indirect or triangular) exports;

+ transport of passengers carried out partly in Italy and partly outside Italy;

+ transportation services relating to the export of goods;

+ hiring of transport means for the purposes of the above transportation services;

+ services carried out in airports and harbours;

+ supply, repair maintenance, chartering and hiring of certain seagoing vessels and aircraft;

+ supply of fuelling and provisioning to the same vessels and aircraft;

+ — supply of intermediary services relating to goods to be imported/exported or in transit; and

+ intra~Community supplies (direct or triangular transactions).

1 Intra-Community supplies

Regarding the proof of intra-Community transport, as of 2020 the rules provided for by Art. 45a of the EU
Regulation 282/2011 as implemented by the EU Regulation 2018/1912 of 4 December 2018, apply.

Additionally, the operation shall be VAT exempt under the conditions that the purchaser is included in the
VIES archive and the supply is correctly reported in the Intrastat form.

322 | Italy

--- Page 139 ---
According to the clarifications issued by the Italian Tax Authority the indications provided by the Ttalian Tax
Authority for proof of transport are still valid.

According to the Italian Tax Authorities, the following documentation needs to be maintained in order to

apply the VAT exemption in case of Ex Works (EXW) supplies:

+ the invoice issued to the EU customer registered for VAT purposes (in a EU Member State other than Italy);

+ the documentation concerning the agreements between the parties;

+ the Intrastat forms reporting the invoice;

+ the transport document (such as CMR) signed by the carrier and the recipient of the goods and statement
from the purchaser whereby the latter states that goods have been delivered; and

+ proof of payment.

With respect to Ex Works (EXW) supplies, the tax authorities have clarified that if the transport document is
missing (or it does not report the signature of the recipient), the proof of the transport can be provided by any
other document suitable for proving the transport of the goods to another EU Member State.

The combination of documents made available must provide clear evidence of the transportation of the goods
to another EU Member State.

An invoice needs to be issued in the case of an intra-Community transfer of own goods.

7.1.2 Export supplies

In order to apply the VAT exemption to exports, it is necessary to furnish proof of exportation. Said proof

must be furnished for the following cases:

+ direct exports by means of the third copy of the SAD or the invoice (or a transport document) endorsed by
the customs authorities where the goods leave the EU;

+ triangular exports (which is a peculiarity provided by the Italian VAT Law) by means of the thitd copy of
the SAD, endorsed by the Customs authorities where the goods leave the EU, for the Italian customer and
by means of the invoice issued by the Italian supplier endorsed by the Customs authorities for the first
Italian supplier; and

+ indirect exports (it means when the non-Italian resident customer is in charge of transporting the goods
from Italy to the NON-EU country of destination) by means of the invoice endorsed by the Customs
authorities where the goods leave the EU (since the SAD will be kept by the non-resident customer).

Furthermore, regarding the implementation of the Export Control System (ECS) in the EU, introduced by EU
Regulation no. 1875/2006, as of 1 July 2007, the proof of exportation is represented by an electronic message.
In particular, the proof of direct exportation is represented by the “confirmation of exit” message resulting
from the customs authorities’ website, to be checked by means of a Movement Reference Number (MRN).

In addition, the company must also keep in its files all the other documentation providing evidence of the exit

from the EU, such as:

+ the transport document signed by the recipient of the goods or a transport document with the evidence of
delivery to the destination issued by the legal representative of the carrier; and

+ proof of payment.

Other exempt transactions include supplies of goods and services to the diplomatic seats, to the Military
headquarters of an EU Member State or to the Italian Ministry of Defence in execution of a NATO program,
to the EU Commission or to the United Nations.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse
Subject to certain conditions, both national and EU goods can be stored in a VAT warehouse.

As long as they are stored in a VAT warehouse, goods cannot be sold to private persons, since the withdrawal
from a VAT warehouse may be performed exclusively by taxable persons.

Italy | 323

--- Page 140 ---
Apart from the above rule, and also on the basis of clarifications provided for by the Customs authorities, the

following goods are not allowed to be admitted for storage under a VAT warehousing arrangement:

+ non-BU goods (i.e., goods not put into free circulation within the EU);

+ temporarily imported goods;

* goods stored in temporary custody warehouses before receiving a Customs destination; and

+ compensating products obtained from equivalent goods to be exported from the EU before importation of
the import goods through advance export.

It is possible to place non-EU goods in a VAT warehouse, but only when the importer provides the Customs
authorities with a warranty for the amount of the import VAT. The warranty is released when the goods are re-
moved from the warehouse and the relating VAT, if applicable, is correctly accounted for by the releasing entity.

Until the adoption of the definitive procedure, Customs authorities have clarified that in order to release the

warranty, the following documentation must be filed with them:

+ a copy of the self-invoice or copy of the invoice (in case of export or intra-Community supply) integrated
with the references of the registration in the VAT ledgers. As an alternative to the integration, copy of the
VAT ledgers can be shown; and

+ a statement according to DPR no. 445/200, reporting the declaration of conformity of the copies to
the original as well as the registration in the VAT ledgers. A copy of an identity document of the legal
representative is also necessary.

The warranty is not required from:
+ authorized economic operators; and
+ taxable persons that are known to the Customs authorities to be solvent.

The status of solvency must be ascertained by the Customs authorities after checking the following

documentation to be filed with them:

* application to be exempted from the provision of the warranty;

+ historical certificate issued by the Chamber of Commerce, which indicated that the taxable person has
been registered at the Chamber of Commerce for (at least) 1 year;

+ certificate issued by the Italian Tax Authority indicating the lack of tax debts; and

+ statement according to DPR no. 445/2000 indicating the amount of the imports carried out during the last
12 months. The mentioned imports must not have been carried out irregularly and, therefore, must not
have been subject to penalties.

In case of a positive check, the authorization is valid for 1 year and for an amount equal to the VAT
corresponding to the imports carried out during the previous year, including the imports made during the
previous year through the VAT warehouse.

The VAT warehousing regime also applies in the case of services carried out in places close to the VAT
warehouse, including processing and handling transactions (that must not be longer than 60 days) carried
out on goods delivered to the VAT warehouse keeper. It is not required that the goods are unloaded from the
means of transport

Considering that the function of a VAT warehouse is the storage and custody of goods (although no minimum
storage period is established and no unloading from transport means is required), an ‘accounting’ storage is
not permitted.

As of 1 April 2017, all supplies of goods introduced into the VAT warehouse shall be possible without VAT
payment independently of the nature of the goods and of the ‘status’ (Italian, EU or non-EU) of the parties to
the transactions.

Additionally as of the date of 1 April 2017, the release of goods (goods imported as well as domestic supplies
of goods placed in the VAT warehouse) from a VAT warehouse which are to be used or supplied in the
territory of the Italian state can only be carried oul by VAT taxable persons and implies the payment of VAT
by this VAT taxable person who releases the goods.

324 | Italy

--- Page 141 ---
In case of extraction of goods previously imported and put into free circulation through introduction in the
VAT warehouse, VAT is still due by the VAT taxable person through the reverse charge mechanism but, in
principle, upon filing of a warranty (which is different than the one filed at the moment of the introduction
of the goods. See above explanation). The cases in which this warranty is due, the content of it and the
formalities are defined through the decree of the Minister of Economy and Finance dated 23 February 2017
and the following implementing measures of:

+ VAT taxable persons exempted from the warranty;

+ Cases of exclusion from the warranty; and

+ Compulsory Warranty.

VAT taxable persons are exempted from the warranty if the following conditions are met (‘solvency

conditions’):

+ filing of the annual VAT return during the 3 tax periods preceding the extraction of the goods;

+ VAT payments carried out (if die) arising from the latest 3 annual VAT returns filed at the moment of
extraction of goods;

+ lack of definitive notices of assessment - for which the payment has not been carried out — regarding
the issuance and use of invoices for non ~ existent transactions. The said notices must be notified in the
current tax period or in the 3 tax periods preceding the extraction of goods; and

+ lack of formal knowledge regarding the starting of criminal proceedings or imposition of criminal fines
for the specific criminal tax violations provided for by d.lgs. no. 74/2000.

The presence of the above conditions is stated through a specific form (approved by the Italian Tax Authority)
to be filled in by the taxable person extracting the goods and to be filed with the VAT warehouse keeper when
the first extraction is carried out; the statement is valid for one year. The warehouse keeper must file the form

with the Tax Authority.

Cases of exclusion:

The requirements of solvency as indicated above are deemed to exist if | of the following conditions are met:

+ the importer (who placed the goods into the VAT warehouse) and the VAT taxable person extracting the
goods from the VAT warehouse are the same; and

+ the VAT taxable person extracting the goods from the VAT warehouse is: (i) an authorized economic
operator; or (ii) a company that is known as solvent by the Custom authorities.

In the above cases, VAT is due by the VAT taxable person extracting the goods on the basis of the reverse
charge mechanism.

Compulsory warranty

If the ‘solvency conditions’ are not met, VAT is due through the reverse charge mechanism and a specific
warranty (the same one as is used for VAT refund purposes) must be filed, The amount to be guaranteed is
equal to the VAT and the warranty is effective for 6 months as from the date of extraction. A copy of the
warranty must be filed with the Tax Authority when the goods are extracted.

In case of extraction of goods previously introduced through a domestic supply in order to be used or supplied
in the territory of the Italian state, VAT is due by the taxable person extracting the goods and is paid, on his
behalf, by the warehouse keeper, jointly responsible with the taxable person extracting the goods.

The payment must be carried out through the F24 Form before the 16" of the month following the one in
which the extraction has been effected. ‘The VAT due cannot be offset against other credits.

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

VAT can be suspended for supplies of specific goods under excise duty suspension (gasoline or diesel fuel
intended for use as motor) carried out in the fiscal warehouse or the transfer of the said goods from a fiscal
warehouse to another fiscal warehouse or between a fiscal warehouse and the warehouse of a registered
consignee.

Italy | 325

--- Page 142 ---
Specific rules regarding the payment of the VAT for the said goods apply in relation to the release for
consumption from a fiscal warehouse or extraction from a registered consignee’s warehouse.

7.1.3.3 Bonded warehouse
Goods may be stored in a bonded warehouse in Italy.

If goods transported from a third country are directly placed under a bonded warehouse regime, customs
duties and VAT are not due. The supply of the said goods in the bonded warehouse is out of scope. However,
an invoice must be issued reporting that the amount is not subject to VAT. The said fulfilment must be carried
out by non-resident taxable persons too according to the clarifications issued by the tax authority.

When goods are released from a customs warehouse and placed into free circulation in Italy, import VAT must
be immediately paid to the Customs authorities,

In addition, it is possible to place the goods into VAT-free zones in Italy. The goods placed into a free zone
may be transformed, processed, eto, or stored in warchouses, without payment of VAT or customs duties.

7.2 Exemptions without the right to deduction

7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest

Exemptions without the right of deduction are mainly provided for the following supplies (the list is not
exhaustive but covers the main supplies):

* postal services;

+ services supplied by hospitals, clinics and authorised health institutions;

+ ambulance services:

+ services supplied by orphanages, asylums, rest homes etc.;

+ social welfare and assistance to employees;

+ education if certain conditions are met;

+ supply of human organs, blood and human milk;

+ services supplied by libraries, museums, galleries etc; and

+ free supplies of certain assets to non-profit organisations if certain conditions are met.

7.2.2 Other exemptions

Other exemptions concern:

+ financial-insurance activities;

+ credit transactions and related services, including the granting of warranties and similar transactions;
+ management of investment funds;

+ activities relating to foreign exchange and transactions in foreign currencies;

* insurance and reinsurance activities;

+ transactions relating to shares, bonds and other securities not representing merchandise;

+ _ activities relating to authorized betting and lotteries; and

* intermediary services relating to transactions referred to above.

7.3. Option for taxation

The supplier can opt for the application of VAT with reference to the following exempt operations:

+ sale of residential real estate carried out by the building company (or the one that carried out the
renovation of the building) afler 5 years from the completion of the building/renovation provided that the
option for taxation is expressed by the supplier in the deed of transfer;

« _ sale of social housing provided that the option for taxation is expressed by the supplier in the deed of
transfer;

+ sale of instrumental buildings carried out by the building company (or the one that carried out the
renovation of the building) after 5 years from the completion of the building/renovation provided that the
option for taxation is expressed by the supplier in the deed of transfer;

+ sale of instrumental buildings where the supplier is not the building company (or the one that carried out

326 | Italy

--- Page 143 ---
the renovation of the building) provided that the option for taxation is expressed by the supplier in the
deed of transfer;

+ _ renting of residential real estate if the lessor is the building company (or the one that carried out the
renovation of the building) provided that the option for taxation is expressed by the lessor in the rental
agreement;

+ renting of instrumental buildings provided that the option for taxation is expressed by the lessor in the
rental agreement; and

+ sale of investment gold and relating intermediation services in case the option for taxation is expressed
in the annual VAT return, The option can be expressed by entities producing, transforming and trading
in investment gold per single supply or for all the operations but, in the latter case, it applies for at least
three years. The option can be exercised only for supplies to taxable persons and the VAT is due by the
latter through the reverse charge mechanism.

8. _ Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

Several reverse charge rules are provided for by the VAT Law.

In Italy there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B services
provided by non-resident suppliers to taxable persons and legal entities with an Italian VAT ID number
established in Italy.

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)
A general reverse charge rule applies on any supply of goods or services taxable in Italy when it is made by
a non-established person to a taxable person or a non-taxable legal entity resident or established in Italy. The
reverse charge rule also applies when the non-established supplier is registered for VAT purposes in Italy for
having appointed a VAT representative or directly registered.

The reverse charge rule does not apply when the customer is a non-resident company. This means when both
the supplier and the customer are non-Italian established operators, the standard rule applies and VAT is due
by the supplier.

Areference must be made on the invoice to the application of the reverse charge as follows: “VAT due by the
recipient under Article 17, Paragraph 2 of the DPR 633/72”. A non-resident supplier does not have to report
the invoices subject to the general reverse charge rule on the VAT return, even if the supplier is registered for
VAT purposes in Italy.

According to the tax authorities, in case of purchases from EU established suppliers, also those with a VAT
registration number in Italy, the invoice must be issued under the VAT registration number of the country of
establishment of the EU supplier.

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism — specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

Domestic reverse charge rules apply to the following supplies and/or services:

+ supply of investment gold and non-investment gold in case of option to apply VAT;

* _ services relating to the construction of immovable property (as per the ATECO classification) supplied
by subcontractors to the main contractor or to other subcontractors (but not to services supplied by the
contractor to the principal);

+ supply of an immovable property with an option for taxation expressed by the supplier in the deed of
transfer;

* the provision of cleaning services, of demolition, plant installation and completion services relating to
buildings;

+ supply of mobile phones and components of computers (valid until 31 December 2026);

+ supply of video games consoles, tablets pc, and laptops (valid until 31 December 2026);

+ supply of scrap, waste and scrap of ferrous metals and articles thereof, waste paper, rags and scrap of

ltaly | 327

--- Page 144 ---
bones, hides and skins, glass, rubber and plastics, as well as wooden pallets recovered from the cycles

of use subsequent to the first one, including those relating to the aforesaid goods that have been cleaned,
sorted, cut, compacted, ingot moulded or subjected to other treatments to favilitate their use, transport and
storage without changing their nature;

+ supply of natural gas (through any natural gas system situated within the territory of the EU Community
or any network connected to such system), electricity, and heating or cooling energy (through heating and
cooling networks) to “taxable dealers” (valid until 31 December 2026);

+ transfer of allowances to emit greenhouse gases (valid until 31 December 2026);

* transfer of other units that may be used by operators to comply with Directive 2003/87/EC and the
transfer of certificates relating to gas and electricity (valid until 31 December 2026);

+ services rendered in the consortium, in particular services rendered to the consortium by enterprises
which are members of the consortium, being the latter contractor with a public body to be invoiced
according to the split - payment rules. The said reverse charge shall be applicable in case of positive
authorization from the European Union; and

+ supply of services carried out through work contracts, subcontracting, entrusting to consortium entities
or negotiated relationships however denominated characterized by the prevalent use of labor at the
customer’s places of business with the use of instrumental goods owned by the customer or traceable to
the customer in any form, rendered to companies engaged in the business of transport and handling of
goods and provision of logistics services. The effectiveness of the new rules is subject to the authorization
from the European Union. Until the European Union authorizes the rule, as from | January 2025 the
supplier and the customer may opt for the VAT payment to be made by the customer in the name and on
behalf of the supplier (who remains jointly and severally liable for the VAT due),

A supplier must issue an invoice with a reference to the applicable reverse charge law provision.

8.2 Joint and several liability

Italian law provides for joint and several liability for VAT grouping and Italian VAT group settlement as above
indicated.

Also in case of fiscal representation, the representative is

intly and severally liable with the non-resident.

9. Recovery of input VAT

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

Input VAT incurred on certain goods and services is not recoverable (even when the purchase is made for
business purposes) unless the taxable person carries out a business activity relating to the items listed below:
+ business gifts:

+ input VAT incurred when purchasing gifts is, in principle, not deductible unless their purchase cost is
lower than EUR 50;

+ entertainment expenses:

+ VAT incurred on entertainment expenses, other than business gifts is, in principle, recoverable only
when these expenses are also eligible for deduction for corporate tax purposes (i.e., when the above
expenses are deemed to have an advertising character);

+ mobile phones:

+ VAT on the purchase, importation, hire or lease of mobile phones, and related management charges,
may be recovered if the purchase is carried out in the ordinary course of business and to the extent of
their use for this purpose. However, tax authorities may check the business purpose in the case of a
deduction higher than 50%;

+ costs relating to cars (including fuel):

+ only 40% of the input VAT incurred on the purchase, financial leasing, hire, and similar activities
of cars may be recovered. The same limit applies to input VAT on maintenance, repair services and
fael. Input VAT on tracks and vans is always recoverable if they are used in the ordinary course of
business;

328 | Italy

--- Page 145 ---
+ costs relating to aircraft and leisure yachts

+ input VAT incurred on the purchase, hire or leasing of aircraft and leisure yachts as well as on the
purchase of fuel and maintenance or repair services is not recoverable unless the use/purchase of these
goods is the purpose of the business, e.g. the VAT person carries out a transport or leasing business:

+ residential dwellings:

+ input VAT incurred on the purchase, maintenance or repair of residential dwellings is recoverable only
by taxable persons whose exclusive or main business activity is the resale, building and leasing of
such properties; and

+ VAT on the transportation of persons.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT is recoverable and refunded by the VAT authorities in specific circumstances, In particular,
the VAT Law provides for annual and quarterly claims.

Annual claims

For VAT-registered persons, recoverable input VAT exceeding VAT on supplies can either be carried
forward to the next period or refunded, provided that the amount exceeds EUR 2,582 and 1 of the following
conditions is met:

* discontinuation of the activity;

+ the average output VAT rate is lower than the average input VAT rate: in order to apply this condition,
the average rate on purchases and imports must exceed the average rate on supplies, increased by 10%.
Purchases (and or imports) and sales of depreciable assets are excluded from the computation;

* exempt transactions with credit: in order to apply this condition, the amount of exempt transactions with
credit (i.e, exports and intra-EU supplies) must be more than 25% of the overall amount of transactions
carried out in the tax year;

+ up to the amount of input VAT incurred on the purchases and imports of depreciable goods and of goods
and services purchased for R&D purposes;

* when the taxpayer carries out, for the most part, transactions not subject to VAT because of territoriality
rules;

+ non-resident taxable persons (cither directly registered for VAT or with a VAT representative); and

*  inany case, the refund claim can be submitted for an amount equal to the lower VAT credit of the last 3
years.

‘The annual VAT refund claim is included in the annual return to be filed each year from 1 February up to the
deadline for filing the VAT return, i.e. 30 April of the year following the relevant one.

The refund up to the amount of EUR 2,000,000 (on an yearly basis) is known as a refund through the ‘fiscal
account’, and the taxable person’s bank account must be credited by the tax collector with the amount of
VAT credit claimed within 60 days from the date on which the refund request is filed. If this deadline is not
respected, interest accrues start from the 60" day following the date of the request.

In case of a refund up to the amount of EUR 30,000, no warranty is due. For a refund exceeding EUR 30,000,
alternatively (with the exclusions reported below), it is possible:

+ to file a warranty (ie, bank or insurance guarantee); or

* to file the VAT return or the request for a refund with a certification from specific professionals (so called
‘endorsement of conformity’ or ‘visto di conformité’ in Italian) or with the signature of the body in charge
of the accounting audit,

Regarding the second option, it is also necessary to file a statement (which must be attached to the annual/

quarterly refund form) whereby the following must be stated:

+ — the net equity has not decreased — compared to the accounting results of the latest tax period — more than 40%;

+ the amount of real estate has not decreased — compared to the accounting results of the latest tax period —
more than 46% for sales not made in the normal course of the activity carried out;

Italy | 329

--- Page 146 ---
+ the activity has not ceased or been reduced as a result of the transfer (total or partial) of going concerns
included in the accounting results;

+ incase of a claim filed from companies (in Italian ‘socicta di capitali’) not listed on regulated markets, the
shares of the claimant have not been sold for amounts exceeding 50% of the share capital during the year
preceding the request for a refund; and

+ the payment of social security and insurance contributions has been carried out.

‘The warranty is mandatory (i.e. no certification is possible) when the claim is filed by:

+ taxable persons carrying on a business for less than 2 years (other than the start-up innovative businesses);

+ taxable persons to whom, in the 2 years preceding the request for refund, a notice of assessment has been
issued.

From the said notice of assessment, it must result, for each year, a difference between the amounts assessed
and VAT due or the credit declared higher than:
- 10% of the amounts declared if they do not exceed EUR 150,000;
- 5% of the amounts declared if they exceed EUR 150,000 but less than EUR 1,500,000;
- 1% of the amounts declared with a minimum of EUR 150,000 if EUR 1,500,000 is exceeded;
+ taxable persons lacking certification (or signature from an audit body) or without a statement as specified
in art. 47 DPR 28 December 2000, n. 445; and
+ taxable persons requiring the refund of the VAT credit as a result of the act of cessation of the activity.

‘The warranty is to be filed by a bank or an insurance company. Please note that the amount to be guaranteed
is equal to the VAT amount claimed for refund plus interest. The interest amount to be guaranteed is
calculated:

+ for a period of 3 years after the date of payment of the VAT refund; or

«until the expiration deadline of the statute of limitations, if this period is shorter than 3 years.

For any amount exceeding EUR 2,000,000, the VAT credit refund must be claimed with the competent office
of the tax authorities “Agenzia delle Entratc’ by filing the so called ‘ordinary procedure”

In this case, according to the rules currently in force, the refund should be granted no later than 90 days
starting from the date of the request being filed by the taxable person. Interest accrues starting from the 90"
day following the date the request is filed. The same conditions (guarantee or certification), as described
above for a refund through the fiscal account, are required in this case.

Quarterly claims

Quarterly VAT credit refund claims can be filed by electronic means for the first 3 quarters of the year:
+ by 30 April for the first quarter;

+ by 31 July for the second quarter; and

+ by 31 October for the third quarter.

But this may only be done if at least 1 of the following conditions is satisfied:

+ average rate: the average output VAT rate is lower than the average input VAT rate; the average rate on
purchases and imports exceeds the average rate on supplies, increased by 10%; purchases (and or imports)
and sales of depreciable assets are excluded from the computation;

= exempt transactions with credit must be more than 25% of the overall amount of transactions carried out
in the tax year;

+ incase of purchases and imports of depreciable goods, the refund can be claimed only if the mentioned
purchases and imports of depreciable goods exceed 2/3 of the total taxable amount of purchases and
imports carried out in the quarter,

+ claimant is a non-resident taxable person (either directly registered for VAT or registered with a VAT
representative); or

+ the taxable person carries out, for the most part, transactions not subject to VAT due to territoriality rules.

330 | Italy

--- Page 147 ---
In particular, in order to file the claim, the following two conditions regarding the transactions must be met:

+ the taxable person renders to subjects established outside the territory of Italy for an amount higher than
50% of the total amount of all the transactions, work on movable goods, transport services of goods
and ancillary services and related intermediary services or insurance and financial services to subjects
established outside the EU referred to goods to be exported outside the EU; and

* the refund must be claimed from the tax authorities ‘Agenzia delle Entrate’, who will make the payment
within 20 days from the end of the second month following the relevant quarter. Should said deadline not
be respected, interest accrued from the 30" day following the due date of the taxable person.

The same rules (guarantee or certification) as described above in case of a refund through the fiscal account,
are also required in case of a quarterly refund.

9.3 Capital goods — adjustment periods

+ immovable property - 10 years
+ movable property - 5 years

Italian law does not provide for an adjustment period for services other than certain services relating to
depreciable assets, for which either the 5 or the 10 year adjustment period applies.

94 VAT refunds for foreigners

EU and non-EU taxable persons may apply for the refund of Italian VAT through the EU VAT Refund Portal

on purchases and imports of movable goods and/or services provided that:

+ they do not have a fixed establishment in Italy;

+ they do not supply goods and services in Italy apart from zero rated international transport of goods and
ancillary services or supply of goods and services taxable in Italy for which the option for OSS or JOSS
has been exercised; and

+ the supply of goods and services is to a person who is liable for payment of VAT through the reverse
charge mechanism,

In order to be refundable, the VAT must be deductible according to the relevant rules (see 9.1 for information
on the non-recoverability of VAT)

9.4.1 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

The VAT refund application must be filed:
+ _ by electronic means with the EU Member State in which the taxable person is established;
+ with respect to a period of not less than 3 months and for an amount of not less than EUR 400,

Should the amount relating to | or more quarters be less than EUR 400, the refund may be claimed with
reference made to the calendar year, provided that the amount of VAT is not less than EUR 50. The reference
period can be less than 3 months where it represents the remainder of a calendar year. The reference period
cannot be more than | year,

The deadlines to file the application are the following:

+ ifthe reference period is a quarter, it should be filed as of the first day of the month following the
reference period up to 30 September of the calendar year following the reference period; and

+ ifthe reference period is a calendar year, it should be filed from 1 January up to 30 September of the year
following the reference period.

‘The application is considered to be filed on the date on which the file, as forwarded by the EU Member
State where the applicant is established, is fully received by the competent Italian Tax Authority (i.e., Centro
Operativo di Pescara).

Invoices should be attached.

Italy | 331

--- Page 148 ---
The Italian tax authorities will notify the applicant, by electronic means, also through the EU Member State
of establishment, of the date on which it received the application.

Notification of the decision to approve or refuse the refund application will be made by the Italian Tax
Authority (j.e,, Centro Operativo di Pescara) within 4 months from its receipt.

The bank account will be credited within 10 working days from the date of notice that the application has
been approved.

The refund is made to an Italian bank account or, upon request of the applicant, to a bank account in another
EU Member State. In the latter case, the amount paid is net of transfer expenses.

Should the Italian Tax Authority need further information in order to check the application, this information
will be requested of the applicant or of its EU Member State through electronic means within 4 months from
the receipt of the application. The requested information must be provided within 1 month from the date

on which the request has been received. In this case, the decision to approve or refuse the refund will be
communicated within 2 months from the receipt of the information or, if there is no reply, within 2 months
from the expiry of the term.

Additional information may be requested by the Italian Tax Authority, If that is the case, the information
should be provided within 1 month from the date on which the request has been received. In this case,
notification of the decision to approve or refuse the refund should be made within 8 months of receipt of the
refund application.

Interest accrues at 2% per year as of the deadline to carry out the payment (ie., 10 working days from the
date of notice that the application has been approved). Should further or additional information be requested,
interest accrues from the due date to submit the additional information,

Interest does not accrue if the applicant does not submit the information requested. Interest will also not apply
so long as requested further documents are not received.

With respect to the content of the refund application, the list of requirements is available on the website:
https:/itinyurl.com/uabus8ct

In any case, it should be noted that the description of the goods and services should be indicated by using the
codes and sub-codes set by EU Regulation 30 November 2009, n. 1174,

Itis possible to correct the application already filed by filing a new one (without adding new invoices or
import documents) before the normal deadline (i.¢., 30 September of the following year).

Finally, the Italian Tax Authority should be informed in case of adjustment of the deductible proportion within
the following calendar year.
The decision to refuse the refund can be appealed within 60 days as of the date of receipt of the decision.

9.4.2 VAT refunds to non-EU taxable persons

The refund of VAT to non-EU subjects is allowed, provided that the ‘condition of reciprocity’ with the other
State is met.

Currently, Switzerland, Norway, Israel and the UK are the only non-EU countries for which this condition is
satisfied.

In case of option for OSS or IOSS regimes, the refund is allowed also in the lack of the ‘condition of
reciprocity’ and in presence of operations carried out in Italy for which the said regimes apply.

332 | Italy

--- Page 149 ---
The deadline to file the application is 30 September of the following year and the competent Italian Tax
Authority (i.e., Centro Operativo di Pescara) will carry out the refund within 6 months from the receipt of the
application or within 8 months should further information be requested,

The refund can be requested with respect to a quarter or a year.

The form to be used is the “Modello IVA 79” that should be sent by courier to the Italian Tax Authority (i.e.
Centro Operativo di Pescara).

(htips:/tinyurl.com/394srfid)

The following documents must be enclosed with the above-mentioned form:

+ original invoices;

+ proof of payment; and

+ declaration from the tax authorities of the State of establishment that the claimant is a taxable person.

Interest accrues at 2% per year as of the deadline to carry out the refund (j.¢., 6 months from the receipt of the
application or 8 months should further information be requested).

Interest will not accrue if the applicant does not submit the information requested. Interest will also not apply
until requested further documents in support of the refund application are received.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoicing

10.1.1 Invoice content

An invoice must indicate:

+ the date of issuance;

+ anumber with a specific and unambiguous reference:

+ the VAT identification number of the supplier;

+ the VAT identification number of the customer and, if the customer is established in another EU Member
State, the VAT identification number issued by the EU Member State of establishment; in case the
customer is not acting as a taxable person, the tax code; in case of intra-community supplies, the VAT
identification number (included in the VIES archive) must be mentioned in the invoice;

+ the name and address of both the supplier and the customer, and the data of the VAT representative of the
supplier or of the customer, if any;

+ adescription of the goods and services: quality, nature and quantity;

+ the applicable VAT rate;

+ the amount of the VAT due and of the taxable basis, rounded to the eurocent, which must be split per
applicable rate;

+ the date on which the supply of goods or the provision of services is carried out, or the date in which the
consideration is paid in whole or in part, where this date is different from the invoice date;

+ an indication that the invoice is issued by the customer or by a third party, if this is the case (see below).

For the following transactions it is necessary to report whichever circumstance is applicable:

+ supply of goods in transit or subject to special Customs regime (such as Customs warehouse regime):
“operazione non soggetta” (i.e., transaction not subject to VAT);

+ VAT-exempt transactions that give the right to recover input VAT on purchases according to Article 8,
8-bis, 9 and 38-quarter of DPR 633/72: “operazione non imponibile” (i.e. transaction not taxable);

+ VAT-exempt transactions that limit the right of deduction according to Article 10 (except for betting and
gambling transactions) of DPR 633/72: “operazione esente” (i.e exempt transaction);
+ transactions subject to a special margin scheme as provided for by Decree no. 41/1995: “regime del

margine — beni usati” (margin scheme — used goods);

Italy | 333

--- Page 150 ---
+ “regime del margine-oggetti d’arte” (margin scheme — work of art); or

+ “regime del margine — oggetti di antiquariato o da collezione” (margin scheme ~ antique or collection
objects);

+ transactions carried out by travel agencies subject to margin scheme as provided for by Article 74-ter
of DPR 633/72: “regime del margine ~ agenzic di viaggio” (margin scheme ~ travel agencies).

For all the above transactions, it is possible to make a reference cither to the domestic provision or to the EU
Directive provision.

For the following transactions carried out with taxable persons not established in Italy, the applicable one of

these texts is reported:

+ supply of goods or services by suppliers established in Italy to VAT taxable persons liable for payment of
the tax in another EU Member State: “inversione contabile”; (i.¢. reverse charge)

+ supply of goods and services by suppliers established in Italy deemed to be carried out outside the EU:
“operazione non soggetta” (i.c. transaction not subject to); and

+ purchases of goods and services from non-EU suppliers: “autofatturazione” (i.e. self-billing)

10.1.2 Time limits

The invoice must be issued within 12 days as from the date in which the operation is deemed to be carried
out. VAT is due regarding the month in which the chargeable event takes place.

For supplies of goods whose delivery is proven by a transport document, a single invoice can be issued for
all supplies taking place by the 15" of the month following the month of delivery, provided it mentions all
relevant transport documents.

‘The above rule is also applicable to the supply of services, as specified in suitable documentation, rendered
to the same customer during the same month. The invoice (reporting the details of the transactions) must be
issued by the 15" of the month following the month in which the transaction is deemed lo be carried out.

10.1.3 Currency
The taxable amount can be expressed in any currency while the VAT must be expressed in Euros.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

In case of a consideration and/or expenses in a foreign currency, the exchange rate of the day the transaction
is carried out for VAT purposes must be used or, in the absence of a relevant indication on the invoice, the
exchange rate of the date of issuance of the invoice. Lacking an exchange rate on this day, the exchange rate
should be that of the nearest day. It is possible to adopt the exchange rate published by the European Central
Bank for all the transactions carried out during the calendar year.

10.1.5 Self-billing

Under Italian VAT legislation, self-billing is allowed and, according to the tax authorities, authorization by the
supplier is necessary. In addition, the procedure (o be followed must be agreed upon by the two patties.

In order to allow the issuance of invoices by persons from non-EU countries if no mutual assistance

agreement exists with these countries, the following is required:

+ aprevious communication must be filed with the tax authorities; and

+ the activity of the Italian supplier must have started at least 5 years prior and no VAT violations must have
been made in the past 5 years.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice can be issued in cases where the amount of the invoice is not higher than EUR 100. The
said amount can be increased by up to EUR 400 by a specific decree. Furthermore, on the basis of a specific
decree, the simplified invoice can be applied without a threshold to specific sectors or specific taxable persons.

The simplified invoice must indicate:
+ the date of issuance;
+ anumber with a specific and unambiguous reference;

334 | Italy

--- Page 151 ---
» the VAT identification number of the supplier:

+ the name and address of the supplier and the data of the VAT representative of the supplier, if any;

* the name and address of the customer and the data of the VAT representative of the customer, if any; as an
alternative, it is possible to report exclusively;

* the tax code or the VAT registration number if the customer is established in Italy; or

* the VAT registration number issued by the EU Member State of establishment if the customer is
established in another EU Member State;

+ adescription of the goods and services;

+ the total amount due and the resultant VAT amount or the data necessary for its calculation; and

+ — for the invoices issued according to Article 26 of DPR 633/72, the reference to the changed invoice and
an indication of the changes made.

The simplified invoice cannot be issued for the following transactions:

+ intra-Community supply of goods; and

+ transactions as provided for by Article 21 (6bis) (a) of DPR 633/72 (supply of goods and services to a
taxable person liable to pay the tax in another EU Member State or supply of goods and services to be
deemed as carried out outside the EU).

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices must be kept available until 31 December of the 4" year following the year in which the annual
return is filed. If the annual return is not filed, the invoices must be kept until 31 December of the 5" year
following the year in which the annual return should haye been filed.

Invoices must be kept available until 31 December of the 5® year following the year in which the annual
return is filed. If the annual return is not filed, the invoices must be kept until 31 December of the 7" year
following the year in which the annual return should have been filed

However, according to the Italian Civil Code, invoices as well as other relevant business documents must be
kept available for 10 years.

Electronic invoices must be stored in electronic form.
Similarly, paper invoices may be saved on electronic storage systems.

Invoices, ledgers and other documents provided for by DPR 633/72 may be saved electronically in another
EU Member State with which a mutual assistance agreement exists.

For control purposes, the taxable person guarantees online access to the data concerned and guarantees that all
the documents therein saved (including the ones granting the authenticity and integrity of electronic invoices)
can be printed and saved in a storage medium

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Electronic invoicing is mandatory for B2B and B2C operations carried out between subjects resident or
established in Italy.

Non-resident subjects, although they are registered for VAT purposes in Italy for having appointed a VAT
representative or directly registered subjects, are excluded from the electronic invoicing.

The above excluded subjects can opt for electronic invoicing,
Regarding the operations with non-resident subjects, taxable persons established in Italy could opt to issue
electronic invoices to the non-resident subjects in order to avoid the fulfilment sub 10.2.4. In case of issuance

of a electronic invoice, the non-resident must receive a copy of the electronic invoice issued.

‘The paper copy must report exactly and exclusively the data reported in the electronic invoice and it is
necessary to state that the paper invoice is a certified copy of the original electronic invoice.

Italy | 335

--- Page 152 ---
Electronic invoices must be issued in a specific xml format (eXtensive Markup Language) and sent to the
Exchange System (SDI) by the taxable person directly or through designated intermediaries.

SDI is an electronic system managed by the Italian Tax Authority, through which electronic invoices are
received, delivered to the recipient and, in presence of certain conditions, electronic invoices are stored
electronically according to the law.

In order to receive the electronic invoice, the taxable person can use a Certified Electronic Mail or a recipient

10.2. VAT compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period
Each VAT taxable person (foreign or local) is required to fill in and file electronically an annual VAT return

‘The reporting period of the return is the calendar year.

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment

Annual VAT return

Taxable persons (foreign and local) should electronically file the annual VAT return for the previous calendar
year between 1 February and 30 April.

The annual VAT return must be drafted on the official form approved, each year, by the tax authorities
“Agenzia delle Entrate’ and made available on their website (/ttp://www.agenziaentrate.gov.it) at the
beginning of the year of filing,

Payments
On a monthly basis, output VAT on supplies carried out in the previous month must be offset against input
VAT allowed for deduction relating to purchase invoices booked in the same period,

The monthly VAT computation (so called ‘VAT settlement’ or ‘Liquidazione IVA’ in Italian language), and
payment of the VAT due, must be made by the 16" day of the month following the month being reported.

If the turnover of the previous tax period does not exceed EUR 500,000 for services or EUR 800,000 for
other activities (stich as supplies of goods), the taxable person may opt for quarterly payments to be made by
the following deadlines:

First quarter 16 May

Second quarter 20 August (deadline postponed from the normal 16 August due to the holiday season)
Third quarter 16 November

Fourth quarter/

yearly balance 16 March

In this case, the amount due for the quarterly computation must be increased with 1% interest. Taxable
persons are not required to report the monthly VAT computation on the Sales Ledger, but since the tax
authorities can request how the amount of VAT was computed, evidence should be retained.

‘The VAT Law provides for an annual VAT advance payment. When the VAT balance of the Jast relevant

period (month of December or Fourth Quarter) of the previous calendar year (referred to in this paragraph as

“year X-1”) shows a VAT liability, the following amount must be paid in advance:

+ 88% of the VAT liability due for the same period (December or Fourth Quarter) of the previous calendar
year (year X-1) (historic method); or

+ 88% of the VAT, if any, due for the current period (year X) (forecast method): or

+ 100% of the VAT, if any, due for the last relevant period (year X) on the basis of the VAT transactions that
have taken place up to 20 December (effective method; i.e,, | December - 20 December),

No advance payment is due when:
