+ the last relevant period of the current calendar year (X) will not show a VAT debt. The advance payment
is due by 27 December of each year.

10.2.3. European sales listing

The European sales listing has been merged with the Intrastat obligations (only one form to be filed,
containing both the relevant data for the European sales listings and the Intrastat data). In order to simplify the
completion of the monthly Intrastat forms (see also Paragraph 10.4), completion of the columns “statistical
value”, “delivery term” and “mode of transport” are not required if the intra~Community dispatches and
acquisitions do not exceed EUR 20,000,000 per calendar year.

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

Cross-border operations must be communicated using the same form as for the electronic invoice through the
Interchange System (Sd1).

The Italian law provides for the communication of the VAT computations.

Each taxable person must communicate electronically the data of the periodical VAT computation on a
quarterly basis within the end of the second month following the quarter (i.e. for Q2, the deadline is 16
September).

‘The deadlines are as follows:

+ QI 31May;

+ Q2_ 16 September;

+ Q3 30 November

+ Q4 end of February of the following year.

The communication must be filed even when there is a VAT credit shown by the periodical VAT computation.

‘The quarterly communication of the periodical VAT computations must be filed by electronic means to the
Italian authorities in the form of an XML file prepared according to the technical specifications published on
the website of the Italian administration.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

The tax assessment period is the S" year following the year in which the annual return has been filed. If the
annual return over 2021 is filed in April 2022, the VAT due over 2021 can be assessed until 31 December
2027. If the annual return is not filed, the invoices must be kept until 31 December of the 7" year following
the year in which the annual return should have been filed.

10.3 Penalties

Administrative penalties have been reviewed by the Legislative Decree no. 87 of 2024 and the new penalties
will apply to violations committed as from 1 September 2024.

‘Asa consequence of the application of the new rules as from | September 2024, penalties applicable in case
of violations committed before and after | September 2024 are indicated.

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

The tax authorities can apply a penalty ranging from EUR 500 to EUR 2,000 (no change as from 1 September 2024),
10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

If the VAT return is not filed on time and no VAT is due, the penalty ranges from EUR 250 to BUR 2,000 (no
change as from | September 2024).

If the annual return is filed within 90 days of the deadline, it is possible to regularise the late filing by paying
a reduced penalty equal to 10% of EUR 250 (no change as from | September 2024).

Italy | 337

--- Page 154 ---
In case of failure to submit the annual return or submission of the return more than 90 days after the deadline,
if VAT is due, the penalty ranges from 120 to 240% of the total VAT owed with a minimum of EUR 250.

Tf the said violation is committed as from I September 2024, in case of failure to submit the annual return or
submission of the return more than 90 days after the deadline, if VAT is due, the penalty is equal to the 120%
of the total VAT owed with a minimum of EUR 250.

In case the omitted annual VAT return is filed within the deadline for filing the annual VAT return of the
following tax period but before any audit from the Tax Authority of which the taxpayer bas been formally
informed, the penalty ranges from 60% to 120% of the VAT with a minimum amount of EUR 200.

In case the said violation is committed after 1 September 2024 and the omitted annual VAT return is filed
within the expiration deadline of the statute of limitations (but before any audit from the Tax Authority of
which the taxpayer has been formally informed), the penalty is equal to 75% of the VAT due. In case no VAT
is due, the penalty is equal to EUR 250.

In case of omission, late or incorrect filing of the Intrastat, a penalty from EUR 500 to EUR 1,000 for each
omitted (or incorrect) Intrastat is applicable. The penalty is reduced by 50% if the Intrastat is filed within
30 days of the request of the Tax Authorities (no change as from | September 2024).

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

If VAT is not paid on time or a lower amount than the amount due is paid, a penalty of 30% of the unpaid

amount is applied. In case of payment within 15 days afler the deadline, a pro-rata (1/15) penalty reduction

for each day until the 15" day applies. In case the payment is carried out within 90 days after the deadline,

the penalty is reduced to 15%. However, it is possible to regularize the omitted payment by paying a reduced

penalty, The determination of the reduced penalty is made according to the following schedule:

* 1/10 of 30% if the payment is made within 30 days as from the deadline;

+ 1/9 of 30% if the payment is made within 90 days as from the deadline;

+ 1/8 of 30% if the payment is made after 90 days past the deadline but within the deadlinc for the filing of
the VAT return relating to the year in which the violation has been committed;

+ 1/7 of 30% if the payment is made within the deadline for the filing of the VAT return relating to the year
following the one in which the violation has been committed;

+ 1/6 of 30% if the payment is made after the deadline for the filing of the VAT return relating to the year
following the one in which the violation has been committed; and

+ 1/5 of 30% if payment is made atter the report drawn up by the Tax Authority further to the audit carried out.

The rectification implies that the reduced penalty and the unpaid VAT, including interest on delayed payment
are paid within the deadline provided for the rectification.

‘The interest for late payment of the VAT due is 2% per year as of 1 January 2025.

‘The rectification is no longer possible once a notice of assessment or a communication of irregular payments
has been received.

In case of omitted or late payment carried out as from | September 2024, a penalty of 25% of the unpaid
amount is applied. In case of payment within 15 days after the deadline, a pro-rata (1/15) penalty reduction
for each day until the 15" day applies. In case the payment is carried out within 90 days after the deadline, the
penalty is reduced to 12,5%. However, it is possible to regularize the omitted payment by paying a reduced
penalty and interest.

As a consequence of the review of the regularization regime too, the determination of the reduced penalty is

made according to the following schedule:

+ 1/10 of 25% if the payment is made within 30 days as from the deadline;

+ 1/9 of 25% if the payment is made within 90 days as from the deadline;

+ 1/8 of 25% if the payment is made after 90 days past the deadline but within the deadline for the filing of
the VAT return relating to the year in which the violation has been committed:

338 | Italy

--- Page 155 ---
+ 1/7 of 25% if the payment is made after the deadline for the filing of the VAT return relating to the year in
which the violation has been committed;

+ 1/6 of 25% if the payment is carried out after the communication of the “scheme of assessment act” not
preceded by the tax audit report and without tax settlement proposal having been filed;

+ 1/5 of 25% if the payment is carried out after the tax audit report without filing of accession to the tax
audit report and, in any event, before the communication of the “scheme of assessment act”;

+ 1/4 of 25% if the payment is carried out after the communication of the “scheme of assessment act”
relating to the violation resulting from the tax audit report without tax settlement proposal having been
filed.

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

Tn case the VAT return indicates an amount of VAT less than the one due, or the VAT return indicates that
the deductible or refundable amounts are higher than those claimable (e.g., this may be the case if VAT is
incorrectly deducted or the VAT is related to specific taxable transactions which have not been declared
and previously not documented and/or not registered, etc.), the penalty ranges from 90% to 180% of the
difference.

It is possible to rectify the incorrect return by filing an additional VAT retum and paying a reduced penalty as

follows:

+ 1/9 of the minimum (90%) if the payment is made within 90 days as from the deadline to carry out the
fulfilment;

+ V/8 of the minimum (90%) if the payment is made after 90 days past the deadline but within the deadline
for the filing of the VAT return relating to the year in which the violation has been committed;

* 1/7 of the minimum (90%) if the payment is made within the deadline for the filing of the VAT return.
relating to the year following the one in which the violation has been committed;

+ 1/6 of the minimum (90%) if the payment is made after the deadline for the filing of the VAT return
relating to the year following the one in which the violation has been committed; and

+ 1/5 of the minimum if payment is made after the tax audit report is drawn up by the Tax Authority after a
tax audit is carried out. The reduction is not applicable in case of specific violations such as the omitted
issuance of tax receipts or transport documents.

Regarding VAT returns filed as from 1 September 2024, the penalty is equal to the 70% of the difference with
a minimum amount of EUR 150.

It is possible to rectify the incorrect return by filing an additional VAT return and paying a reduced penalty.
The reductions mentioned in the previous paragraph (from 1/9 to 1/4 ) apply to the mentioned 70% penalty.

The penalty is equal to 50% if the violation arises from an integrative VAT return filed within the expiration
deadline of the statute of limitations but before any audit from the Tax Authority of which the taxpayer has
been formally performed.

A rectification is not possible once a notice of assessment has been received.

10.3.5 Offense penalties

Omitted payment

‘The failure to pay the VAT due in the VAT return, constitutes a criminal offence subject to imprisonment
from 6 months up to two years if the unpaid amount is more than € 250,000 per tax period and the payment
is not carried out before 31 December of the year following the one in which the annual VAT return has been

filed (therefore, for example, for 2023 omitted VAT payment, the penal violation applies if the payment is not
carried out before 31 December 2025).

The offence penalty does not apply in case the tax debt is being paid by instalments further to the receipt of a
communication issued by the Tax Authority (so called avviso bonario).

Italy | 339

--- Page 156 ---
In the event of forfeiture of instalments, the offence penalty applies if the residual debt is higher than EUR 75,000.

‘The offence is not punishable if the act depends on causes beyond the signatory’s control that occurred after
the collection of VAT. The court takes into account the non-transitory liquidity crisis of the signatory due to
the non-recoverability of debts due lo ascertained insolvency or over-indebtedness of third parties or to the
non-payment of certain and collectable debts by public authorities and the non-possibility of appropriate
actions to overcome the crisis.

The above offence penalty is the result of the changes introduced by the Legislative Decree no. 87 of 2024
and it applies also to violations pending as from the date of entry into force of the decree (i.e. 29 June 2024).

‘The offence shall not be punishable if, before the declaration of the opening of the trial at first instance, the
tax debts, including administrative fines and interest, have been extinguished by full payment of the amounts
due, also following the special conciliatory procedures and adherence to the assessment provided for by the
tax laws, as well as the regularization.

‘rau it VAT return through the fF invoices or other documents for non-existent tt tio1

The filing of a VAT return - where fictitious taxable passive elements are reported using invoices or other
documents for non-existent transactions in order to evade value added tax - constitutes a criminal offence
subject to from four years up to eight years imprisonment.

In case the amount of fictitious taxable passive elements is lower than EUR 100,000, the imprisonment is
from one and a half year to six years.

Fraudulent VAT return by me: f other artif

The filing of a VAT return - where positive items for an amount lower than the actual amount or fictitious
taxable passive elements are indicated by carrying out objectively or subjectively fake transactions or by
using fake documents or other fraudulent means capable of hindering the assessment and misleading the tax
authorities in order to evade value added tax - constitutes a criminal offence subject to three years up to eight
years imprisonment.

The above offence penalty applies should the below conditions jointly occur:

- the evaded tax is higher than EUR 30,000; and

- the amount of undeclared positive items, also by indicating fictitious passive elements, is higher than 5%
of the total amount of positive items declared or higher than EUR 1,500,000.

infai AY n

The filing of a VAT return - where declared supplies or non-existent passive elements are lower than the
actual ones, with the aim of evading tax — constitutes a criminal offence subject to two to four and a half years
imprisonment should the below conditions jointly occur:

- the evaded tax is higher than EUR 100,000,00; and

- the amount of supplies not declared also by indicating non-existent passive elements, is more than 10% of
the total amount of the positive items indicated in the return, or, in any case, is higher than EUR 2,000,000.

Omitted VAT return

The omitted filing of the VAT return in order to evade VAT, constitutes a criminal offence subject to two to
five years imprisonment provided that the evaded VAT is higher than EUR 50,000.

10.4 Intrastat

Separate Intrastat forms must be filed for intra-Community supplies and acquisitions of goods and services.

340 | Ttaly

--- Page 157 ---
10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

The Intrastat form regarding intra-Community acquisitions of goods and services must be filed on a monthly

basis:

+ regarding services, by taxable persons having carried out a total amount of transactions exceeding Euros
100,000 in at least one of the 4 preceding quarters; or

+ — regarding goods, by taxable persons having carried out a total amount of transactions exceeding Euros
350,000 in at least one of the 4 preceding quarters.

The Intrastat form regarding intra-Community supplies of goods and services must be filed monthly or

quarterly according to the amount of the intra-Community transactions realized during the preceding 4

quarters, In particular, Intrastat declarations (either for goods or for services) must be filed with the following

frequency:

+ ona quarterly basis by taxable persons having carried out, in the 4 preceding quarters and for each kind
(supply of goods or services), a total amount of transactions not exceeding EUR 50,000 per quarter; and

+ onamonthly basis, by taxable persons who have exceeded the above threshold.

The filing of the data for statistical purposes is compulsory in the event that the taxable persons have realised,
in the four quarters preceding the reference quarter, a quarterly total amount of intra-Community supplies of
goods or services exceeding EUR 100,000.

In the case of activity which lasted less than 4 quarters, the Intrastat must be filed quarterly provided that the
first condition mentioned above is fulfilled.

Should the threshold be exceeded during a quarter, the Intrastat must be filed monthly as of the month
following the month in which the threshold has been exceeded.

In the above case, the Intrastat forms relating to the previous months, duly marked, must be filed.

Intrastat must be filed exclusively by electronic means within 25 days following the end of the reporting
period,

The call-off stock operations are communicated too.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

The information which must be reported for both the arrival and the dispatch statistics is:
+ VAT number supplier/purchaser;

+ EU Member State of origin/destination;

+ code of the province of origin/destination;
* — supplementary unit in some cases;

+ nature of the transaction;

+ delivery term;

+ mode of transport;

+ $-digit commodity code of the goods;

+ invoice value of the goods in EUR;

+ net weight of the goods; and

+ statistical value.

Latvia

The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented through the “Value Added Tax Law’ (hereafter
referred to as the “Latvian VAT Law” or “VAT Law”). In Latvia, the VAT is referred to as “Pievienotas
vértibas nodoklis” or “PVN”.

As of | January 2013, a completely new VAT Law came into force. The new VAT Law was implemented
to make the VAT provisions more compliant with the EU VAT Directive, by following the structure and
terminology of the EU VAT Directive.

Latvian value added tax law in force: https://tinyurl.com/3347irev (in Latvian) h¢tps://tinyurl.com/Sewvvdny
(in English version all amendments not included though).

1. Taxable Persons

According to the Latvian VAT Law, any person who independently carries out any economic activity in any
place, whatever the purpose or result of that activity, is considered a taxable person. Taxable persons can be
either registered or not registered with the tax authorities.

LL Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

If the total value of taxable supplies of goods and services provided by domestic taxable person has not
exceeded EUR 50,000 during the preceding 12 months, no registration is required. Taxable persons can,
however, register before reaching this threshold.

The registration is with the Latvian State Revenue Service (SRS). The following documents must be provided
for the registration:

+ aregistration form (the registration form can be downloaded on https://tinyurl.com/3eutnsmn),

+ ifan individual is registering, a copy of his/her passport or some other official identification; and

+ _ if'an authorized person performs the registration, a power of attorney.

12 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

Generally there is no registration threshold for foreign legal entities unless the SME Scheme is applied. The
threshold for application of the SME scheme is EUR 50,000. The foreign taxable person’s total turnover in
the EU must not exceed EUR 100,000.

When registering, the following documents must be submitted:

*  aregistration form;

* the extract from the Commercial Register; and

+ if the authorized person performs the registration, a power of attomey.

The registration form can be submitted via the Electronic Declaration System (EDS), or sent via email by
using a secure electronic signature to vid@vid.gov.lv.

The registration forms can be downloaded at: hitps.//tinyurl.com/w7kn2a2x

According to the law the VAT registration should be done in 5 working days after the submission of all the
required documents to register for VAT. In practice, however, the actual registration time is extended due to
additional questions from the tax authorities in ascertaining the objectivity of planned activities. Thus, lately
the procedure usually takes two to three weeks, depending on how fast additional questions can be addressed
and additionally requested documents provided (if at all).

In some circumstances registration can be avoided, because of the application of the reverse charge
mechanism or the appointment of a fiscal representative.

Latvia | 343

--- Page 160 ---
13 VAT Grouping

The conditions for the registration of a Latvian VAT group are: the participants of the VAT group may only
be taxable persons, the maximum number of participants in Lhe VAT group is not limited and a taxable person
may not be a participant of another VAT group at the same time.

A VAT group may be established if the total value of the taxable supplies of goods and services provided by
at least one participant of the VAT group during the preceding 12 calendar months up to the month when the
application for the registration of the VAT group is submitted, is at least EUR 350,000.

‘The participants of the VAT group can only be companies registered for VAT purposes in Latvia. Also, they
must be included in one group of companies (in accordance with the Group of Companies Law) or a branch
ofa foreign merchant (legal person), provided that the foreign merchant, in accordance with the Group of
Companies Law, is a member of the same group of companies in which the other participants of the VAT
group are included.

Holding companies receiving only dividend income cannot be included in the VAT group. Foreign companies
cannot be included in the VAT group, even if they have a fixed establishment in Latvia, However, if the
foreign company registers a branch in Latvia, this branch could be inchuded in the VAT group provided that
the above conditions stipulated in the Group of Companies Law are met,

‘The VAT group members must enter into an agreement. This agreement must be entered by all the members
with one of them being appointed as the principal entity, the hcad company of the VAT group.

VAT grouping is optional. A VAT group is regarded as one taxable person for any supply to or by the VAT
group. For VAT purposes, supplies of goods or services between members of a VAT group do not fall within
the scope of VAT. Supplies of goods or services made by third parties to an individual member of a VAT
group are deemed to have been made to the VAT group. A taxable person can withdraw from the VAT Group
not earlier than 12 calendar months after it joined the VAT group, unless other conditions for the VAT group
are no longer met.

The VAT group members use their own VAT registration number for transactions with taxable persons which
are not members of the group. The registration number granted to the VAT group shall be used only for filing
the VAT return of the VAT group and for the payment of the VAT. Group members are jointly and severally
liable for payment of the group's VAT.

14 Fiscal Representative

Latvia has introduced the concept of fiscal representation, A fiscal representative is a taxable person who,
based on a wrilten agreement, is liable for paying VAT to the tax authorities and represents a taxable person
from another EU Member State or a non-EU country for tax purposes in the following transactions:

+ when importing goods followed by an intra-Community supply;

+ when importing goods followed by a domestic supply;

* on receipt of goods domestically, if the goods have been received for the purpose of exporting them
further and placed in a customs warehouse or a tax warehouse, and in the course of the subsequent
exportation of those goods;

+ when purchasing goods in the territory of the European Union, if these goods have been purchased for the
purpose of exporting them further and placed in a customs warehouse or tax warehouse, and in the course
of subsequent exportation of those goods; and

+ when goods are purchased in the territory of the European Union, where they are actually received
domestically for the purpose of supplying them to another Member State.

Both non-EU persons and EU taxable persons may - but are not obliged - to appoint a fiscal representative
when registering for VAT.

The requirements for a fiscal representative are that he:
+ should file one of the following:
* abank certification that he has deposited enough funds to cover the potential tax debts - at least 20%

344 | Latvia

--- Page 161 ---
of the average VAT taxable transactions value reported in the VAT returns of the previous 3 taxation
period tax declarations and no less than EUR 14,200; or
+ a bank or insurance company guarantee of at least EUR 285,000;
+ should be registered as a taxable person in Latvia for a time period of at least 2 years and have performed
business activities in Latvia uninterrupted since his VAT registration;
+ does not have any outstanding tax debts when submitting the VAT registration application or does, by
decision of the SRS, have an arrangement to divide the term for the payment of tax debts into instalments;
+ does not have a criminal record for fraud, falsification of documents, tax avoidance or criminal activities
that could result in a reduction of the taxable amount;
* files the tax returns and the information requested by the SRS in due terms;
+ is registered as the user of the EDS; and
+ can be contacted at its legal address.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

The transfer of business is not considered a supply of goods or services if the following criteria are met:

+ the transferee takes over the rights and liabilities of the transferor of the business; and

+ the transferee continues the operations of the business and these operations are not connected to the
liquidation of the business or sale of the received assets.

The general rules for transfer of business are provided by the Latvian Commercial Law. It should be note
that according to the Latvian Commercial Law, the transferor and transferee are jointly liable for the liabilities
incurred prior to the transfer of the business for 5 years.

It should also be noted that the parties have to notify the Latvian tax authorities within 30 days as of the
transfer of business in order to avoid input VAT correction for the transferor.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

Under Article 7 of the Latvian VAT Law, the provision of samples for business purposes or certain low value
gifts are not taxed while the supplier retains his right of deduction.

Under Latvian VAT Law a low value gift refers to goods or services which are given without consideration
and the value of which without the tax does not exceed EUR 15 during a calendar year per one person.
However, goods or services related to advertising or representation expenses are excluded.

2.3 Importation

2.3.1. Importation followed by an intra~Community supply
It is possible to apply an exemption on the importation of goods into Latvia if it is followed by an intra-
Community supply within 30 days.

Conditions have to be met in order to apply the exemption, for example, that the name of the taxable person
in the EU to which the goods will subsequently be delivered, and its VAT registration number be shown on
the delivery/import documents,

Postponed accounting on importation

is a special importation regime for the import of goods in Latvia providing for import VAT to be
deferred to the VAT return. In order to apply the special importation regime for imported goods, a taxable
person must submit an application to the tax authorities, With this licence, importers avoid payment of VAT
at the time of importation, Instead, the importer will have to pay the VAT due in its Latvian VAT return. If the
importer is entitled to deduct VAT, the VAT paid can be reclaimed in the same VAT return.

Latvia | 345

--- Page 162 ---
The licence to apply the VAT deferment regime for imported goods will be issued to a taxable person if:

«he is registered with the Latvian Commercial Register or registered with the SRS as a performer of
business activities;

+ he has carried out economic activities domestically for at least previous 12 months and has been
registered as Latvian VAT payer;

+ he has sufficient technical and financial resources (o perform the particular type of economic activity;

+ he does not have tax debts on the day of submission of the application for the licence;

+ during previous 12 months provided tax and informative declarations and financial statements within
the time periods specified in the laws, as well as provided additional information within the time periods
specified by the SRS, which is necessary for determining the amount of tax to be paid to the State budget
or overpayment of tax;

+ persons that have representation rights have no criminal record of fraud, falsification of documents,
avoidance of taxes and the payment of taxes compared thereto or other criminal offences which may
affect the determination of the amount of taxes;

+ the representative of the VAT payer (if the VAT payer is a legal person) or the VAT payer himself (if the
VAT payer is an individual) is not regarded a person of risk (determined in accordance with the Law On
Taxes and Fee);

+ it is registered as a user of EDS system of the SRS.

Special importation regime is also applicable to the importation of fixed assets no licence is required then.

‘The licence is not issued to companies not established in Latvia.

SL Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

There was a simplification in regard of call-off stock introduced in Latvia. Also, as of 2020 new rules in
regard of call-off stock were introduced, in line with the EU VAT Directive.

The transfer of goods by a VAT registered taxable person from one EU Member State to Latvia to create

a stock of goods from which their customer can ‘call-off” (use and pay for) the goods as and when they
require them, can be described as call-off stock. If certain conditions are met, the supplier does not perform a
transfer of own goods followed by a local supply, but an intracommunity supply to his customer. Latvian VAT
legislation is in line with the articles newly introduced in the EU VAT Directive. The tax authorilies generally
agree with the content of the Explanatory Notes with regard to the 2020 Quick Fixes.

3.2 Installation supplies

Installation supplies are treated as supplied where they are constructed or installed. In Latvia, there is no
formal definition of the term “supply with installation or assembly”. In determining whether the services are
related to immovable property or installation services are provided, the authorities examine the works actually
performed.

The reverse charge applies if a non-established company that is not registered for VAT in Latvia supplies
goods with installation in Latvia and the customer is VAT registered in Latvia.

If the goods are supplied to a company or individual that is not VAT registered in Latvia, the supplier must
register as a taxable person in Latvia.

33 Distance sales

On or after 1 July 2021, registration may be simplified for distances sellers from within the EU, and for
distance sellers of low-value goods from outside the EU, under the EU-wide “One Stop Shop” (OSS) regime.
As such, the rules and thresholds for distance sales (both intra-Community distance sales and distance sales
of imported goods) have been harmonized across the EU with no leeway for Member States. The distance
selling threshold for sales to customers in other EU Member States without a VAT registration number

346 | Latvia

--- Page 163 ---
(e.g, private individuals) is now EUR 10,000 for intra-Commmunity distance sales. Turnover generated from
the supply of ‘electronically supplied services’, telecommunication services and broadcasting services are
included in the EUR 10,000 threshold. A threshold of EUR 150 per consignment applies for the application of
the special scheme for distance sales of imported goods.

If OSS regime is not chosen, a foreign taxable person must register for VAT in Latvia:

+ within 30 days from the day when the total value of the sales in the previous or current calendar year has
exceeded the threshold; or

+ before the transaction, if the goods are subject to excise duties in Latvia, irrespective to the value of
excise goods.

A foreign taxable person opting to register must file an application, The option is binding, implying that
Latvian VAT must be applied starting from the date the person is registered for Latvian VAT. No special
requirements apply.

3.4 Simplified triangulation

Under Latvian VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rufes have been implemented in line with the EU
VAT Directive Article 141

‘Taxable persons A, B and C in the ABC transaction must be registered for VAT in different EU countries (i.e. in
Member States 1, 2 and 3). The transport must be organized by either party A or party B. If party B arranged the
transport, B must have provided A with a valid VAT ID number of another country than the country of dispatch

and arrival. The goods must be transported directly from A in Country 1 to C in Country 3.

If Latvia is the country of arrival, the triangulation simplification cannot be applied if B is VAT registered in
Latvia. In such situation B should report an intra-Community acquisition of goods followed by a domestic
supply subject to Latvian VAT.

Latvian VAT law does not provide for limitations to apply the triangulation simplification if party B happens
to be VAT registered in the country of dispatch.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

‘The acquisition threshold for non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons engaged in exempt activities and flat-
rate farmers from suppliers established in another EU Member State is EUR 10,000.

3.6 Effective Use & Enjoyment

Use and enjoyment provisions have been implemented in the VAT Law.
The general use and enjoyment rule as per the Latvian legislation provides that:

+ Art. 59a(a): where the supply of relevant services would otherwise be regarded as made in Latvia, but
the effective use and enjoyment of those services is outside the Community, the supply is deemed to be
outside of the Community; and

+ Art, 59a(b): where the supply of relevant services would otherwise be regarded as made outside the
Community, but the effective use and enjoyment of the services is in Latvia, the place of supply is
deemed to be Latvia.

The special provisions for effective use and enjoyment as per (a) and (b) above, apply to:
+ short-term hiring of means of transport, B2B and B2C;

+ hiring of movable goods, other than means of transport, B2B and B2C; and

+ transport of goods services, other than intra~Community transport of goods, B2B.

Latvia | 347

--- Page 164 ---
4. Chargeable event & Chargeability of VAT

41 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods
or services are supplied and the VAT invoice is issued. [f an invoice is issued or payment is received before
the goods or services are supplied, VAT becomes chargeable on the date of issuing the invoice. Invoices shall
be issued no later than 15 days after the chargeable event occurred.

The VAT on the purchase of a service, which falls under the main rules of Articles 44 and 196 of the EU VAT
Directive, is nevertheless due at the time the service is supplied.

42 Advance payments

Where a payment is to be made on account before the goods or services are supplied, VAT becomes
chargeable on receipt of the payment.

4.3 Continuous supplies

Generally, the time of supply and the date at which VAT is chargeable for a supply of goods, is the date the
goods are supplied or transportation begins, but not later than the date the buyer receives them. In the case
ofa continuous supply of goods and/or services over a long period (excluding hire purchase) resulting in
periodic tax invoices or payments for deliveries, the transaction is deemed to have taken place at the end of
the period covered by the invoices or payments, but at least every six months.

Where the supply of goods within the territory of the EU takes place permanently over a continuous time
period and exceeds one calendar month, it shall be deemed that the transaction has occurred in the end of each
calendar month until the time when the supply of goods is completely finished.

4.4 Cash accounting

Upon request, the tax authorities may permit a taxable person to opt to apply a cash accounting scheme for

the payment of the VAT, if:

+ the total turnover in the previous calendar year did not exceed EUR 100,000; or

+ when registering for VAT purposes, the amount of planned transactions is less than EUR 100,000 in the
taxation year.

The cash accounting can still be used by the following VAT taxable persons:
+ a registered taxable person whose total transaction value in the previous tax year has reached
EUR 100,000 but has not exceeded EUR 500,000 and who is:
+ a registered taxpayer who operates in the fisheries sector - in relation to supplies of fresh, frozen or
refrigerated fish and crustaceans; or
* a producer of agricultural products or a cooperative society of agricultural services - in relation to the
supply of such agricultural products: (a) live animals, (b) milk and dairy products, (c) bird eggs, (d)
natural honey, (e) vegetables, root plants, tuberous plants, (f) cereal products, (g) oilseeds and fruit,
miscellaneous grains, seeds and fruit.
+ a registered taxable person whose total transaction value in the previous tax year has reached
EUR 100,000 but has not exceeded EUR 2,000,000 and who provides residential house management
services, in relation to the provision of residential house management services.

The total amount of transaction value shall not include the value of fixed assets and intangible investments
supplied by the taxpayer, if the taxpayer performs such supply once within a period of 12 months.

Where the scheme applies, output VAT is chargeable when the payment is received, and input VAT is
deductible when the payment is made. If cash accounting is used, the remark ‘cash accounting” should be
indicated on the invoice.

348 | Latvia

--- Page 165 ---
5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad Debt Relief

A taxpayer can reduce its VAT liability for bad debt VAT, provided that certain criteria are met:

+ the goods or services supplied are invoiced;

+ the debt has been incurred within the last three tax years;

+ the transaction has been subject to VAT and is shown on the tax return for the tax period concerned;

+ the amount of the debt has been written off from the specific provision for bad debts or directly to losses
(expense) in the accounts of the taxable person in the current tax period or in a previous tax period;

+ the recipient of the goods or services is not a related person within the meaning of the Law on Taxes and
Duties;

+ the supply of goods or services to the relevant recipient has been interrupted for at least three months and
has not been resumed; and

+ the registered taxable person has not transferred (assigned) his right of claim to another person.

The supplier must establish that he or she took measures to recover the lost debt, and must notify the recipient
that the debt in question is considered as lost. The claim can be done in any (monthly) VAT return.

For bad debts of exceeding EUR 1,000 (excluding VAT), in addition to meeting the conditions described
above, the taxpayer must also obtain a court decision for collection of the debt and a statement from a court
bailiff that collection is not possible.

5.2 Cash Discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice

“Supply of goods EUR 1000

21% VAT EUR 210

Total to be paid — EUR 1210

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days,y ou can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount.”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days.

If the invoice states the transaction and VAT amount with the discount, namely, the amounts taking into
account the 2% cash discount, no separate credit invoice is necessary.

If however, the amounts are not shown on the invoice, the supplier must send a credit invoice for the 2%
discount. Thus the overpaid VAT can be recovered and input VAT adjusted accordingly.

5.3 Open Market Value

The supply of goods or provision of services between related parties must be set at the market value if the

transaction value is:

+ less than market value and the recipient is not entitled to deduct imput VAT in full;

+ less than the market value and the supplier does not have the right to deduct input VAT in full and the
supply of goods or services is exempt; or

+ more than market value and the supplier is not entitled to deduct input VAT in full.

“Market value” is, generally, the price that would have to be paid to an unrelated buyer in circumstances of

fair competition. Where no comparable price for goods or services can be ascertained, market value shall

mean:

* in respect of goods — an amount which is not less than the purchase price of the relevant goods or of
similar goods or, in the absence of a purchase price, the cost price, determined at the time of supply; or

* in respect of services — an amount that is not less than the full cost of providing the service.

Latvia | 349

--- Page 166 ---
54 Returnable Packaging

If the deposit fee may be reimbursed to the buyer, the Latvian VAT Law provides businesses not to include
the cost of returnable packaging in the taxable amount of the total supply, In cases where the packaging is
pot returned, supplier must pay back the VAT at book value of the unreturned packaging when submitting the
annual VAT return

6. VAT rates

6.1 Standard rate

The standard rate in Latvia is 21%,

6.2 Reduced rate

The Latvian VAT Law provides for a First Reduced Rate of 12% and a Second Reduced Rate of 5%, subject
to certain conditions and requirements. The First Reduced Rate applies to certain essential supplies such as
registered medications and baby food. The Second Reduced Rate generally applies to printed and electronic
literature and publications.

First Reduced Rate of 12% applies to:

+ registered medicinal products, registered medical devices and parts;

+ specialised food and products for infants;

* domestic passenger and baggage transport;

* tourist accommodation;

+ timber products used for domestic heating:

+ other types of heating energy used for domestic purposes; and

+ fresh fruit, berries and vegetables, including washed, pecled, peeled cut and prepacked but not cooked or
otherwise (e.g, frozen, salted, dried) — the list of fruits and vegetables subject to the First Reduced Rate of
12% is included in the legal acts and will be applicable until 31 December 2024.

Second Reduced Rate of 5% applies to:
* education literature and original literature ~ both physical and digital;

+ colouring books, sheet music, maps and other such printed and digital works; and
+ newspapers, magazines and other periodicals, including annexes to such works.

6.3 Super-reduced and zero rates

Latvia has not introduced a 0% VAT rate on supplies of goods or services other than the zero rates mentioned
below in Chapter 7.

7. Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with the right of deduction

7.1.1 Intracommunity supplies

In order for an intra-Community supply to qualify for the zero rate, the following two conditions must be met:

+ the customer to whom the supply is made must provide his or her VAT identification number to the
supplier; and

+ the transportation of the goods from Latvia to the other Member State must be confirmed by the relevant
transportation documents.

In addition, the supplier must comply with the obligation to submit a duly completed EC Sales list. In the case
of transfers of own stock, an internal invoice is required for reporting purposes. In Latvia it is an obligation to
report each supply or acquisition as per invoice number and date.

350 I Latvia

--- Page 167 ---
7.1.2 Export supplies
Exports of goods are generally zero-rated for VAT purposes.

This enables the supplier to recover input VAT on exports or dispatches of goods or services with a place of
supply outside Latvia.

The supply of goods from Latvia to a taxable person in another Member State is also generally zero-rated.

However, when goods are supplied to a consumer in another Member State (i.e., a person not registered for
VAT), Latvian VAT is generally applied in the same way as if the supply was made to a Latvian customer,
subject to the distance selling rules discussed in paragraph 3.3.

7.1.3. Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse
There is no concept of VAT warehouses introduced in the Latvian VAT law.

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse
An excise warehouse permits the storage and handling of goods subject to excise duties. As of 2022 it
is possible in Latvia to store goods in a joint customs and excise warehouse. For more information see
paragraph 7.1.3.3. below.

7.1.3.3 Bonded warehouse

It is possible to put the goods into a bonded warehouse regime if the goods are not yet imported into Latvia or
if the goods will be further exported or are intended for cargo consolidation (e.g. when the goods are stored
while the ship, railways or other vehicle is being loaded fully before actual export out of EU).

Supplies of goods performed in customs warehouses are subject to 0% VAT if the goods are:
«received from non-EU countries and not released for free circulation inland; or
«received from other EU countries and for which the export procedure is started in other EU countries.

The parties responsible for payment of the goods are:

+ the holder of the customs warehouse;

+ the owner of the goods; and

+ the party providing guarantee for the tax authorities,

In case of tax debt, the authorities would approach all of these parties. Generally, the order of approaching
them depends on the type of customs warehouse and the agreements between the owner of the goods and the
warehouse,

7.2 Exemptions without the right to deduction

‘The following supplies are exempt without the right of deduction:

* insurance and re-insurance services;

+ financial services:

+ _ sale of real estate, except unused real estate and building land — option to tax applies if the supplier and
customer are both taxable persons;

+ rent of premises to private individuals;

+ investment funds’ management;

+ gambling and lotteries;

* — services between the members of cost-sharing associations;

+ medical services supplied to a patient (a list of the exempt services is defined);

+ services related to medical services (transportation of the patient with specialized vehicles,
accommodation and catering in medical institutions during treatment, diagnostic services occurring in
other medical institutions);

* postal services;

+ supply of human organs, milk and blood;

* dental services and services of dental technicians;

Latvia | 351

--- Page 168 ---
+ social services;

+ educational services supplied by state approved educational institutions and kindergartens;

+ fees for children staying in children’s camps organised in accordance with the requirements of the laws
and regulations governing education;

+ supplies of goods and services by public benefit organizations (special registration is required) for the
purpose of defending children’s and young persons” rights;

+ participation fees for sports competitions and practices organised by non-profit associations and
foundations;

+ cultural services supplied by theatres and circuses, museums, libraries, also concerts, events for children;
and

+ supply of gold and cash to the Bank of Latvia.

Input VAT deduction is allowed for insurance and financial services provided to customers outside the EU.

7.3 Option for taxation

Latvia provides for the possibility to opt to tax the supply of used immovable property if the seller and buyer are
both Latvian VAT registered persons and the seller has registered the property with the SRS prior to the sale. It
is possible to opt for all types of immovable property(including residential property) as long as it’s “used”.

In Latvia, there is no possibility to opt to tax financial services.

8 Liability

81 Reverse charges

In Latvia there are reverse charge rules for general B2B services provided by foreign entities to taxable
persons and legal entities with a Latvian VAT 1D number. This reverse charge applies in all EU Member
States and follows from Articles 44 and 196 EU VAT Directive. There is also (he reverse charge for the supply
of natural gas and clectricity supplied by non-resident suppliers to “taxable dealers” in Latvia registered for
VAT purposes in Latvia (Article 195 EU VAT Directive).

8.1.1 General reverse charge — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)
Art, 194 of the EU VAT Directive is not introduced in Latvia in relation to the domestic supply of goods,

As regards the supplies of services, the Latvian VAT law provides, that the recipient of the services who is

a VAT taxable person must reverse charge the VAT for the following services received from a non-resident

supplier:

- services related to immovable property (defined in Art. 47 of the EU VAT Directive);

- cultural, artistic, sporting, scientific, educational, entertainment and similar services (defined in Art. 53 of
the EU VAT Directive).

At the same time, the VAT law also provides for an obligation for the foreign service providers to register for
VAT in Latvia if they supply services related to immovable property and cultural etc. kind of services. The
foreign supplier supplying these types of services, should VAT register in Latvia prior to commencing the
supply of these services.

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 Directive)

Latvia applies the reverse charge on the following domestic supplies between VAT taxable persons:

+ construction services;

+ certain supplies of scrap metal and related services;

+ supplies of mobile phones, tablet PCs, laptop computers, electrical components and game consoles. The
domestic reverse charge does not apply to electronic accessories, e.g., batteries, connection cables and.
charges, if they are sold separately;

+ supplies of certain types of timber, including wood intended for use as fuel;

+ certain supplies of cereals and industrial crops (including oilseeds);

352 | Latvia

--- Page 169 ---
+ certain supplies of unwrought precious metals, precious metal alloys and their semifinished products, and
metal coated with precious metal; and
+ certain supplies of semi-finished products of ferrous and nonferrous metals,

Only non-cash money settlements are possible in order to apply local reverse charge to the supplies
mentioned.

8.2 Joint and several liability

Under Latvian law, the management of a company is not jointly and severally liable for the unpaid taxes
of the businesses they manage in default. However, tax authorities can request unpaid taxes from the
management of the company, if all of the criteria are fulfilled:

+ the amount of arrears exceeds the total of 50 minimum monthly salaries in the Republic of Latvia;

+ the decision on the recovery of arrears has been notified to the legal entity;

+ it has been established that after the decision to conduct a tax audit (audit) has been taken or after the
initiation of a tax audit (control), as well as after the arrears have been created, the legal entity has
alienated the assets it owns and as a result of the actions or inactions of a member of the Management
Board, the arrears of the legal entity have not been paid in full within the time limits established by the
law;

+ a deed of impossibility of recovery has been drawn up; and

+ the legal person has failed to comply with the obligation under the Insolvency Law to file for insolvency
proceedings.

Joint and several liability exists for businesses that form part of a VAT group for the VAT debts of each
member of the VAT group for the entire period that the relevant entity was part of that VAT group.

A fiscal representative is responsible for the tax liability arising out of documents and tax returns they submit
and the taxpayer and/or the bailiff is responsible if property is sold at auction and monies due to the State are
not paid.

Other express abuse provisions or chain transaction provisions are not expressly addressed in the law. As a
general rule, a counter-party of a third party cannot be held liable for a principal’s VAT obligations, e.g., in
“carousel fraud.” missing taxpayer transactions.

9. Recovery of input VAT

OL Non-recoverable input VAT

Some input tax is specifically irrecoverable, for example:

+ VAT paid on goods or services acquired for non-business use;

+ 60% of the input tax from representation expenses (e.g., business meals, public functions, receptions and
generally occasions that involve or include customers, clients, etc.);

+ VAT paid on the purchase, lease and import of a luxury car with a value of more than EUR 75,000
(excluding VAT);

+ VAT paid for fuel and running costs for luxury passenger cars for the first 60 months as of purchase.
However, deductions are not denied if the car or truck is used for certain qualifying purposes, e.g.,
transportation, vehicle hiring, emergency vehicle services or a car sale or hire purchase business;

+ 50% of the VAT paid on passenger cars (except for businesses which primarily use cars. e.g., taxi services,
car rentals and emergency vehicle services) and related maintenance services and costs, including fuel;
and

+ VAT paid on items relating to a business’s sales of goods or services that are exempt from VAT.

9.2 Excess input VAT

When input VAT exceeds output VAT, overpayment VAT is reflected in the VAT return. Overpaid VAT is
evaluated on a monthly basis and refunded into the taxpayer’s bank account within 30 days of the submission
of the VAT return to the SRS.

Latvia | 353

--- Page 170 ---
‘A taxpayer can make corrections to submitted VAT returns up to 3 years after the date when the tax was due
0 as to obtain a refund, provided an audit has not begun or curried out in connection with the relevant period.

9.3. Adjustment periods

The Latvian VAT Law provides the following adjustment periods:
+ Immovable property - 10 years;
+ Movable property - 5 years (if the value without VAT exceeds EUR 70,000).

Latvia does not have an adjustment period for services.

9.4 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

The claim should be filed electronically with the tax authorities of the country where the taxable person is
established.

‘The minimum limit for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year is
EUR 50. The minimum interim limit for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least 3
months is EUR 400.

The minimum period for claiming a refund is 3 months, except the last months of the calendar year ~ then
claims for a shorter time period are accepted. Maximum period is one calendar year.

The claim should be filed by 30 September of the following year. Claims filed after the deadline are not
accepted. When considering the refund claims, Latvian tax authorities often request invoice copies for the
expenses and additional explanations. Sometimes the authorities request originals. The time limit for a
decision on a refund is 4 months. If additional information is requested, the refund decision is issued within 6
months after filing the claim. If additional information is requested for the second time, the time limit for the
decision is 8 months.

The refunded amounts are transferred within 10 working days but no later than 6 or 8 months after filing

the claim (depending on the number of additional information explanation requests). If the tax authorities”
decision is negative, the appeal should be filed within one month after the receipt of the decision. The date of
sending the decision via electronic systems is considered the date of receipt.

The VAT incurred for the following expenses is not refunded:

+ for tax amounts included in an invoice issued by the taxable person that does not conform with the
requirements set out by the VAT law;

+ for a transaction that has not happened;

+ — for goods or services related to immovable property located in Latvia (unless the received services are
used for transactions where the recipient of the services is liable for payment of the tax);

+ expenses for goods and services received for personal use— catering and use of cars; and

+ expenses incurred by a tour operator acting under the tour operators margin scheme for its services
inchided in a tour package.

Detailed information can be found on the official Latvian tax authorities’ website: https.//tinyurl.com/Swtcdxey

95 VAT refunds to non-EU taxable persons

VAT is only refunded to non-EU taxable persons if a reciprocity agreement exists. Currently, Latvia has
concluded reciprocity agreements with Switzerland, Iceland, Monaco and Norway. Reciprocity agreement
with the UK is in process.

Electronic filing for such an application is not possible, as original supporting docuwsents must be filed to the
Latvian tax authorities.

‘The claim should be filed on paper, in Latvian or English. The applicant should either send the application by
post or file it personally at the following address:

The claim can be signed by the authorized signatories of the taxable person or authorized persons (an original
power of attorney is required).

‘The application form can be downloaded at: hrtps://tinyurl.com/mn7m846n.
‘The minimum limit for claims made for a calendar year or the reminder ofa calendar year is EUR 50.

‘The interim minimum limit for claims made for less than a calendar year but at least 3 months is EUR 400.
‘The maximum period for making a claim is one calendar year.

The minimum period for making a claim is 3 months, except if the claim is made for the last months of the
calendar year — then claims for a shorter time period are accepted.

The deadline for making the claim is 30 September of the following year. Claims filed after the deadline are
not accepted.

The following supporting documents are required:

+ original invoices for which the VAT refund is claimed;

+ original importation documents if the refund is claimed for the import of goods;

* payment documents (bank orders of payment, cash receipts);

* original VAT registration certificate, valid for 12 months from the date of issue; and
+ original power of attorney if the claim is filed by an authorized person.

The tax authorities will decide on the refimd within 4 months. If the tax authorities request additional
information, then the deadline for the decision can be extended up to 8 months. The refund is processed
within 10 working days after the decision is taken but no later than within 4 months from receipt of the claim
(or 8 months if additional information has been requested).

The appeal of the tax authorities’ decision should be filed within one month after the receipt of the decision.

VAT is not refunded for the following expenses:

* for tax amounts indicated in tax invoices which do not satisfy the requirements of VAT law;

«for transactions which did not take place;

+ — for goods or services related to immovable property located in Latvia (unless the received services are
used for transactions where the recipient of the services is liable for payment of the tax);

+ for goods or services received for personal use (car expenses, catering, entertainment); and

+ for goods or services supplied to a tour operator applying the tour operators margin scheme.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

Latvian VAT-registered businesses must supply VAT invoices to VAT-registered customers for all their
taxable supplies (including zero-rated sales, but not exempt sales without input credit) and may incur a fine
if they do not do so when requested by a VAT-registered customer or the SRS. Invoices or other supporting
documentation containing necessary information are required for VAT accounting purposes and to support
input VAT deductions. As an anti-fraud measure, certain types of taxpayers are required to use cash registers.
Taxpayers such as restaurants, shops, etc. who accept cash payments are obliged to use cash registers. The
cash registers must meet certain technical requirements, among other things, to issue VAT invoices. Some
taxpayers (¢.g. private doctors, lawyers, craftsmen) are allowed not to use cash registers, but should issue an
e-receipt.

Latvia 1 355

--- Page 172 ---
There are certain circumstances in which a supplier should not issue a VAT invoice (e.g,, suppliers that are not
registered for VAT or for certain special billing schemes). In addition, invoices do not need to be issued for
supplics made under the One Stop Shop (OSS) unless specifically requested by the customer.

10.1.1 Invoice content

A VAT invoice must contain the following information:

+ date when the invoice was issued;

*« _ sequential numbering of the invoice, which provides a unique identification of the invoice;

* name (natural person - given name, surname, personal identity number) and legal address (natural person
declared place of residence) of the supplier of the goods or the services;

+ VAT registration number of the supplier of the goods or provider of the services;

+ name (natural person - given name, surname, personal identity number) and legal address (natural person
declared place of residence) of the customer;

+ VAT registration number of the customer if the customer is liable for VAT and for intra-Community
supplies;

+ — date of the supply of the goods or date of the provision of the service if such differs from the date of issue
of the invoice, or the date when a payment has been received in advance if such date is known and it
differs from the issue date of the invoice;

+ description, amount (volume) and unit of measurement of the goods supplied or services provided;

+ price (value of one unit without VAT) of the goods supplied or services provided;

+ discounts applied if they are not deducted from the unit price;

+ VAT rate applied;

+ calculated amount of VAT payable;

* the total of the transaction without VAT;

+ incase of self-billing a corresponding reference “pasaprékins” is required;

+ the information necessary to determine whether there is a new means of transport;

+ special wording or reference to the VAT law or the directive, if 0% VAT, reverse charge, or another special
regime applies, for instance:

+ recipient of goods or services — nodoka apgriezta maksa¥ana;

+ special procedure of paying the tax and deduction of input tax is applied (cash accounting} naudas
lidzek{u uzskaite;

+ intra-Community supply — pretu piegiide Eiropas Savieniba;

* export supply - eksports;

+ margin scheme - second-hand goods - pe]gas dalas rezims lietotfim precém;

+ margin scheme - works of art - pelnas dalas reZims makslas darbiem:

+ margin scheme - collector’s items and antiques - pe]yas dalas rezims kolekciju priekSmetiem un
senlietim;

* tour operator margin scheme - pe|nas dalas reZims celojumu agentiram.

+ ifthe authorized representative is responsible for making the tax payment — the registration number of the
representative in the SRS VAT payer register, a name of legal person (name and family name of a natural
person) and legal address (declared residence for a natural person); and

+ if the fiscal agent is responsible for paying the VAT - the registration number of the representative in the
SRS VAT Payer Register or in a taxpayer register of another Member State, a name of legal person (name
and family name of a natural person) and legal address (declared residence for a natural person).

10.1.2 Time limits

For domestic supplies, an invoice should be issued within 15 days after the supply or receipt of the advance
payment. For continuous domestic services, an invoice should be issued at least every 6 months. For EU
supplies of goods and services taxable according to Article 44 of Directive 2006/112/EC, the invoices should
be issued by the 15" day of the month following the supply. For continuous (longer than a year) services
subject to reverse charge according to Article 44 of Directive 2006/112/EC, the service received and VAT
calculated should be reported at least once at the end of each calendar year.

10.1.3 Language

‘There are no official requirements for the language or currency of invoices, however invoices in languages
other than Latvian must be translated on request.

356 | Latvia

--- Page 173 ---
10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

Foreign currencies should be converted to Euro using the official exchange rate by the European Central Bank
on the date of the issue of the invoice.

10.1.5  Self— billing

Under Latvian VAT law, self-billing is allowed in case there is an agreement between the two parties (for
instance a contract or written agreement).

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

A registered taxable person is allowed to issue a simplified invoice in the following cases:

* for an inland transaction the value of which excluding tax is less than EUR 150; and

+ in case of amendments made in the initial invoice. In this case the simplified invoice must indicate the
identification number and date of issue of the initial tax invoice, as well as the particular data to be
amended in the additional simplified tax invoice.

A simplified invoice consists of the following information:

+ date of the invoice issuance:

+ the name of the legal person (for natural person — name and surname), address of the supplier of goods or
services, as well as the registration number with the SRS VAT register;

+ the name of the legal person (for a natural person — given name, surname), legal address of the recipient
of goods or services, as well as the registration number with the SRS VAT register or register of taxable
persons of another Member State;

» the name of the goods or the type of the services as well as quantity;

+ the price of goods or services (including tax or excluding tax) and the total amount of the transaction
(including tax or excluding tax); and

* the tax rate and calculated tax amount.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices, and other relevant documents for VAT compliance, must be stored for the minimum period of 5
years, unless otherwise stated in the law (i.e. 10 years under OSS schemes).

Invoices must be stored in the same form as they were received or issued i.e. received paper invoices must
be stored in paper form and electronically received ones must be stored in electronic form. For electronic
invoices, the authenticity of origin, integrity of content and readability must be guaranteed during the storage
period.

Original paper invoices must be stored at the premises of the business. Electronic invoices can be stored
elsewhere, even overseas. However, in such cases, the tax authorities must have the right to access those
invoices, and to download them in a usable format, and within a reasonable period of time.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Invoices can be issued electronically and must contain the same information which is required on a paper
invoice. Both parties involyed in the transaction must agree in writing as to the form of an acceptable
electronic signature that will be used to authenticate the invoice (it may be included in the agreement on the
supply of goods and services).

The “Law On Accounting” also provides that the electronic invoice is accepted as a supporting document if
the transaction and the accuracy of the information in the invoice is confirmed by the responsible person of
the recipient of the document (this person should be specially appointed by the management of the recipient
company).

As of 2025 it is mandatory to use e-invoices with public entities. Electronic invoices to public entities can
be transmitted using the PEPPOL network, a third-party invoicing provider, or via the Latvian government’s
eAddress platform. It is envisaged that e-invoicing will be mandatory in domestic B2B transactions as of
2026.

Electronic invoices to other recipients may be transmitted via any practical methods. There are no specific
validation or reporting requirements.

--- Page 174 ---
10.2 Compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

‘The VAT retum reporting period is monthly for all VAT payers newly registered in the VAT register and
remains so at least 6 consecutive months from the VAT registration.

‘The reporting period changes to quarterly if the VAT taxable person does not exceed EUR 50,000 threshold
for VAT taxable transactions, does not perform intra-Community supplies or acquisitions of goods, does not
provide services taxable pursuant to Article 44 of the Directive 2006/112/EC.

If'a taxable person performs EU supplies or acquisitions and has to submit European sales listings, monthly
filing is always required.

VAT returns should be submitted electronically via the Electronic Declaration System (EDS).

10.2.2 Filing of a VAT return and payment

As of 2021, the VAT due is payable by the 23" day of the month following the VAT return period, The VAT
returns are still due on the 20" of the month following the VAT return period. The deadline is transferred to
the next working day, if the due date is during the holidays.

To submit a VAT refund application addressed to another Member State, the Latvian taxpayer must be
registered as an EDS user. In order to fill in the refund application form, the EDS user in the document group
“Outgoing documents for VAT refund in European Union” chooses the document “VAT refund application”
and enters data in accordance with the criteria indicated by the EDS. After filling it in, the document must be
signed in accordance with the general requirements of the EDS.

Please note that the tax authorities only accept returns filed via the EDS.

10.2.3 European sales listing

Registered taxpayers must file an ESL if they engage in any of the following activities:

+ zero-rated intra-Community supplies;

+ transferring goods from the taxpayer’s activity in Latvia to an activity it undertakes in another Member
State;

+ selling services VAT-free to a trader who provided a VAT registration number in another Member State; or

+ supplying goods in connection with call-off stock transactions.

Taxpayers must comply with recapitulative statement reporting obligations in order to obtain a zero rate on
intra-Community dispatches.

The ESL must provide details of each customers’ residence and VAT registration in an EU jurisdiction outside
Latvia, The list must also include the aggregate value in euros, of the supplies made to each customer in the
applicable taxable period.

The ESL must be filed with the SR$’s EDS on Form PVN2 or using the agency’s e-service or file transfer
service, no later than the 20" day of the month after the expiry of the applicable reporting period. An ESL for
services only must be filed quarterly and an ESL for goods, or goods and services, must be filed monthly.
10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

There is a form of national recapitulative statement in Latvia. With the taxable person’s monthly or quarterly
VAT-declaration, an attachment to the declaration (Attachment 2 “PVN 1 - I”) must be filed. It must detail
each purchase and sales transaction over EUR 150.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period
The tax assessment period in Latvia is 3 years following the day the VAT becomes payable.

358 | Latvia

--- Page 175 ---
10.3 Penalties

Various administrative penalties can be applied for failure to comply with reporting, accounting and
administrative requirements. The amounts of the penalties vary depending on the particular violation and the
status of the person who has committed the violation.

Penalties can also be assessed for the underpayment or nonpayment of tax due as well as for obtaining
incorrect refunds of overpaid tax. The penalties range from 20% to 50% of the tax liability assessed, under
Articles 32-34 of the Taxes and Duties Act.

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration
Ifa natural or legal person, who has registered as a performer of economic activities, performs an economic
activity:
+ without registering as a payer of VAT; or
+ within 30 days after the term determined by the tax administration:
* does not submit the tax declarations provided for by tax laws, and
+ _ also does not submit the business and accounting records requested by the tax administration,

then the tax administration assesses and enforces:

+ the tax for the taxation period from the date on which the person had an obligation to register as a
taxpayer;

+ late payment penalty; and

+ apenalty at 100% of the underpaid tax.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time
For failing to file a VAT retum or a European sales listing within the required time or with incomplete

documents, a fine of up to EUR 700 can be imposed on individuals and legal persons, depending on the
period of delay. For not filing the required documents at all, a fine of EUR 700 can be imposed.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities
If the VAT is not paid on time, penalties in the amount of 20%, 30%, 50% or 100% of the VAT payable
depending on the VAT amount not paid, can be applied, as per Article 32 of the Law on Taxes and Fees. Late
payment penalties of 18.25% per year (0.05% per day) on the VAT amount due also apply for each day of the
delay.

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

If the VAT is not paid up to the correct amount, the VAT authorities can also impose a penalty of 20%, 30% or
50% of the VAT payable depending on the VAT amount not paid.

10.4 Intrastat

In Latvia, separate monthly Intrastat returns must be submitted to the Central Statistical Bureau of Latvia. No
Intrastat return for distance sales should be submitted.

The administrative penalty for not filing the Intrastat reports or missing the filing deadlines might be imposed
- minimum EUR 50, but depending on circumstances up to EUR 10,000 (but please note that it is rarely
exercised).

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
Tn 2025 the Intrastat threshold for arrivals is EUR 350,000 and for dispatches the threshold is EUR 200,000.

Detailed returns are required if the special thresholds are exceeded: EUR 5,000,000 for arrivals and
EUR 7,000,000 for dispatches.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported
The information which needs to be reported for both the arrival and the dispatch statistics is:
+ 8digit commodity code of the goods;

+ invoice value of the goods in EURs;

+ net weight of the goods;

+ supplementary units;

Latvia | 359

--- Page 176 ---
+ EU Member State of origin/destination;
* nature of the transaction;

+ mode of transport;

+ delivery terms;

+ statistical value;

+ country of origin;

+ VAT ID of partner.

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

There are no additional requirements.
